# CLAUDE.md

This file captures only what cannot be inferred from the codebase itself.
## Rules for editing this file


Both developers and AI agents are expected to add entries as they encounter surprises.

- **Add an entry** when you encounter something unexpected: a build quirk, a non-obvious constraint, a dependency gotcha, or any behavior that would surprise the next agent or developer.
- **Add an entry** when a developer flags an anti-pattern produced by AI — describe the anti-pattern and the preferred alternative.
- **Do not** add codebase overviews, directory listings, or anything discoverable by reading the source.
- Keep entries concise: one line per lesson, grouped under a heading if a theme emerges.
- **Durable-invariant test** before adding a gotcha: "Would a future agent rediscover this in under 60 seconds via Grep + reading source?" If yes, it belongs in the git commit message, not here. Every entry costs tokens on every session — the bar for inclusion is **future agents will break things if they don't know this**. Per-fix narratives (test name, specific code path, fix story) go in PLAN-PHASE-5.md session notes instead.
- **Format rule (2026-06-10 trim 284→170 KB; 2026-07-06 trim 594→~280 KB after it silently regrew — do not regrow it)**: a new entry is 1–3 lines stating the trap/invariant and where to look — NOT the fix story. No B-numbers, round numbers, or test-name narratives in the entry body (the session note carries those); no new per-round `### Round-NNN gotchas` sections — file new entries under the matching topical section. If an entry needs more than ~5 lines, the invariant probably isn't isolated yet — distill it.
- **THE ARCHIVE IS NOW MOST OF WHAT THIS FILE USED TO SAY (2026-07-26 trim, 425 KB → ~91 KB, owner's context-engineering directive: "less is more").** Every per-diagnostic and per-walker section — Type system / Checker / Overload resolution / Module detection+resolution / Binder / Scanner-Parser / Emitter / Transformer / Lib-suggestion / all the per-TS-code sections — moved WHOLESALE to `docs/history/CLAUDE-GOTCHAS-ARCHIVE.md` (656 KB). Nothing was deleted. What stayed here: cross-cutting architecture of LIVE subsystems, process/build traps, measured negative knowledge, test conventions, Kotlin idioms, and the mission/protocol. **SO: when you touch a dedicated walker, a specific TS code, a corpus-test regression, or a frozen subsystem, GREP THE ARCHIVE FIRST** (`command grep -n '<walker|TSxxxx|test name|B-number>' docs/history/CLAUDE-GOTCHAS-ARCHIVE.md`) — the knowledge is there, it is just no longer loaded into every session. Progressive disclosure is the design; treat a missing entry here as "look in the archive", never as "no such constraint exists".
- **The archive (2026-07-06, Phase 17 residency rule)**: ~250 corpus-era entries — corpus-unique pin/dedicated-walker docs (B-numbers), per-test parser-recovery cascades, emitter/baseline byte-format minutiae, checkJs one-offs — live in `docs/history/CLAUDE-GOTCHAS-ARCHIVE.md`. Their protection is the always-green corpus suite (a 2-minute hard gate), not this file. **Grep the archive (by walker name / B-number / corpus test name / TS-code) BEFORE modifying or deleting a dedicated walker, investigating a corpus-test regression, or working in a frozen subsystem (parser error-recovery, comment emit, baseline formatting, decorator metadata).** Residency here is reserved for: cross-cutting architecture of LIVE subsystems, process/build traps, and measured negative knowledge ("X was tried and regressed") — per-test/per-walker detail goes straight to the archive.

## Known gotchas

- **A `for (result in binderResults)` LOOP IS NOT EVIDENCE OF A TAIL WALKER — 33 OF THE 252 SUCH LOOPS ARE *LOOKUP HELPERS* THAT SCAN THE PROGRAM TO FIND A DECLARATION (`resolveIdentifierInFile`, `findTypeParamDeclByName`, `findTypeAliasByName`, `getEnumMemberValue`, `isReferencedAliasDeclaration`, `computeTypeParamInfo`, …), AND GATING ONE ONTO `checkedResults` BREAKS NAME RESOLUTION RATHER THAN DROPPING A DIAGNOSTIC (2026-08-22, (INC.7) batch 3).** They are all 0.00 ms, so there is nothing to gain and everything to lose, and **a mechanical `sed` over the loop header hits every one of them.** The membership test is a registered `pass("<name>") { <fn>() }`; gate only passes, by name, one at a time.
- **…AND THE COROLLARY IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE OBVIOUS ONE: A GATEABLE WALKER WHOSE *PRIVATE HELPER* ALSO SCANS `binderResults` IS STILL SAFE, **BECAUSE** THE HELPER IS NOT A PASS AND SO IS NEVER GATED — it keeps resolving against the whole program while the walker's own loop narrows (2026-08-22; `checkMixinClassConstructor` is the shipped instance, `checkImportTypeUsedAsType` and `checkBareAtTypesExportEqualsMissingNamedImport` are two more, refused only as batch-3 conservatism).** What such a helper does must still be READ, because the count alone cannot tell a whole-program RESOLUTION (`binderResults.any { … declare module "fs" … }` — safe) from an ENUMERATION that emits per file inside the helper (a second walk in disguise, which double-reports). Count the refs to find the sites; read the direction to judge them.
- **GRADLE DOES NOT FORWARD `-Dfoo=bar` TO THE TEST JVM, SO A TEST GATED ON A SYSTEM PROPERTY SILENTLY DOES NOTHING — USE AN ENVIRONMENT VARIABLE (2026-08-21).** `./gradlew … -Dkir.probe.file=…` sets the property on the GRADLE daemon; the test worker is a separate process and `System.getProperty` there answers null, so the gated body is skipped and the run passes with no output. `System.getenv` crosses the boundary (`KIR_PROBE_FILE=… ./gradlew …`), which is why `LibraryProbe` reads env.
- **A DEADLINE PLACED AFTER A BLOCKING PIPE READ IS UNREACHABLE, AND THE SYMPTOM IS A SUITE THAT HANGS AT 100% CPU WITH A TIMEOUT IN THE SOURCE (2026-08-21).** The KIR corpus runner had `process.inputStream.readText()` (which returns only at EOF, i.e. at process exit) ABOVE its `waitFor(2, MINUTES)`, so a generated program that looped without printing hung the whole run and the deadline never ran. Redirect a child's output to FILES and enforce the deadline around `waitFor`; the same shape bites any harness that reads a child's pipes before waiting on it.
- **A HASH-FAMILY *SHARE* IS NOT EVIDENCE ABOUT ANY PARTICULAR CONTAINER UNTIL ITS KEY-COUNT DISTRIBUTION IS CENSUSED — THE KIR PROPERTY BAG READ 28.3% OF A TOML PARSE'S LEAF SAMPLES AND A SMALL-BAG LINEAR SCAN AIMED AT IT MEASURED **+21% SLOWER** (2026-08-21, reverted).** The population is BIMODAL and one number hid it: `smol-toml`'s `ParseContext` is a 4-field scanner state the scan suits, while the parsed document's tables are the other half (the root table alone has 18 keys), and every bag that outgrows the inline capacity pays the arrays AND the promotion AND the map. Same shape as round 902's "price a per-operation change with a histogram weighted by the OPERATION". Corollary: an identity-first key compare is a pure loss wherever keys come from DATA rather than from emitted literals — a parsed TOML key is never the interned string the scan hopes for.
- **A LOOKUP COST IS WEIGHTED BY *READS*, AND A POPULATION CENSUSED BY ALLOCATION CAN BE BIMODAL WHERE THE READ POPULATION IS UNIMODAL — THE KIR BAG'S READS ARE **93.6% ON A THREE-KEY BAG** (99.1% ≤ 4, tail 0.9%) WHERE ITS *SETS* ARE THE BIMODAL HALF §2 MEASURED (2026-08-21).** That is the most favourable population an identity-compared scan could be handed, so the cleanest possible scan was built — no promotion, single-shaped `get`, everything else cold — and it measured **NO EFFECT**, as did a `LinkedHashMap` merely SIZED to the census. **Both looked like small regressions (+6.6%, +2.3%) until the baseline was replicated and moved 692 -> 735 ms on the same bytes** — round 858's law on a fourth instrument, and the reason `scripts/kir-screen.sh` states its own ±6% drift in its header. With the two earlier promoted designs (+21%, +31%, large enough to survive that band) that is FOUR designs and no win. **A hash probe on an interned key with a cached hash is about two memory reads, and a bounds-checked 1-3 iteration loop is not fewer** — and a smaller table costs more read collisions than it saves in allocation whenever reads outnumber writes (here 3.47:1). The container family is CLOSED; only the NOMINAL half (a `getfield`, not a cheaper lookup) remains. `docs/perf/kir-backend-levers.md` § 2a.
- **AND THE SAME CENSUS REFUTED ITS NEIGHBOUR WITHOUT A BUILD, BY ROUND 896's DIVISION: `jsTruthyBooleanOrNull` READ 7.2-7.4% OF SAMPLES OVER **298 CALLS PER PARSE** = **8.2 ns** FOR `value != null && value` (2026-08-21).** Impossible by ~20x for an inlinable null-check-and-unbox, so that row is a LOCATION and not a price — where the bag's own row divided to ~4.9 ns/op, exactly a `String`-keyed map probe, and survived. Divide before designing; the two rows sat next to each other in the same profile and only one of them was real.
- **…AND THE SECOND ATTEMPT REGRESSED IT **31%**, WHICH CLOSES THE WHOLE DIRECTION: THE KIR PROPERTY BAG IS EXPENSIVE IN THE *NUMBER* OF OPERATIONS, NOT IN THEIR UNIT COST (2026-08-21, reverted).** Giving `JsObject` the shape its LITERAL declared and promoting at the first undeclared key — chosen precisely to leave the dictionary half alone, and it did, `HashMap` fell 38.3% -> 4.7% of samples — cost the bag **709 -> 771 samples** for the same work while the run grew 22%, the rest landing on unrelated `program.*` and regex frames (what a bigger, two-shaped `get`/`set` does to callers it used to be inlined into). A `LinkedHashMap` probe on an interned key is already about as cheap as a three-element scan. **Only removing the operations helps — the NOMINAL half, (KIR.PERF.1)** — and `KirPropertyBagTest` is representation-independent by construction, so both refuted attempts passed all of it unchanged: it is the grading harness, not the gate. `docs/perf/kir-backend-levers.md` §2.
- **A BACKEND PERFORMANCE CLAIM NEEDS THE THIRD ARM, OR IT CANNOT BE ATTRIBUTED — `scripts/kir-bench.sh` runs tsgo->JS->node, xtsc->JVM->java AND xtsc->JS->node, and the last one is the whole point (2026-08-21).** The first two differ in BOTH the compiler and the target, so a gap between them says nothing about which is responsible; the control holds the runtime fixed and varies only the compiler. Measured, our JS lands on tsgo's (1.01x/1.02x), which is what licenses reading the KIR arm's gap as a BACKEND number. Its equivalence gate runs before any timing and is not optional: a JS file that throws on import prints nothing, and a wall-clock harness reads that as the fastest arm.

- **A KOTLIN COMPILER PLUGIN RUNS ON KOTLIN/NATIVE, BUT **KLIB SERIALIZATION SEES ONLY THE FRONTEND'S FILES** — A FILE THE PLUGIN *ADDS* TO THE MODULE FRAGMENT IS DROPPED WHOLE, THE BINARY STILL LINKS, AND IT DIES AT RUN TIME WITH AN `IrLinkageError` NAMING A SYMBOL THE IR VALIDATOR HAD JUST ACCEPTED (2026-08-21, the KIR native backend).** Producing a program is "one-stage" compilation, i.e. it writes an intermediate klib, so there is no way round it: re-parent every generated declaration into the SEED's own file (which then needs per-file name prefixes — eight lowered TypeScript files contribute eight `moduleInit`s to one package). Three siblings from the same session: Kotlin 2.4 has no `cli/pipeline/native`, so the backend rides in as an `IrGenerationExtension` under `-Xplugin` rather than driving phases; Native's IR validator REJECTS the public fields the JVM backend accepts; and **the entry point is resolved by the FRONTEND**, so `-e pkg.main` answers "could not find" for a generated `main` however valid the IR is — the seed declares the `main` konanc finds and the plugin gives it a body. `scripts/kir-native.sh`, `docs/perf/kir-backend-levers.md` § 6.
- **A `-Xplugin=<dir>` MUST NAME THE *RESOURCES* DIRECTORY AS WELL AS THE CLASSES ONE, OR THE PLUGIN IS NEVER DISCOVERED AND konanc EXITS **0** HAVING COMPILED THE EMPTY SEED (2026-08-21).** The registrar is found by `ServiceLoader` and its `META-INF/services` file is a RESOURCE, which Gradle stages in `build/processedResources/jvm/main`, not in `build/classes/...`. The tell is a binary the size of a hello-world and no plugin output; the guard is a POSITIVE CONTROL — the plugin announces itself on stderr and the script REFUSES a run without that line (round 853's shape, one toolchain over).
- **A `-P` PATH PROPERTY IS RESOLVED AGAINST THE *GRADLE DAEMON'S* WORKING DIRECTORY, NOT THE INVOCATION'S — `File(relative).absoluteFile` IN A TASK LOOKED FOR `~/.gradle/daemon/9.7.0/<relative>` (2026-08-21).** Round 873's defect one process over, and the same fix: resolve against a directory the build KNOWS (`rootDir`), never against `user.dir`. It fails loudly here only because the task checks the directory exists; a task that WRITES to such a path would silently create it under the daemon's home.
- **KOTLIN/NATIVE HAS NO ESCAPE ANALYSIS, SO A BACKEND WHOSE VALUES ARE `Any?` PAYS A REAL ALLOCATION FOR EVERY ONE — MEASURED 4-29x THE JVM PER PRIMITIVE, AND *THAT* IS THE WHOLE 4-7x WHOLE-PROGRAM GAP (2026-08-21).** `jsAdd(Any?, Any?)` is 0.95 ns on the JVM and **28.05 ns** native; boxing one `Double` is 0.86 vs **8.61**. So a "make the dynamic representation cheaper" refutation measured on the JVM does NOT transfer: C2 was deleting the boxes the whole time. `kotlin.text.Regex` is a second, independent 5.2x over `java.util.regex`. Corollary for any cross-backend microbenchmark: **consume every result** — the first JVM run of the primitive table read `0.0 ns/op` on six of ten rows because C2 removed the loops, which reads exactly like a fast backend.
- **SPECIALIZING JavaScript `===` ON TWO NUMBERS MUST COMPARE *PRIMITIVES*, NEVER BOXED `Double`s — `Double.equals` holds `NaN == NaN` and `0.0 != -0.0`, and JavaScript inverts BOTH (2026-08-21).** Kotlin gives IEEE-754 for two statically-primitive `Double` operands and `equals` for anything it reads as `Any`/a type parameter, so the difference is invisible in the source; write the comparison in a function whose PARAMETERS are declared `Double` rather than emitting an IR `EQEQ`, and pin `NaN !== NaN`. The mirror rule for `==`: abstract equality coincides with strict equality only when both operands are the SAME primitive, because `1 == true` and `null == undefined` are true — no mixed case may specialize. And both half-specialized directions (`…AnyNumber` / `…NumberAny`) must exist: reaching one entry point by swapping the operands reorders two expressions that may both have effects.
- **`K2MetadataCompilerArguments.metadataKlib` LEFT FALSE WRITES THE *LEGACY* METADATA LAYOUT UNDER THE SAME `.klib` NAME, WITH NO DIAGNOSTIC, AND A MULTIPLATFORM CONSUMER RESOLVES NOTHING FROM IT (2026-08-22, the API export).** Both wrong and right answers exit 0 and produce a plausible artifact, so the only instrument is a pin on the LAYOUT (`default/manifest` + `default/linkdata/`). Three more from the same seam: `K2MetadataCompiler` is deprecated in Kotlin 2.4 and this build turns that into an error (`KotlinMetadataCompiler` is the live class), a klib resolves on a consumer's classpath both as a DIRECTORY and as a ZIP, and a surface naming only Kotlin BUILT-INS needs no classpath at all — which is what makes the exported artifact self-contained. `docs/kir-kotlin-metadata.md` §1.
- **AN *ABSENT DECLARATION* IS NOT EVIDENCE OF AN ANONYMOUS OBJECT TYPE, AND A `Type.Reference`'s OWN SYMBOL CARRIES NO DECLARATION WHERE ITS **TARGET's** DOES — SO `symbol?.valueDeclaration ?: symbol?.declarations?.firstOrNull()` ANSWERS NULL FOR EVERY GENERIC INSTANTIATION, INCLUDING `Promise<string>` (2026-08-22, measured on the API export; the same line is live in `ErasedTypes.mapObject`, queued as (KIR.LOWER.2)).** Read as "anonymous, therefore a property bag", that types a promise as a bag whose members it lacks — silently. Two rules separate the cases: consult `(type as? Type.Reference)?.target?.symbol` for the declaration, and treat a type that HAS a name but no reachable declaration as a library type the backend does not know.
- **AN API-SURFACE TYPE MAPPER IS *NOT* THE IR ERASURE AND MUST NOT BE MERGED WITH IT: THE CODOMAINS DIFFER (a NAME versus an `IrType` needing a live `IrBuiltIns`) AND SO DO THE FAILURE MODES — inside a body an unmappable type is an operation that cannot be lowered, so `ErasedTypes` answers null and the program is REFUSED; in a SIGNATURE there is no operation, so it erases to `Any?` (2026-08-22).** The same split decides refusal GRANULARITY: an API export omits the one declaration and reports it, because an absent declaration is a compile error at the consumer's use site where a wrongly-typed one is silent. **AND A MAPPER WITH NO LIBRARY-TYPE TABLE MUST DEMAND *POSITIVE* EVIDENCE THAT A SHAPE IS A PROPERTY BAG** — `Date` and an unmappable type-level constraint both answer `Any?` there, so `ErasedTypes`' permissive reading of an intersection (an unmapped member is a constraint) types `Date & Tag` as a bag whose members the value does not have. `kir/api/ApiErasure.kt`'s KDoc carries the divergence list.
- **POINTING THE COMPILER AT AN UNFAMILIAR REAL LIBRARY FINDS DEFECT CLASSES THE 13k-BASELINE CORPUS AND THE 8 PROFILES STRUCTURALLY CANNOT (2026-08-21, six in one session).** All six were silent — a guard written `const isX = (n): n is X => …` narrowing NOTHING, a default-imported function typing as `any`, a module-level `const` widening where a body-local one does not — and none is expressible as a tsc-sources shape, because the corpus is ONE codebase's style. The loop is two commands: `tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc --noEmit -p <dir>` for the front-end half and `KIR_PROBE_PROJECT=<dir> … --tests '*LibraryProbe*'` for the backend half; `docs/kir-library-readiness.md` carries the standing measurements.

- **A CAPTURE SPAN IN A FILE THE CHECK PARTITION OMITS IS NEVER WALKED PAST, SO ITS ANSWER COMES BACK **ABSENT** RATHER THAN WRONG — AN EMPTY TOOLTIP, AN EMPTY COMPLETION, NO ERROR ANYWHERE (2026-08-22, (INC.2b)).** That is why `Project.captureIn` DERIVES `recheckOnly` from the request's own spans instead of taking a file set beside them: a call site cannot forget a file it asked about if it never states the set. **A fifth span list added to `TypeCaptureRequest` must be added to `captureFiles` in the same commit** — `ProjectCaptureNarrowingTest` reddens per channel and is the only thing that notices. Corollary for reading its gates: a divergence ROW in the member/scope/signature channels is a LIST, so one display mechanism reaches every caret on that receiver — census the distinct first DIFFERING ELEMENT (286 rows were five causes), never the row count.
- **(API.5)'s OCCURRENCE SET IS COMPLETE ENOUGH TO *HIGHLIGHT* AND NOT COMPLETE ENOUGH TO *EDIT*, AND NO REFERENCE PIN CAN SEE THE DIFFERENCE — a missing occurrence is simply not drawn (round 925: on one fixture tsc's member rename edits **5** spans where our resolution finds **2**).** The three it misses are a binding element's `propertyName`, an `o["p"]` (a string literal, outside the identifier population by construction) and an IMPLEMENTOR's member (a different symbol here) — so anything that EDITS from that set (rename, extract, inline, a code action) must carry `Project.renameAt`'s completeness net: a SPELLING scan used as a safety net and never as the answer, split by member-vs-free position because a member declaration name resolves to nothing and an unsplit net refuses every ordinary local rename.
- **AND A REFACTORING VERIFIED BY *COUNTING DIAGNOSTICS* CANNOT SEE A CAPTURE (round 925).** Renaming a file-level `a` to `b` where some function body holds its own `b` moves that body's reads onto the local, with agreeing types and NO diagnostic anywhere; the only instrument that sees it is re-asking what each span RESOLVES to after the edit — every renamed occurrence, and every identifier that already spelled the new name, must answer what it answered before. tsc's own language server checks neither this nor a plain collision.
- **`tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc` IS THE ONLY REFERENCE COMPILER RUNNABLE ON THIS BOX AND IT **DIVERGES FROM PRISTINE tsc**, SO WHERE A CORPUS BASELINE EXISTS IT OUTRANKS A tsgo MEASUREMENT — AND ROUNDS 933-937 USED tsgo AS THE SOLE REFERENCE (round 938, two divergences measured in one family).** For a duplicate member, tsgo emits TS2300 at BOTH declarations where pristine emits it at the second only (`classWithDuplicateIdentifier`, `duplicateIdentifierComputedName`), and tsgo emits TS2300 for a LATE-BOUND duplicate where pristine emits TS2717 alone (`dynamicNamesErrors`) — implementing tsgo's answer compiles, looks like a fix, and REDDENS that corpus test. **Before landing a diagnostic measured only against tsgo, ask `scripts/pristine_oracle.py` (round 939) — `--code`/`--pattern`/`--fixture`, and it labels every hit ACTIVE vs not-generated, i.e. whether the suite already gates it**; a green suite is the backstop but the baseline is the reason. Four things about that oracle a next agent will otherwise get wrong: an ABSENT `.errors.txt` beside a present case means pristine was SILENT and is evidence; `tests/cases` in this clone is INCOMPLETE (6,537 files against 9,055 error baselines — whole conformance directories missing), so a pattern search must go through the `.js`/`.errors.txt` baselines, which echo every input verbatim; `--extract DIR` writes pristine's own input back out so OUR binary can be run over exactly what pristine saw (round 939 did that for ~326 ungated fixtures and found no false positive attributable to rounds 933-938); and the `.types`/`.symbols` baselines answer a NAMING question with no diagnostic at all (`computedPropertyNames10_ES6.types` records `` [`hello bye`]() `` as the member `"hello bye"` and a SUBSTITUTING template as `[x: string]`). **A fixed scratch `tsconfig` manufactures false positives** — honour the case's own `// @target`, or re-read every ours-only row at a second target (round 939 lost two rows that way). No `node` is installed — that binary is a native ELF, run it directly.
- **LOGICAL PARITY IS THE BAR, NOT BYTE PARITY (owner directive 2026-07-26; supersedes every "N corpus tests regress" veto in this file and the archive).** "The logical value of the compiler output at maximal performance should always be the deciding factor. Byte-by-byte parity is secondary if it can be achieved without extra cost." **So a corpus baseline that differs only in FORM — union member order, an equivalent message, a differently-shaped-but-equivalent elaboration — is no longer a blocker: write a NEW test case pinning the logic and switch the old one off** (say so in the session note, with the divergence and why it is logically equivalent). A baseline that differs in MEANING — a diagnostic that should fire and does not, a wrong type, wrong emitted semantics — is still a hard regression. This unblocks the general-engine work the byte gate priced out: many entries here and in the archive record a walker or rule as "DEAD — regressed N tests" **without checking whether those N were form or meaning**; such an entry is now a lead to re-examine, not a veto. **THE MECHANISM (round 717): a baseline is switched off ONLY by adding a `LogicalParityDivergence` to `logicalParityDivergences` in build.gradle.kts** — the generator then emits that subtest `@Ignore`d (so it stays VISIBLE as skipped, not vanished from the total), rewrites the ledger in `docs/logical-parity.md`, and FAILS the build on a stale entry or a `pinnedBy` class that does not exist under src/commonTest. Commenting an emission out, deleting a baseline, or widening a skip predicate to absorb a diff is NOT this mechanism. The form-vs-meaning decision tables (what counts as which, and the equivalence obligation per axis) are in `docs/logical-parity.md` § 2 — read them before classifying, and note that a form-only diff is a *candidate*, not an entitlement: byte parity is still preferred where it is free.
- **A PER-NODE MARK IS USELESS UNLESS SOME CONSULTATION SITE CAN PASS THAT NODE *KIND*, AND NOTHING IN THIS REPO NOTICES WHEN IT CANNOT — the three spine-anchor mark families ran for their whole life writing 319,777 marks that fed 48,868 consultations of which **0** were affirmative, because the mark sites and the test sites were DISJOINT in (bit x node kind) (round 887, deleted).** 87% of consultations landed on a node that WAS marked, just never with the bit asked for (`testInRangeOtherBitSet 42,513` vs `testInRangeThisBitSet 0`), so the truncation branches were unreachable by construction — invisible to the corpus, to `cost_gate.py`, to a `--listAll` diff and to round 886's program-wide ablation alike. **If you add a mark, add its consultation site in the SAME commit and pin an input where the guard actually fires**; and a produced-vs-consumed census (round 801) needs BOTH controls or its zero is a round-849 blind one — a POSITIVE control (read each mark back at its own key) and a KEY control (did the consultation find an array for its file at all).
- **`java.lang.Long.hashCode` IS `(int)(v xor (v ushr 32))`, SO A PACKED `(a shl 32) or b` KEY HASHES TO **`a xor b`** — AND WHENEVER THE TWO HALVES HAVE CORRELATED MAGNITUDE THAT COLLAPSES ONTO THEIR DIFFERENCE AND THE MAP TREEIFIES (round 889).** `nodeKey(pos, end)` hashed to the set of node LENGTHS — a few hundred values however many nodes the file has — so `nodeToFlow` filled **278 of 32,768 buckets, max bucket 1,765, 98.3% of keys past the treeify threshold**, and `HashMap$TreeNode.*` charged to `recordFlow` alone was 1.11% of a warm rebuild. **Any new packed key needs a finalizing multiply by an ODD constant (a bijection mod 2^64 — `LongKeyMap`'s `0x9E3779B97F4A7C15`), and any map keyed by one may then only be re-mixed if nothing UNPACKS the key and nothing depends on ITERATION ORDER** (`nodeKey`'s three containers are all `mutableMapOf`, i.e. insertion-ordered `LinkedHashMap`s — a plain `HashMap` would have made the re-mix a rounds-754/776/778 order change that no output diff sees). **And the cost is invisible to every gate here** — no diagnostic, no emitted byte, no `cost_gate.py` counter — so the only instrument is a pin on the key's bucket distribution. **SWEPT AND CLOSED at round 890** — `packIdPair` is now the sole id-pair packer in `Checker.kt` and its KDoc records which sites were degenerate (`Relation.cache`: 43,080 real keys onto 18,201 hashes, **1,140 in one bucket**, because type ids are minted SEQUENTIALLY and a relation asks about NEIGHBOURS), which were measured already-fine (`internKey`, `walkMemoKey`, the three `LongKeyMap`s), and which are REFUSED (`PassTiming.redundantPairNanos` is the repo's one key that is UNPACKED). `docs/perf/hash-key-spread.md` § 5.
- **`LongKeyMap`/`IntKeyMap` RESERVE A SENTINEL KEY AND `nodeKey(0, 0)` **IS** `0L` — SO MOVING ANY `nodeKey`-KEYED CONTAINER ONTO ONE NEEDS `flowKey`'s +1 SHIFT, OR AN ERROR-RECOVERY ZERO-WIDTH NODE AT OFFSET 0 THROWS AT `put` (round 896).** The shift is a bijection, so the spread and round 889's finalizer are untouched; it only moves the sentinel to `(-1, -1)`, where `recordFlow` never writes and a synthetic node's READ correctly answers null. Prefer these containers to a plain `HashMap` wherever an audit says "never iterated" — they have no iterator, so the rounds-754/776/778 order hazard becomes a compile error — but `Binder.nodeToSymbol` IS iterated and must not follow.
- **A JFR OWNER ROW'S ms DIVIDED BY THAT OWNER'S OWN POPULATION IS THE CHEAPEST REFUTATION IN THIS REPO, AND TWO OF ROUND 894'S FIVE CEILINGS FAIL IT BY ~20x (round 896).** `getTypeOfSymbol`'s 28.2 ms "insert 100%" row over its only `java.util` insert — `symbolTypeResolutionInProgress.add`, **24,232 calls, max live 3** — is 1,164 ns per add, impossible for a boxed `HashSet` probe on a 16-slot table. Divide before designing; an owner row not divided by its population has not been priced.
- **A PROBE BOUNDARY IS AN *OPTIMIZER BARRIER*, SO A SECTION THAT IS NOTHING BUT FIELD SAVE/RESTORES IS OVER-READ IN PROPORTION TO HOW WELL THE JIT WOULD HAVE REMOVED IT — `withCpaFrameAmbient`'s install+restore reads **676 ns** for ~16 `putfield`s, one copy of an EMPTY collection and a 2.9-iteration loop (round 908, ~20x physically impossible).** `System.nanoTime()` cannot be reordered across, so bracketing such a block forces every store to happen and to be ordered where production coalesces and register-allocates them. The test is round 896's — divide the row by its own population — and when the implied per-op cost is impossible, **the CENSUS of what the block does, not its timing, is what bounds the prize**. Corollary: before believing an O(n) walk is a cost, census `n` (here 2.9, with an empty result 91% of the time). `docs/perf/spine-frame-bookkeeping-price.md` § 5.
- **THE `dispatch` TIER (`SpineDispatch.PROBE`) BYPASSES ROUND 888's `spineEnterMask` — `spineEnterNode` opens `if (mode != OFF) { spineEnterNodeProbed(node); return }` — SO THE PER-HANDLER TABLE PRICES THE *PRE-888* REGIME AND IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO THE ONE LEVER THAT REGION HAS BANKED (round 908).** Worth ~73 ms on its ~3,234 ms total (round 867's figure for the whole skipped population), i.e. small — but it means a handler's row is NOT "what production spends consulting it", and a `CtaSections` table taken today is not comparable to a pre-888 one either (its eligibility-gate row fell 915,543 -> 120,026 consultations).
- **…AND THE SAME LAW DECIDES CONTAINER SWAPS: A *SCOPE* POPULATION IS NOT A *PROBE* POPULATION, AND THE TWO DIFFERED BY **193x** ON THE ONE CASE THAT WAS MEASURED (round 902).** `LexicalScope.symbols` holds **1.51** own symbols averaged over SCOPES (46.7% empty, 98.7% ≤ 8) and **290.94** averaged over REAL PROBES, because the unresolved-names ascent walks OUTWARDS and 35.5% of probes land on the big outer levels (mean 815). Round 901 priced a parallel-array replacement at 0.41-0.47% off the scope histogram; measured, the hybrid it proposed (array ≤ 8, map above) costs **+13.75 ns per probe = −10.1 ms = −0.19%**, a regression. **Price a per-operation change with a histogram weighted by the OPERATION, never by the container** — an allocation is weighted by containers, a scan by probes. Corollary that closed it without an implementation: even a FREE replacement of the ≤8 levels is 0.25-0.32%, under this arc's floor. `docs/perf/lex-level-scan-price.md`.
- **…AND THE COUNT QUESTION CLOSES IT: `lexLevelHasName` IS CLOSED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, BECAUSE A CACHE KEYED BY THE SAME KEY AT THE SAME GRANULARITY AS THE MAP IT FRONTS ***IS*** THAT MAP (round 907).** The unresolved-names ascent is **563,466 ascents, 2,079,962 NameScope chain steps and 870,231 real probes = 1.544 per ascent** (every family under 2; `typeParamConstraintOf` is called **0** times), so the whole probe stream is **31.85 ms = 0.60%** and no ascent-level oracle can recover more than **0.54 probes per ascent (11.23 ms = 0.21%)** unless it is cheaper than a probe. Measured ceilings, both under the floor: an ascent memo **9.92 ms (0.19%)** with the memo itself FREE — a repeat ascent performs **1.32** probes and a memo probe replaces them with 1 — and a per-file proof-of-absence filter **7.96 ms (0.15%)** as an upper bound. **An ascent's DEPTH is not its COST**: 58% of the level visits are refused by the untrusted/non-head-fn rules or are hash-free EMPTY maps, so "O(depth)" described a 3.69-step pointer walk. `docs/perf/lex-ascent-count-price.md`.
- **A PIN OVER A *SUM* IS A PIN OVER ITS LARGEST MEMBER — split the identity by whatever axis the injected mistake could be confined to (round 907's C1, blind on the first pass).** `chainSteps > ascents` summed over five ascent families stayed GREEN against a census whose ascent count was inflated to the chain-step count, because wiring ONE family's recursion back to its public entry leaves the other four recursing and `has` alone is 47% of the population. Same shape as round 901's A2 (a pin satisfied vacuously by the dominant member) and round 902's B5/B6 (one assertion that two defects both break and neither separates).
- **THE WHOLE BOXED-PRIMITIVE-KEY FAMILY IS MEASURED AND REFUSED — 14 sites, **2,698,745** map/set operations per warm rebuild, a **6.58 ns** per-operation premium, so **17.7 ms = 0.334%** for ALL OF THEM TOGETHER and **3.42 ms (0.064%)** for the largest single one (round 904).** Do not re-open round 899 § 33.8 candidate (6) from a leaf profile. Three reusable constants, and the threshold that makes a NEW site free to refuse: a `LongKeyMap`/`IntKeyMap` probe is **~2 ns** (1.96 round 904, 2.11 round 903 — two sites, two rounds), a boxed `HashMap<Long,·>` probe is **8.53 ns**, so **a site needs ~1.7 M operations to be worth a swap** against the ~17 ms floor — and the whole spine only visits 856,962 nodes, which refuses every per-node memo by arithmetic. **§ 33.8's "an `Integer`-keyed probe is ~15-30 ns" is over by 1.8-3.5x — do not inherit it.** Add one `MapCensus.bk` hook and read its ops; no fix and no amplifier is needed to refuse one. `docs/perf/boxed-primitive-key-price.md`.
- **…AND A SINGLE-`r` `A - B` OVER-READS AN AMPLIFIED PREMIUM BY UP TO 23%, BECAUSE THE TWO ARMS' TIMESTAMP BOUNDARIES ARE NOT EQUAL AND ONLY CANCEL IN THE *SLOPE* (round 904).** The standing advice is that the boundary "cancels between the arms at equal `r`"; measured, arm A's pair cost **88.0 ns** and arm B's **76.1 ns**, so `A - B` read 8.07 at `r=8` and 7.07 at `r=24` against a true **6.58** — and `6.58 + (88.0-76.1)/r` reproduces both to 0.01 ns. Fit `p(r) = cost + boundary/r` per ARM at two `r` and difference the COSTS. **AMENDED ROUND 905, WHICH MEASURED THE SAME ERROR AT 76% AND NAMED THE MECHANISM: A BOUNDARY IS A PROPERTY OF THE *ARM*, NOT OF THE HARNESS** — it absorbs everything charged per CALL, and an iterator-constructing arm builds its iterator there, so the gap between two arms' boundaries is itself part of what you are trying to measure. Fitted boundaries were **60.0 vs 43.3 ns** on `anyIdentical`, where the single-`r` form read 4.85 ns against a true **2.75**. **So the "both must land near ~90 ns" check is WITHDRAWN** — round 905's four arms read 67.9 / 61.4 / 60.0 / 43.3 and were all correct. The check that survives is arithmetic and self-contained: `premium + (b_A − b_B)/r` must reproduce the measured single-`r` `A − B` at *both* `r` (it closes to 0.01 ns when the fit is sound).
- **A `LongKeyMap`/`IntKeyMap` SWAP IS REFUSED OUTRIGHT FOR ANY CONTAINER THAT IS *ITERATED* — those types have no iterator by design, which is what makes the rounds-754/776/778 order hazard a compile error (round 904 found `Binder.lexicalScopes` is iterated at `for ((_, scope) in result.lexicalScopes)`, the same exemption CLAUDE.md already gives `Binder.nodeToSymbol`).** And a transient add/remove STACK (max live 27/18/5) is not a `LongKeyMap` shape at all — its successor is a linear-scan primitive array, which an amplifier arm against a hash map prices in neither direction. Check both before quoting a family total as attainable.
- **A CACHE-LOCALITY CHANGE CANNOT BE AMPLIFIED (round 759's shape prices an L1 hit by its second repetition) AND DOES NOT HAVE TO BE — HOOK THE ACCESSES AND MODEL THE LAYOUTS, BECAUSE A MISS-COUNT DELTA IS A COUNTER, i.e. deterministic and able to resolve far below the ±1.0% warm band (round 906).** Round 875 § 5.2's memo transposition (`<= 0.8%`) is measured at **8,888,467 accesses of which 97.0% ALREADY hit L1**, a ceiling of **2.65-15.99 ms for ANY layout**, and the candidate itself at **+3.90 to +24.20 ms — a REGRESSION**: the spine walks in PREORDER, so each classifier's 1-byte-per-node array is swept SEQUENTIALLY (one line serves ~14.2 consultations, and 89.8% of ascent steps stay within 64 ids) where a 45-byte per-node row serves ~3.8. **Before believing a per-object ROW is more local than a per-attribute COLUMN, ask what ORDER the column is swept in.** `docs/perf/reach-memo-transposition-price.md`.
- **AN ABLATION ARM CAN BE *DEAD* RATHER THAN THE PIN BLIND, AND `git diff --shortstat` CANNOT TELL YOU WHICH — IT PROVES THE EDIT LANDED, NEVER THAT IT *DOES* ANYTHING (round 902, two of six arms).** One arm guarded on a predicate an earlier line had already made true; the other corrupted a table that the FIXTURE never populated. Both compiled, both produced a real diff, both changed nothing, and both printed as "ALL PINS GREEN" — indistinguishable from a redundant guard. **Before recording a green arm, confirm the injected mistake is REACHED** (a counter it must move, or a fixture check that the corrupted structure exists at all). Round 855's "dry-run each arm for a real diff" is necessary and not sufficient.
- **…AND WHEN RANKING SUCH KEYS, A *KEY* POPULATION IS NOT A *MAP* POPULATION: a transient add/remove set's bucket distribution is bounded by its max LIVE size, not by the keys it ever saw (round 890).** `relationComparisonStack` saw **51,447** distinct keys and `resolvedPropertyTypes` **10,482**, so by total keys the stack is the family's worst offender — but it never holds more than **27** entries at once, so its table never grows past `MIN_TREEIFY_CAPACITY` and it cannot treeify however bad its hash is, while the grow-only cache does. A sweep ranked by distinct keys mis-prices every transient container it touches in BOTH directions; census the max live size in the same run.
- **…AND A PROBE OF AN *EMPTY* `HashMap` IS FREE, SO A PROBE CENSUS THAT DOES NOT SPLIT EMPTY FROM NON-EMPTY MANUFACTURES A PRIZE OUT OF OPERATIONS THAT COST NOTHING (round 901).** `getNode` reads `table` BEFORE it hashes the key, and a `mutableMapOf()` that was never written keeps `table == null` — so **271,684 of `lexLevelHasName`'s 1,009,275 probe-path calls are a null check and a return**, and pricing them at the arc's 20-50 ns reference would have invented 7-13 ms. Round 898's admission test one level down: an operation that short-circuits before doing anything is not one of the operations you divide the ms by. Same round, the reference band itself: **it was measured on `perFileScope`, whose keys are file PATHS in a populated table**, so it does not transfer to a map holding 1.5 entries (round 789) — measure the rate with `--lexLevelAmp`'s shape, which amplifies BOTH arms in one call so the ~90 ns boundary cancels BETWEEN them and the FIRST-probe (i.e. production) rate becomes readable. `docs/perf/lex-level-probe-price.md`.
- **…AND THE LEAF INSTABILITY IS NOT PER-ROW BUT PER-*MECHANISM*: THE BOXED-PRIMITIVE LEAF FAMILY READS **72.9 ms** IN ONE PROCESS AND **19.0 ms** IN THE OTHER OF ROUND 899'S OWN PAIR OF DUMPS — SAME BINARY, SAME ROUND, A **4x** DISAGREEMENT (round 904).** Round 868's "LEAF attribution is NOT stable across processes" is usually quoted about `HashMap.getNode`; it applies to every stdlib leaf small enough for C2 to inline, so an aggregation that SUMS such leaves inherits the instability at family scale. **Two processes is the minimum for any leaf-family share, and a family whose two readings disagree 4x has not been measured at all** — the non-JFR answer there was 17.7 ms, i.e. under both. `scripts/boxed_key_leaves.py`.
- **…AND A JFR FAMILY MATCHED BY THE PREFIX `"java.util.HashMap."` SILENTLY EXCLUDES EVERY TREEIFIED-BUCKET SAMPLE, WHOSE FRAMES ARE `java.util.HashMap$TreeNode.*` (round 889).** That one absent `$` hid **6.46% of compile-thread samples** — the whole degenerate-hash family — for two rounds, and made round 886 report the hash family as 24.3% when it is **26.42%**. Any stdlib-family aggregation here must be checked against the `$`-nested inner classes before a share is quoted.
- **EVERY WHOLE-SOURCE SUBSTRING SCAN IN `Checker.kt` GOES THROUGH `srcHas`/`srcIndexOf`/`srcLastIndexOf`, WHICH CONSULT A PER-FILE n-GRAM FILTER BEFORE SCANNING — A NEW `source.contains("…")` WRITTEN DIRECTLY IS CORRECT AND COSTS A WHOLE-PROGRAM PASS PER FILE, AND NOTHING IN THIS REPO NOTICES (round 895).** The 149 gated sites are 3,827 calls over 488 M characters per rebuild to find 14 needles; the filter refuses 97.3% of them. **`SourceScanFilter`'s soundness is that its build fold and `hashOf` are ONE expression written twice — diverge them and pin walkers silently stop firing**, which no counter, no `cost_gate.py` and no wall can see; `--verifySrcScan` with the `--srcScanBogus` positive control is the instrument, and the 8-profile grid is the gate. CHAR searches (`indexOf(';', pos)`) are deliberately NOT routed — a single character is below the window width. `docs/perf/whole-source-scan-census.md`.
- **…AND EVERY COMMITTED `round*-grid.sh` HAS BEEN A *ONE*-PROFILE GRID: `bench-compile-tsc.sh` names the compiler profile `tsc-project-<commit8>` and the other seven `tsc-<name>-<commit8>`, so the universal glob `build/bench/tsc-project-*` matches the compiler profile and NOTHING ELSE (round 895).** Round 888's output directory holds exactly one profile's captures. Enumerate profile dirs by the presence of a `tsconfig.json` and REFUSE below 8 — `scripts/round895-grid.sh` is the reference — and materialize them with `scripts/bench-compile-tsc.sh --project all --no-emit --no-log`.
- **A COLD CENSUS OF A TEXT SCAN OVERSTATES IT AND A COLD CENSUS OF A REGEX UNDERSTATES IT — THE JDK `String.indexOf` INTRINSIC WARMS **5.07x** WHILE A HAND-WRITTEN SCANNER WARMS **3.49x** AND `java.util.regex` WARMS ~1.5x, ALL AGAINST A ~3.4x REBUILD (round 895, measured both regimes in one round).** So gating a scan is worth LESS warm than cold (1.49% → 1.24% here) while an ungated regex is worth MORE (round 859). Never rank a text-scanning change from a cold table in either direction.
- **A REGEX OVER WHOLE-PROGRAM TEXT NEEDS A LEADING LITERAL OF AT LEAST *FOUR* CHARACTERS, OR IT IS ATTEMPTED AT EVERY ONE OF THE PROGRAM'S ~10 M POSITIONS — `BnM.optimize` (Boyer-Moore) refuses a shorter `Slice` outright and never sees a pattern starting with `\b` / `(?m)^` / an alternation / a class / a lookaround (rounds 860, 862, 863: three members, ~273 ms banked, each found by accident until the class was swept).** A bare `^` WITHOUT `(?m)` is the exception (a `Begin` root, attempted once). **The census of all 110 `commonMain` sites is `docs/perf/whole-program-regex-census.md` — read it before adding a whole-file matcher, and note that nine members are gated to zero on the tsc profiles and only wake on an `allowJs` / `commonjs` / `@types` project.** The fix is always an EXACT hand-written scan anchored on a literal via `indexOf`, with the pattern kept LIVE as the differential oracle — never a legality gate (round 792).
- **…AND THE EMIT PATH IS A BLIND SPOT FOR *EVERY* INSTRUMENT IN THIS REPO: round 738's `skipEmitOutputs` gate makes `--noEmit` skip `Transformer.transform` and `Emitter.emit` entirely, so `BenchMain` (check-only until round 863's 5th argument `emit`), `cost_gate.py` and the `--noEmit --listAll` 8-profile grid are ALL blind to a cost living there at once (round 863).** Anything touching transform/emit must be measured with `BenchMain <proj> <w> <i> <tiers> emit` and gated with an EMIT-mode `diff -r` of the 78 emitted files; a green grid there is a control, not a gate.
- **A `var` CAPTURED BY A NON-INLINE CLOSURE IS BOXED INTO A `Ref$ObjectRef`, AND EVERY READ OF IT ANYWHERE IN THE FUNCTION THEN COSTS A `getfield` + `checkcast` — ~1,008 BYTECODES, 4.7%, IN `compileParsedCore` FROM ONE CAPTURE (round 816).** `var options` is captured by the `ParallelCheckMode.workers > 1` branch's task lambdas (a list of function VALUES — the one closure form Kotlin cannot inline away), so all **168** reads spread over its 1,780 lines were boxed; passing `options` as a helper PARAMETER makes it an immutable local again and the boxing disappears, which is why that split came out **1,241 bytecodes SMALLER** than the monolith. Two consequences: **"a split's parts must sum to at least the original" is not a law**, and `javap -c … | grep -c 'Ref$ObjectRef'` is a one-line test worth running before hunting for a big method's size in what it DOES. Per-source-line attribution: `scripts/method_bytes_by_line.py` (it also shows ~23% of that method was INLINED stdlib, at synthetic line numbers past EOF).
- **A HELPER WHOSE PARAMETER LIST HAS TWO SAME-TYPED CONTAINERS MUST BE CALLED WITH NAMED ARGUMENTS — a positional permutation type-checks and is silently catastrophic (round 816).** `cpcScanFiles` takes 18 parameters including three `MutableList<Pair<String, String>>` and four `MutableSet<String>`; swapping `sourceEchoes` with `jsonOutputs` compiles cleanly and makes a multi-file build echo nothing while emitting the echoes as JSON outputs. The compiler cannot help here, so the call convention is the guard and a pin is what notices if it lapses.
- **A RUNNING-SECTION PARTITION OF A *RECURSIVE* FUNCTION MUST HAND THE CALLER'S ROW BACK, NOT SKIP ON DEPTH (round 756, `CtaSections` level D).** The `if (depth != 1) return` shape used by levels A–C is only correct for a non-recursive function: applied to a recursive walker it charges the ENTIRE descent to whichever row was open at depth 1 (the dispatch row) and answers nothing. Instead `beginD()` closes the caller's running row and RETURNS it, `endD(prev)` closes its own and reopens `prev` — every row is then SELF time, exclusive of nested invocations, and the rows sum to the true total. Two consequences a reader must be told: the count column is boundary CLOSES (a row is also closed by every nested entry made while it is open), so per-invocation populations come from an arm census, never from that column; and the probe's activation window must be an explicit flag rather than `cur == <row>`, or the level dies under `COARSE` where the outer level's interior anchors do not fire — which is exactly what makes its own ON-vs-COARSE differential possible.
- **QUALIFYING THE ABOVE (round 786, `CtaSections` level E): the hand-back shape is for a recursion spread over MANY arms — a function that recurses at exactly ONE place is better served by a dedicated ROW that the "depth != 1 ⇒ return" shape charges the whole nested descent to**, which keeps every other row exclusive and the count column meaning invocations rather than boundary closes. Pin the "exactly one place" (an `invocationsNested == 1` assertion on a two-level fixture), or a recursion site added later silently mis-attributes a subtree.
- **AN EXIT ROW NAMES A *PREDICATE*, NOT A POPULATION — CLASSIFY THE SUBJECT BEFORE DESCRIBING WHO LEAVES THERE (round 797, a 14x miss inherited through two rounds).** `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`'s `!isSimpleCheckableType` exit was described from round 759 onward as "arrows and callbacks typed under an installed contextual type"; it is 48% bare Identifier and 32% PropertyAccess, arrows are 3.4%, and a contextual type is installed for 1.5% of all iterations. The predicate reads the PARAMETER (an interface is not simple-checkable) while the cost belongs to the ARGUMENT. Any "the N% that exit at X are …" sentence is a hypothesis until a per-subject census answers it — and such a census costs no boundary if it is taken inside a row the partition already opened.
- **A PARTITION OF A FUNCTION WHOSE ELIGIBILITY TEST LIVES INSIDE IT MUST OPEN ON THE WRAPPER, OR ITS LARGEST POPULATION IS INVISIBLE (round 786).** 61% of `checkAssignmentExpression`'s 17,179 invocations (10,432) exit at its own first guard doing nothing at all — a caller-side row cannot see them, and a partition opened on the function BODY would divide the cost by the wrong denominator. Check whether the caller pre-filters before choosing where to open.
- **`nodeId` RESTARTS AT 0 IN EVERY `SourceFile`, so a PROGRAM-WIDE `HashSet<Int>`/array keyed by a raw nodeId silently collapses one node per file onto each id (round 787).** A visits-vs-distinct census built that way is inflated without bound — it read 2.35x ("the walk re-checks a third of its property accesses") where the true answer, keyed by `(fileName.hashCode(), nodeId)`, is exactly **1.000**. Per-file side tables (INV.2's whole point) are fine; anything program-wide needs the file in the key. An identity set is NOT the alternative: AST nodes are data classes, so `HashSet<Node>` deep-recurses `hashCode()` (round 471).
- **A METHOD OVER 8,000 BYTECODES IS NEVER JIT-COMPILED — `DontCompileHugeMethods` is a PRODUCT flag defaulting to TRUE, so it runs INTERPRETED for the whole process and NO gate here can see it (round 802).** `-XX:+PrintCompilation` prints nothing (the compile is never *proposed*, so never *skipped* — 0 hits in an 11,796-line log), a JFR profile smears the cost onto the compiled callees, and the corpus/cost-gate measure no cost at all. **19 of 13,910 methods are over it today**, including `checkMemberAccessMissingCore` (46,567) and `forEachChild` (9,750) — which is why rounds 787–800 kept partitioning those functions and finding "no concentration". Run `python3 scripts/huge_methods.py` (static `javap` census) before and after any change that GROWS a hot function, and treat crossing 8,000 as a permanent whole-run cliff, not a style issue.
- **WHICH SEAM PINS DISCRIMINATE CANNOT BE PREDICTED FROM READING THE DOWNSTREAM GATES — round 809 predicted two and was wrong about BOTH** (the prototype seam, argued to double because the target-kind dispatch below it reaches `checkPropertyAccessAssignment`, reads 0 because that walker is silent for an `X.prototype.p` target; the class-value-vs-construct-signature seam, argued redundant because `canUseTypeEngine` skips such pairs, is the round's only single-pin failure). Reading the gates is how you CHOOSE which seams to pin; only the one-mistake-at-a-time ablation decides, and a pin that does not discriminate must be RENAMED to say what it actually tests.
- **…AND THE ESCAPE FORM IS THE PART THAT BITES: A STRIPPER THAT HANDLES `'x'` STILL DESYNCHRONISES ON `'\''` (`Checker.kt:35190`, `matchClosingParen`) — IT LANDS ON THE *CLOSING* QUOTE AND RE-OPENS A CHAR LITERAL, AND THE LENGTH-PRESERVATION INVARIANT BELOW DOES **NOT** CATCH IT (2026-08-22, (INC.7)).** It blanked ~2,500 `fun` declarations and the analyzer then printed a confident "no hazard" over a closure of ZERO functions — failing in the reassuring direction, which is the only direction that matters for a classifier. Kotlin `${…}` string templates are a second, independent runaway with the same signature. **Pin a stripper with a POSITIVE control (a known function it must still find), never with a shape invariant alone.**
- **A KOTLIN COMMENT/STRING STRIPPER USED FOR BRACE MATCHING MUST TREAT `'` AS A CHAR LITERAL (`'x'` / `'\n'` / `'\uXXXX'`), NEVER AS "SCAN TO THE NEXT APOSTROPHE" (round 809).** Checker.kt carries raw-string regexes with `'` inside a character class (`(["\'])`, `[^'"]+`); a naive scanner desynchronises there and then reports a 1,477-line function as 25,660 lines — silently, because the file still parses. The cheap invariant that catches it: every stripped line must keep its ORIGINAL LENGTH.
- **STOP THE DAEMONS *BEFORE* ANY GRADLE-INVOKING GATE, NEVER AFTER — a gate can re-trigger a compile that the stop then KILLS, leaving a WIPED output directory behind an earlier `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` (round 851, one full measurement cycle).** The canonical measuring script is build → `cost_gate.py` → `huge_methods.py` → `./gradlew --stop` + `pkill KotlinCompile[D]aemon` → measure; but `cost_gate.py` dumps the classpath through Gradle, which found `compileTestKotlinJvm` out of date, began recompiling, and had its daemon killed mid-flight — `build/classes/kotlin/jvm/test` ended with **0 class files** and both java runs died instantly. **The tell is a `ClassNotFoundException`/`Could not find or load main class` for a class the build log says it compiled**, with an output dir whose mtime is LATER than that log. Put every gradle-invoking step before the daemon stop, and check the class dir is non-empty between the stop and the first sample.
- **…AND ROUND 789's "COMMIT THE HARNESS FIRST" HAS A SECOND FORM: COMMITTING IT ONCE IS NOT ENOUGH — *any* edit made to an ablated file AFTER that commit is destroyed by the ablation's own `git checkout --` (round 851).** The round's repair of a blind pin added a function to `BenchMain.kt`; the `report-order` ablation reverted that file and deleted it, and the next build failed on an unresolved reference from a *test* file the ablation never touched. Commit before EVERY ablation batch, not once per round.
- **COMMIT A PROBE HARNESS *BEFORE* ABLATING IT — `git checkout <file>` to undo an injected fault also deletes every UNCOMMITTED change in that file, and a probe lives in the same file as the code it measures (round 789, ~15 minutes lost).** The ablation-discrimination protocol ("verify the pins fail against a broken binary") ends in a revert, and the natural revert is exactly the command that destroys the round's own work. Land the harness first, then ablate on top of a clean tree so the revert is scoped to the fault.
- **AN ABLATION DRIVER MUST PROVE IT *DISPATCHED* BEFORE ANY ARM'S RESULT IS READ — `"${@:-A1 A2 A3 A4}"` EXPANDS THE DEFAULT AS ONE WORD, so a no-argument run hits the `unknown arm` branch four times and still prints `complete; tree restored` (rounds 855/856).** Nothing is corrupted and nothing is mis-recorded, which is exactly why it is dangerous: an all-green sweep from a driver that ran no arm is indistinguishable from "every guard is redundant", and the round-807 protocol then credits pins with discrimination they have never been tested for. Use an array default (`ARMS=("$@"); [[ ${#ARMS[@]} -eq 0 ]] && ARMS=(A1 …)`), and before trusting a sweep **dry-run each arm's edit and confirm it makes a real diff that reverts clean** — a `git diff --shortstat` per arm costs seconds and is the only thing separating a clean ablation from a dead one.
- **ROUND 855's "DRY-RUN EACH ARM FOR A REAL DIFF" IS **VACUOUS** ON A TREE THAT ALREADY CARRIES THE ROUND'S OWN UNCOMMITTED WORK — `git diff --shortstat` PRINTS THE WHOLE ROUND'S DIFF, IDENTICALLY, FOR EVERY ARM (round 922: `2 files changed, 146 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)` for a1 AND a2), SO IT CANNOT TELL A LANDED EDIT FROM AN UNLANDED ONE.** Diff each ablated file against the ablation's OWN SNAPSHOT (`cmp -s "$f" "$SNAP_DIR/..."`) — the only baseline that is a property of the ARM — or land round 789's "commit the harness first", which fixes it and the restore hazard together. The other thing that carries such a run is an anchor-count assertion in the patch helper (exactly one occurrence, else exit non-zero); a substitution helper without one is a second way for an arm to be silently dead.
- **A DETACHED SCRIPT THAT REWRITES A SOURCE FILE, BUILDS, AND THEN RESTORES IT CAN BE KILLED IN THE MIDDLE — LEAVING THE ABLATED SOURCE IN THE TREE WITH NO MARKER AND NO `.done` FILE (round 805).** The A/B-vs-a-previous-commit shape (`git show HEAD~1:X > X` → build → snapshot → `git checkout X`) is the standard one, and its window is a multi-minute compile; when the script dies there, every later build silently compiles the OLD code and the only tell is `git status --porcelain` showing a file the round has not touched since committing. **Run any stage that rewrites a source file in the FOREGROUND and detach only the java runs**, and `git status` before believing any binary.
- **A SEAM PIN THAT STAYS GREEN UNDER ITS OWN ABLATION IS AS OFTEN *BLIND* AS THE GUARD IS REDUNDANT, AND THE CHEAP WAY TO TELL IS A WHOLE-OUTPUT DIFF OF THE ABLATED BINARY AGAINST THE COMMITTED ONE OVER A FAMILY OF SHAPES — NOT A GUESSED RETRY (round 813).** A pin asserting an inherited-index TS2411 read zero because a SIBLING pass emits the same diagnostic for the primitive case; running both binaries over eight variants of the shape showed exactly ONE line differing (the callable-index one), which became the pin and fails on the ablated binary and nothing else. Guessing a retry shape (rounds 810/811) had failed twice; the diff answers in one run and also tells you how much of the region is genuinely load-bearing.
- **A SUPPRESSION CHECK PLACED AT THE TOP OF A FUNCTION WHOSE EMISSIONS ARE AT THE BOTTOM IS PAID FOR BY EVERY CALLER AND USED BY ALMOST NONE — measure with an EXIT CENSUS, not with a row total (round 789).** `checkMemberAccessMissing`'s flow-graph suppression blocks are **57% of the largest leaf in the compile** and launch **31% of every narrowing walk xtsc performs**, yet 94.6% of the calls that pay for a walk exit further down the function with the answer consulted by nothing, and the emission tail those blocks defend is reached **0 times** on the compiler profile. The instrument that shows this is recording WHICH ROW EACH CALL RETURNS FROM (and, restricted to the calls that paid, which row THEY return from) — a row's nanos say where time goes, only the exit census says whether it bought anything.
- **A ROW THAT GOES TO ZERO HAS NOT NECESSARILY DELETED ANYTHING — RUN THE PRODUCED-vs-CONSUMED RATIO *BEFORE* THE TIMING, NOT AFTER (round 801, the second round in a row to lose a saving to this).** Deferring `FlowGraphBuilder`'s B464 suffix sets emptied their row (53.5 -> 0.9 ms) and its own census then read **created 1143, materialized 1143** — every value is eventually asked, so the cost merely moved into the checker. That ratio (round 800's `distinct`-vs-`calls` in its simplest form: **1.000 means MOVED**) costs two counters and one run, and it decides the whole question that a timed partition cannot. Corollary from the same round: **an allocation count is not a cost** — removing 367,189 `String` allocations from the same function measured exactly 0 ms.
- **AMENDED ROUND 864 — `Binder.bind` WAS *NOT* CLOSED, AND THE MECHANISM IS THE GENERAL ONE: ROUND 801's PARTITION STOPPED ONE LEVEL SHORT AND *NAMED ITS RESIDUE AFTER THE THING IT EXPECTED TO FIND*.** It subtracted the three B464 collectors from `FlowGraphBuilder.build` and called the ~700 ms remainder "the flow walk at 3.0 us/node"; **43% of it is a SECOND whole-tree walk in a different class** — `FlowGraph`'s constructor filling the INV.2(b) nodeId side table, 876,324 `forEachChild` visits each boxing a `(pos,end)` `Long` to ask the flow map, **70% of them answering null**. Round 864 fills that table from the 262,404 nodes `recordFlow` actually wrote (exactly equivalent: `flowAt`'s fallback IS the same map lookup, and the census says only **168** of 176,935 queries move to it) for **114 ms = 1.665% of a warm rebuild**. **A residue is not a measurement of whatever you then call it** — CLAUDE.md already says this about `checkSpine`'s "dispatch machinery, 42%", and it is the same error one region over. What SURVIVES of round 801: the numbers below, and the flow-MINTING walk (~196 ms warm = 2.9%) which now has no measurable sub-structure — **a per-node probe there would cost 83-173 ms against a 196 ms row, so anything inside it must be priced by counters plus an arm** (`--flowIndexLegacy` is the reference shape). `docs/perf/warm-flow-graph-attribution.md`.
- **`Binder.bind` IS THREE STATEMENTS (round 801, `docs/perf/bind-attribution.md`; read the amendment above BEFORE quoting it as closed).** 1,549 ms = 6.0%: `bindStatements` 31, `bindLexicalScopes` ~470 (876,201 node pops at 540 ns), `FlowGraphBuilder.build` ~1,050 (236,587 flow nodes, ~700 ms of which is the walk itself at 3.0 us/node). **And the ~400 TAIL PASSES are FLAT** — 2,962 ms, largest 75 ms = 0.26%, and **only 2 of 400 call `getTypeOfExpression` while none narrows**, so they are traversals, not type-system work, and their structural treatments are already closed. Both regions are now bounded; re-measure with `--frontEnd` and `--passTiming` before spending a round in either.
- **A SPINE HANDLER'S `SpineDispatch.enterClosure` ENTRY IS A *SYNTACTIC* CLAIM ABOUT ITS TOP-LEVEL KIND GATE, AND SINCE ROUND 888 THE PRODUCTION PROLOGUE ACTS ON IT — SO WIDENING A CLOSED HANDLER'S GATE WITHOUT WIDENING ITS CLOSURE SILENTLY DELETES A DIAGNOSTIC, AND NO GATE HERE NEED NOTICE.** `spineEnterNode` reads `spineEnterMask[kindId]` and skips 40 of its 46 handlers per node; the mask is derived from `enterClosure`, so a handler that grows a `when (kindId)` arm (or an `is T` gate) the closure does not list is never called there. **Run `python3 scripts/spine_closure_audit.py` after touching ANY `spine*EnterNode`** — it re-derives every gate from today's `Checker.kt` and fails closed on a shape it cannot read. Two corollaries: `--spineMaskOff` is the in-binary OFF arm, and it is the only way an equivalence pin can see the failure (round 888's A1 ablation reddens exactly ONE test); and a `CtaSections` table taken with the mask on is NOT comparable to a pre-888 one, because the mask refuses `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor` before the probe's `A_GATE` row can count it.
- **A SUB-REGION'S PERCENTAGE SHARE OF ITS FUNCTION RISES WHEN THE *REST* OF THE FUNCTION GETS FASTER — SO A SHARE IS NEVER A PRICE, AND ONLY THE ms IS (round 830).** `checkSinglePropertyAccess`'s dedicated-walker firewall read 8.0% at round 787 and 20.1% at round 830 with its own cost UNCHANGED (207 -> 209 ms): the engine row beside it fell 2,364 -> 830 ms because rounds 788-795 landed levers in it. A rising share is evidence the *denominator* shrank; quote what deleting a region would return in ms before reading its share as a reason to act. **(ENGINE.3)/(SCOPE.1) are CLOSED on exactly this arithmetic — do not re-raise the § 0.1 "replace the ~1,046 walkers with engine rules" endgame** (four sites, one binary: layer 757 ms = 3.13%, ~22 ms deletable, largest group at EVERY site a rule tsc also has, so it MOVES; `docs/perf/engine-rule-price.md` §§ 9-11).
- **A COLD PER-HANDLER TABLE CANNOT BE READ AS A WARM ONE — a handler's warm share is its cold share x (the spine's warm-up / its OWN), and those differ by up to 1.5x, which SWAPS the top two (round 847, `docs/perf/warm-spine-attribution.md` § 4).** Cold #1 is `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor` (19.1%); warm #1 is `ccetSpineLeave` (18.2%) because it warms 2.75x against the spine's 3.38x, and `ctaSpineEnter` rises 5.4% -> 7.5% at 2.28x. The per-KIND shape by contrast IS regime-invariant (the five statement anchors: 39.9% warm vs 40.0% cold), so re-using a cold KIND table is safe and re-using a cold HANDLER ordering is not. Take a warm one with `BenchMain <proj> 6 8 spine,dispatch,spine,dispatch`.
- **A COUNT OF *SITES* IS NOT A COUNT OF *CALLS*, AND A TRANSFER AUDIT IMPORTS A MECHANISM BUT NEVER A POPULATION — (WARM.32)'s 215 iterator sites make **495,305** calls per warm rebuild where the queue entry's projection implied 4-6x more, and the family prices at **3.90 ms = 0.074%** (round 905, REFUSED by 4.4x).** `forEachChild`'s 70 `list.forEach` positions are only **275,477** calls (most children are direct `action(x)` positions — IDENTIFIER alone is 44.5% of nodes and has none) over lists averaging **1.99** elements, 7.0% EMPTY and 52.4% SINGLETON; only **219,828** of round 875's 3.32 M edge evaluations ever reach an `.any { it === child }`. Measured premium of a heap-iterator loop over an indexed one: **11.95 ns** and **2.75 ns** per call, against the **34.3 ns** the floor would need. **A sibling project's −3.1% on the identical mechanism does not transfer, and neither does any MB figure** — round 801 removed 367,189 `String` allocations for 0 ms and round 893 put warm GC at ~1.7% of wall, so this family is priced in TIME or not at all. `docs/perf/iterator-allocation-price.md`; the two homes (`walkList`/`anyIdentical`, NodeWalk.kt) and `--iterCensus`/`--iterAmp` survive, and routing the 215 sites through them shrank `forEachChild`'s three (JIT.1) partitions 9,256 → 5,929 bytecodes.
- **…AND ROUND 904's "a single-`r` `A - B` over-reads by up to 23%" IS REALLY **76%** — the two arms' fitted boundaries differed 60.0 vs 43.3 ns, because the boundary a fit recovers absorbs everything charged per CALL rather than per rep, and arm A constructs an iterator there while arm B does not (round 905).** So the arms' per-call fixed costs are NOT the same quantity and there is no reason they should be: **fit `p(r) = cost + boundary/r` PER ARM and difference the COSTS, never the `p(r)`s.** The check that says the fit is right is that `fitted premium + (b_A - b_B)/r` reproduces the measured single-`r` `A - B` at every `r` — it closed to **0.01 ns** at both `r` on both halves here.
- **A COST PRIOR FROM ONE FAMILY OF SITES DOES NOT TRANSFER TO ANOTHER (round 789, two predictions falsified in one round).** (ENGINE.1) measured "compute the SOURCE type" as the largest row at all three assignability sites, so round 789 predicted the receiver-type computation would dominate a property access; it is **26.4%** while the FP firewall is **67.2%**. At an assignability site the source type IS the work; at a property access the receiver type is largely resolved already. Round 787 made the mirror-image error in the other direction. State which SHAPE of site a prior came from before reusing it.
- **A SUPPRESSION THAT ONLY EVER `return`s CAN BE DEFERRED TO THE EMISSION WITHOUT ENUMERATING THE EMISSION SITES — THE OBLIGATION IT CREATES IS THAT ITS FUNCTION'S ONLY MUTATION STAYS `diagnostics.add` (round 791, `checkMemberAccessMissing`).** Because the blocks suppressed *everything below*, the deferral has to undo the BODY, not adjudicate ~20 sites: run the body, then remove what it appended at or after the blocks' old position. That is equivalent by construction, and a NEW emission site is covered automatically — but ONLY while the function and its `tryEmit*` helpers append and do nothing else (no `removeAll`, no side-set write, no ambient install, no read-back of `diagnostics`). **Adding any of those to `checkMemberAccessMissingCore` silently breaks `cmamFlowSuppresses` — grep before you add.** Two further traps: the retraction FLOOR is `cmamFlowBase` (the blocks' old position), never `diagnostics.size` at entry, because one emission sits ABOVE it and was never theirs to suppress; and the deferred call must stay INSIDE the same invocation, which is what makes it immune to the round-788 lazy-thunk failure (a thunk forced in a different dynamic scope needs every save/restore site to cooperate).
- **A SINGLE BEFORE/AFTER PAIR CANNOT TELL "THE WORK MOVED" (round 788's law) FROM "THAT RUN WAS SLOW" — RESTORE THE OLD BEHAVIOUR ON THE *NEW* BINARY AND RUN BOTH ARMS TWICE (round 795).** Deferring the TS2793 probe made the next row read 1,442 ms against HEAD's 1,361 with its containing span flat: the textbook picture of a removed cost reappearing in its next asker. Two runs per arm on ONE binary — the eager evaluation restored by the verify flag, which also keeps the boundary count identical (round 793's law) — showed that row flat in both arms. Any verify flag that re-enables the old computation doubles as this instrument; build it that way.
- **A FIXTURE THAT NARROWS A REFERENCE AT AN *ARGUMENT* MUST GUARD WITH AN EARLY RETURN (or a ternary / `&&`), NEVER `if (guard) { … }` (round 796).** Since round 785 a type-guard CALL in an `if` condition writes its narrow into `currentLocalTypes` for the THEN branch, so the argument arrives ALREADY narrowed, the flow read never happens, and any pin about argument-position narrowing measures an empty population — a whole fixture read `refused == reached` with zero substitutions before this was found. Same list CLAUDE.md already gives for enums, now with a second consumer.
- **AN EXIT CENSUS CAN BE ADDED TO AN EXISTING PARTITION FOR *ZERO* NEW BOUNDARIES — record the row that is ALREADY OPEN at a boundary the partition was ALREADY crossing (round 796).** That is what keeps a before/after ROW comparison valid under round 793's law, and it is strictly stronger than differencing adjacent rows' `calls` (which cannot see a `return` inside a loop, charges a prologue return to nothing, and can say WHERE an iteration left but never WHAT it had already paid for). Print the sums as partition checks against the rows they re-attribute; anything less than EXACT means the hooks miss an exit path.
- **TWO GUARDS AT TWO LAYERS ON THE SAME PROPERTY ARE INDISTINGUISHABLE TO *ANY* ABLATION WHEN THE LATER LAYER REFUSES EXACTLY WHAT THE EARLIER ONE WOULD GET WRONG — AND NEITHER IS REDUNDANT (round 927, arms A3/A8, identical 3-test red sets).** A wrong rename-expansion direction and a wrong verification EXPECTATION for it both turn a working rename into a REFUSAL, because the applied-and-recompiled check catches the bad text; they differ only in the conflict's DETAIL, which no pin can assert about an ablated binary. Round 807's "a signal with no uniquely-its-own failure is a REDUNDANT guard" needs this qualifier — record such a pair as ONE observable and say which layer does what.
- **A COMBINED ABLATION CANNOT ATTRIBUTE — RUN ONE MISTAKE AT A TIME, OR A PIN GETS CREDITED WITH DISCRIMINATION IT DOES NOT HAVE (round 807).** Six deliberate seam mistakes injected TOGETHER failed six pins and read as full coverage; re-run alone, one of them (`caasTypeParamConstraintArg`'s `CAAS_CONTINUE`) left every pin green, because a LATER guard (`caasNonSimpleParamChecks`' own CONTINUE) makes the same decision one helper on. That is a second, distinct mechanism for a blind seam pin beyond round 806's ambient-reinstall one, and the only instrument that finds either is the single-mistake ablation. A signal with no uniquely-its-own failure is a REDUNDANT guard — say so, do not claim the pin.
- **EXTRACTING A RUN OF A *LOOP BODY* MEANS ENCODING `continue`/`break`/`return` AS A RETURNED SIGNAL, AND THE BINDING QUESTION MUST BE ANSWERED BY A PARSER, NOT BY INDENTATION (round 807, `checkArgumentsAgainstSignatureCore`).** Only one direction is compiler-caught: an outer-binding `continue` left unconverted is `break and continue are only allowed inside a loop`, while an INNER token wrongly rewritten to a `return` compiles and is silently wrong. Classify with a brace matcher over the string/comment-stripped source. Also: a split that lands JUST over the 8,000-byte limit (this one hit 8,061) is one extraction short, not a failed split — budget a second pass.
- **A SEAM PIN FOR AN EARLY `return` DISCRIMINATES ONLY IF SOMETHING DOWNSTREAM WOULD ACTUALLY EMIT — in the assignability bodies it usually would not (round 808, 3 of 5 seams undiscriminated).** Dropping an extracted region's `return true` does not delete its emission; it only loses the early exit, and every later emitter in `checkVarDeclAssignabilityCore` / its siblings is itself conditioned on the relation verdict (`canUse && !isAssignable`, or `isAssignable`) — which those regions' shapes fail to satisfy anyway. So the signal is a redundant guard and no pin can see it. Check what the NEXT emitter's gate is before writing the pin, and record an undiscriminated seam rather than claiming coverage. **Round 810 adds the sharpest case: when everything after the seam is a LEGACY DOUBLE-CHECK, no shape can discriminate it** — `checkReturnAssignabilityCore`'s elaboration is followed only by the string-based fallback, which emits for none of the shapes the engine has already rejected (literal, widened literal, union, TP target and interface-parameter sources were all tried, in two separate builds). Ask "can the code after this `return` emit at all?" before spending a build.
- **A KOTLIN-DAEMON `GC overhead limit exceeded` DURING AN ABLATION BATCH LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A CLEAN ABLATION (round 808).** Back-to-back `compileKotlinJvm` invocations accrete daemon heap until BUILD.1's 5 GB stops being enough; the build then fails, `jvmTest` never starts, and the XML parser reports **0 pins ran** — indistinguishable from "the mistake changed nothing". Grep the ablation's own build log for `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` before recording a zero, and `./gradlew --stop` + a graceful bracket-pattern Kotlin-daemon kill between batches.
- **A DIFFERENTIAL CALIBRATION IS ONLY AS SHARP AS THE SMALLER OF ITS TWO SPREADS (round 756).** The sanctioned ON-vs-COARSE probe calibration FAILED on level D: Δ = 29 ms against a 26 ms within-mode spread, bounding the boundary at 31–168 ns and no better. Before quoting a differential, print both modes' run-to-run spread; if Δ is not several times the larger spread, say so and fall back to comparing both modes against the PROBE-FREE binary (which here gave 138 and 168 ns, and only the top of the range reconciled the partition with the independently measured total).
- **…AND ROUND 853's LAW HAS A BUILD-LEVEL TWIN: `./gradlew … -q 2>&1 | grep -E '^(e:|w:)'` PRINTS NOTHING FOR A SUCCESSFUL COMPILE, A SKIPPED ONE AND ONE WHOSE OUTPUT HAS NOT LANDED ALIKE, SO "BUILD_DONE" IS NOT EVIDENCE THAT YOUR EDIT IS IN THE CLASS DIR (round 947, ~40 minutes: a `Checker.kt` arm read INERT through three probe cycles and was correct all along).** **Verify a build with a POSITIVE CONTROL, never with its own success message** — make the edited code produce an effect that MUST be observable (round 947 had the walker register a marker name derived from the node class and probed for that name; one run, source to class file to behaviour). Two things that are NOT that control: `println` (`runCli` captures stdout, so a debug hook in the `Diagnostic` constructor prints nothing), and `strings <class> | grep <literal>` (`strings` defaults to a 4-character minimum, so a short literal reads as absent from a class file that contains it).
- **A GATE THAT *PREPENDS* A CLASS DIR CAN LOAD A FROZEN COMPILER, AND ITS SYMPTOM IS THE ANSWER YOU WANTED: `+0.00% ON ALL 18 COUNTERS`, FOREVER (round 853, three scripts, one root cause).** `jvmRuntimeClasspath` resolves DEPENDENCIES only, so `cost_gate.py` must prepend the compiler's own classes — and it prepended the pre-MOD.3 ROOT `build/classes/kotlin/jvm/main`, which won every load; `huge_methods.py` censused the same leftover. Both were green and blind from the module split until round 853, hiding a real **+79% `narrow.walks`**. **So: a gate reading a class DIRECTORY needs a positive control that the code under test is in it** (a class the round just added, or `MainKt` under the module dir — both scripts now have one), and **a run of identical `+0.00%` across rounds that landed real checker work is a REASON TO AUDIT THE INSTRUMENT, not a clean bill of health.** The suite-side twin (`HugeMethodLimitTest`) was never fooled because it locates classes from a marker RESOURCE on the test classpath — which is the general fix, and why CLAUDE.md's "the suite test is the SECOND instrument on purpose" earned its place twice.
- **A TEST ASSERTION OVER A *TIMED* SUB-MILLISECOND REGION IS NOT A PIN, IT IS A COIN FLIP — AND THE FIX IS TO ASSERT AN ORDERING OVER RECORDED OPEN/CLOSE TIMESTAMPS, WHICH IS DETERMINISTIC (round 868).** `PostCheckerPartitionTest`'s `residue * 4 < post` over a 272 us region failed once in a 14,120-test suite and passed in isolation; one stop-the-world pause in the residue window is all it takes, and a pin that cries wolf is ignored on the day it is right. A probe's `close` already holds both timestamps, so recording `firstAt`/`lastAt` per section lets a partition be asserted as "records exactly once, `nanos == lastAt - firstAt`, nested inside the region it decomposes, siblings ordered and disjoint" — facts about a monotonic clock. **State what the swap costs**: an unattributed GAP of real work between two blocks is no longer detectable, because bounding a gap means bounding time.
- **A JFR SHARE IS A SHARE OF *WALL TIME IN A FIXED WINDOW*, NOT OF A REBUILD — SO AFTER A ROUND MAKES THE COMPILE FASTER, EVERY UNCHANGED COST'S SHARE *RISES*, AND A CROSS-ROUND TABLE MUST BE CONVERTED TO ms/REBUILD BEFORE ANY ROW IS READ AS A REGRESSION (round 870).** The window is `delay=60s,duration=90s`, so both rounds draw ~8,000 samples however fast the compile is; rounds 868/869 removed ~10%, and `ctaSpineEnter`/`ctaFnBodyFrame`/`lookupPerFileForNode` all "rose" by exactly the denominator ratio 7,766/7,068 = 1.099 while costing the same ms. Multiply share by THAT round's median rebuild, and re-aggregate the OLD dumps with the CURRENT script (they are kept under `build/bench/round86*/deep*.txt`) so the two tables differ only in the binary — `scripts/leaf_owner_profile.py` is that script and carries round 868's three traps (refuses a 5-frame-truncated dump, filters to `xtsc-deep-stack`, charges stdlib leaves to the nearest non-stdlib OWNER).
- **A JFR OWNER/FAMILY TOTAL IS A *LOCATION*, NOT A PRICE — DIVIDE IT BY THE OWNER'S OWN OPERATION COUNT AND REFUSE ANY IMPLIED PER-OP COST THAT IS PHYSICALLY IMPOSSIBLE, BEFORE A BUILD (rounds 894/896/898/899/903: TEN candidates ranked off JFR rows, NINE measured, ALL over by 2.1-21x, clustering at ~3x wherever the owner really is one map operation).** 110 ns to copy one map entry and 1,164 ns to add one `Integer` to a 3-element set are refutations on their face; a `String`-keyed probe with a cached hash is 20-50 ns, an equal-but-distinct `String.equals` 14.6 ns, an insert tens of ns. Corollaries: **name the ONE map operation the row is supposed to be** (round 896's candidate (4) was real cost in a function that was not that set at all); **price a container swap NET** (round 899: `nodeToFlow`'s -42.6 ms gross returned +19.1 ms as the replacement's own frames); and **a real cost with no known lever is CLOSED, not open** (`lookupPerFileForNode`'s 29 ns/probe is textbook and has no fix). `docs/perf/warm-hash-owner-census.md` § 12, `docs/perf/warm-leaf-profile.md` § 33.8.
- **…AND THE ONE ROW THIS FILE CERTIFIED AS "WORTH BELIEVING" WAS THE NINTH OVER-READ, BY 6.3x — BECAUSE A PLAUSIBILITY CHECK THAT APPEALS TO AN UNMEASURED MAGNITUDE IS NOT A CHECK, IT IS THE HYPOTHESIS RESTATED (round 903).** `getTypeFromTypeNodeCore`'s 161 ns/op was admitted here for four rounds on the grounds that its key is an AST *data class* with a recursive `hashCode` (round 471) — true, and it licenses *any* rate you like, because nobody had measured how deep the recursion goes. **It is 2.7567 nodes probe-weighted, 73.6% of probes presenting a key of at most TWO nodes**; 161 ns would need a **29.4**-node mean, i.e. 10.7x what exists. Measured: the deep key's whole premium over a `(file, nodeId)` `LongKeyMap` is **15.09 − 2.11 = 12.98 ns**, so all 354,131 hash-and-equals ops are **4.60 ms = 0.085%** and the candidate is **REFUSED by 3.7x** (`docs/perf/type-node-key-price.md`). **So when a row survives the division, the survival is a claim about a POPULATION — name that population and census it before the row earns any status at all**; and note the family had a *second owner* the leaf profile could not separate, `isPerFileDependentRefNode`, a recursive walk over the same subtree that runs on every call and prices at 3.70 ms. Two owners summing to 9.04 ms against a 57.1 ms row is what the 6.3x is.
- **A POPULATION QUOTED FROM A SOURCE COMMENT IS NOT A MEASUREMENT, AND IT CAN BE WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS AT ONCE — the queue's "~88 k/rebuild" for `mappedNodeTypeKey` was a transcribed KDoc that is itself 26% stale (the real call count is **110,780**) applied to the wrong quantity (only **25,987** calls reach the key build a fix would act on; 76.5% exit at the foreign-file gate first), so the ranked population was 3.4x high on the one number that mattered (round 912).** Census at the granularity the FIX acts on before pricing anything. Reusable bar from the same round, the allocation twin of round 904's ~1.7 M map-ops: **a pure-allocation candidate needs > 113,000 allocations/rebuild at a generous 150 ns, or > 340,000 at a realistic 50 ns, to clear the ~17 ms floor** — which refuses most per-node allocation candidates by arithmetic against a whole-spine population of 856,962 nodes. `docs/perf/round912-candidate-census.md`.
