- **A BAD `HashMap` KEY IS PRICED BY `HashMap$TreeNode` IN THE LEAF PROFILE AND BY NOTHING ELSE — IT WAS **5.91% OF WARM WALL (348.8 ms/rebuild) AT ROUND 888 AND IS *ZERO SAMPLES* AT ROUND 893**, i.e. rounds 889/890 under-priced their own fix ~9x (round 893).** An amplifier counts map OPERATIONS while a treeified bucket's cost is superlinear in its DEPTH, so a per-operation price is structurally an under-read; `Relation.packKey`'s max bucket was 1,140. Any hash-family pattern must be a `HashMap` SUBSTRING test (as `scripts/leaf_owner_profile.py` does), never `"java.util.HashMap."` — the prefix form misses `HashMap$TreeNode.*`, which is the part with the mechanism, and round 889 lost it for two rounds behind one absent `$`.
- **THE SCANNER AND PARSER DO NOT RUN ON A WARM REBUILD — `CrawlParseCache` serves the program's parse by CONTENT and `RealLibSnapshots` the libs' — AND WHEN THEY DO RUN THEY RUN ON N CONCURRENT `Dispatchers.Default` WORKERS (round 897).** Two consequences that bite in opposite directions: a front-end cost measured warm reads ZERO and must be measured with the cache off, and **any process-global table written from `scanIdentifier`/`Parser` is round 825's data race with no exception to find it by** — `CrawlParseCache.store` lives in the single-threaded fold after the flow is drained for exactly this reason, and its `lookup` is read-only. The tell that you are in the second half: a counter hooked in the Scanner disagrees across processes on one binary while a checker counter is identical to the last digit. A per-FILE table is the only lock-free option and it captures ~none of any cross-file prize.
- **A LEAF-FRAME PROFILE CANNOT SEE A WORKING-SET COLLAPSE, SO A `String`-KEY CANDIDATE RANKED OFF `String.equals`/`String.hashCode` ROWS IS UNDER-READ ~2x (round 897).** Interning names is ~half `String.equals` (measured **14.6 ns** per equal-but-distinct ~10-char compare) and ~half the KEY OBJECTS' cache locality — ~400 k live `String` instances collapsing onto ~22.4 k — which is charged to whatever frame dereferences the object, never to `String.equals`. The control that separates them is a container the probes almost never HIT: any delta there cannot be `equals`, because a `HashMap` calls it only for a matching 32-bit hash.
- **`docs/perf/warm-hash-owner-census.md` § 9 IS A *LOCATION* LIST, NOT A PRICE LIST — EIGHT OF ITS NINE CEILINGS ARE NOW MEASURED BY A NON-JFR INSTRUMENT AND *EVERY ONE IS OVER*, BY 2.1x TO 21x (rounds 896/897/898).** The ratios cluster at ~3x wherever the owner really is one map operation and run higher where the row was mostly something else. **Two BUILD-FREE steps stand between a row there and a decision: divide the owner row by its own POPULATION, and name the ONE map operation the row is supposed to be** — 110 ns to copy one map entry (candidate 8) and 1,164 ns to add one `Integer` to a 3-element set (candidate 4) are refutations on their face. Mechanism: a JFR leaf/EXTENDED share over a tight allocating loop (`HashMap.putMapEntries`) is round 623's counted-loop bias, and the EXTENDED rule amplifies it by design.
- **AN AMPLIFICATION LADDER'S TWO MIRRORED ROTATIONS ARE BOTH BALANCED PALINDROMES, SO A *LINEAR* DRIFT CANCELS IN EACH BY CONSTRUCTION — AND THEY STILL DISAGREED 2x, 4x AND BY SIGN, BECAUSE THE WARM-UP IS STILL RUNNING AT REBUILD 14 (round 898).** What a palindrome cannot cancel is the NON-linear remainder. **The fix is FEWER ARMS and a bigger `r`, never more rotations**: a slope is a difference of two arm MEANS, so a third arm buys a mid-point nothing is differenced against. Two arms at `r = 64` x 8 draws x 2 processes separated every treated draw from every control draw, where three arms at `r <= 32` x 4 draws did not.
- **A GC EXPLANATION FOR A WALL DELTA IS REFUTED BY THE PAUSE *BUDGET*, REGARDLESS OF SIGN, AND ONE `-Xlog:gc` PROCESS PER ARM SETTLES IT (round 893).** Warm GC here is **~92-98 ms of a ~5.4 s rebuild = ~1.7%** on both arms, so nothing GC-shaped can explain an 8% effect — and the faster binary in fact took MORE pauses (86 vs 68) after 2.2 M copied map entries were removed. Removing allocations is not a GC lever at this scale (round 801's "an allocation count is not a cost", one instrument over).
- **A MEMO HIDES ITS *MISS* COST FROM EVERY SECTION PROBE IN THIS REPO, WHICH IS WHY A MEMOIZED FUNCTION IS A PLACE TO LOOK FOR UNBRACKETED WORK RATHER THAN EVIDENCE THAT THE WORK IS BOUNDED (round 870).** `getTypeParamInfo` answers **96.2%** of its 28,663 questions from cache and the surviving 3.8% cost **82 ms = 1.16% of a warm rebuild**, because each miss iterated **2,992,718** symbols to find **2,184** module-flagged ones — the miss's cost lands in whichever ordinary checker predicate happened to ask first (`checkTypeArgCount`, `isUnresolvedGenericType`), so no pass row names it. The general shape to look for: **a miss body that re-derives a property of the FROZEN binder tables per call, when only the key varies** — hoist the table-derived part (`buildModuleSymbolScanIndex`), leave the key-dependent probe live, and the row is CONTROLLED because the population cannot move.
- **A `--serve` REQUEST PAYS ~2 ms OVER AN IN-PROCESS `BenchMain` REBUILD, AND THE ~279 ms A CLIENT ADDITIONALLY WAITS IS A FRESH CLIENT JVM, NOT THE DAEMON (round 871).** Bracket a request three ways — client wall ⊃ server `CompileResponse.elapsedMs` ⊃ the compiler's own `time:` line, all three already printed by the shipping binary — before theorising about daemon overhead; `runCli`'s whole envelope (argument parse, mode ledger, diagnostic formatting, stdout capture, JSON encode) is 1–3 ms warm. So **a warm figure taken with `BenchMain` IS the daemon figure**, and the client cost is the native thin client's job. `scripts/round871-serve-ladder.sh`, `docs/perf/warm-serve-request-attribution.md`.
- **A PARSE MAY BE SHARED ACROSS PROGRAMS — THE AST IS WRITTEN ONLY BY `indexSourceFile` AND `Parser` IS PURE IN `(source, fileName, flags)` — BUT ANY CROSS-REQUEST REUSE MUST BE KEYED ON THE **CONTENT**, NEVER ON AN mtime/size/stat (round 871).** `RealLibSnapshots` has done this for lib files since M2.1(c) and `CrawlParseCache` now does it for program files; the crawl reads every file on every request anyway, so the bytes are in hand and staleness is not expressible. **And know WHERE a mis-keyed hit bites: the compilation core re-checks content at `ParsedSource` (INV.1(e)), so a wrong hit there degrades to a redundant parse and a CORRECT type-check — the place with no second gate is the CRAWL, which has already used the stale tree's `moduleSpecifiers` to decide WHICH FILES EXIST.** The wrong answer is a missing FILE, not a wrong type, and only a pin whose edit ADDS AN IMPORT sees it. BINDING is still per-consumer (symbol merging mutates its inputs).
- **INTERLEAVING A "PARSE" ARM WITH A "NO-PARSE" ARM REQUEST-BY-REQUEST INFLATES THE PARSING ARM BY ~6x — BLOCK THEM (round 871).** One `--parseCacheOff` request dropped into a run of cached ones read **896 ms** of crawl WALL against a blocked-arm median of 138 ms, because the parser had not executed for several requests and was no longer hot. The usual ABBA-rotation advice assumes both arms exercise the same code every draw; when an arm's whole point is that some code does NOT run, rotation de-optimises it.
- **A NEW CLI FLAG THAT IS NOT IN THE USAGE TEXT FAILS `CliModeRestoreTest` *BEFORE* ITS `ledger.restore()`, WHICH LEAVES EVERY SWEPT MODE ARMED FOR THE REST OF THE TEST JVM — AND THE SYMPTOM IS TEN FAILURES IN SIX UNRELATED CLASSES PLUS A CRASHED TEST PROCESS (round 874, ~40 min).** Its sweep derives the flag list FROM the usage text, so an undocumented flag is an unreached flag; the `unreached.isEmpty()` assertion sits above the restore, so `--frontEnd`, `--flowCensus` and the rest stay ON, and a census `ArrayList` then takes appends from the compile thread of every later test — `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: Index 1 out of bounds for length 0` inside `ArrayList.add`, i.e. a data-race signature with no data race in the round's own change. **The tell is that the failures are all in probe/census tests and reproduce ONLY when `CliModeRestoreTest` is in the same run**; document the flag (usage text + `ACCEPTED_FLAGS`, and `BEHAVIOUR_CHANGING` if it changes a decision) and they all go away.
- **A LEAF PROFILE MUST BE READ BY MECHANISM AS WELL AS BY ROW — round 874's whole finding is invisible row by row and is 6.95% by family.** The INV.4 reach classifiers (`spine*Status`/`*Edge`/`*Reached`/`tav*`) are **458.8 ms over 66 owners** of which the largest is 0.57%, i.e. below every candidate floor while being the biggest thing in the warm compile; round 868's own win had the same shape and was caught only because its three rows happened to be adjacent and share a name prefix. `scripts/round874_compare.py` does the aggregation, and its other job is round 870's denominator trap — a share is a share of WALL TIME, so quote ms/rebuild or an unchanged cost "rises" after every win.
- **ON tsc's OWN SOURCES THE WHOLE 46-DIAGNOSTIC OUTPUT IS NETTED BY *ONE* PASS (`checkSpine`), SO ANY PER-PASS OR PER-PARTITION DIAGNOSTICS EQUIVALENCE TAKEN ON A DASHBOARD PROFILE COMPARES AN ESSENTIALLY EMPTY POPULATION — AND ALL EIGHT PROFILES ARE THAT SAME CODEBASE (2026-08-23, (INC.17) census, measured by `PassTiming.diagNetByPass` reading 46 against the build's own 46).** `scripts/partition-equivalence.sh` inherits it: 78 files, 46 rows, one emitter — so a change that silently stopped 204 of the 205 partition-reading passes from emitting would print EQUIVALENT. What actually carries such work is the 13k-baseline CORPUS, which has no partition; there is no instrument here exercising many EMITTING passes and a PARTITION at once ((INC.18)). Read a green profile-side partition sweep as a control, never as coverage.
- **COST GATE (COST.1, round 717): anything that touches the checker runs `python3 scripts/cost_gate.py` alongside the suite.** It runs the compiler profile with `--passTiming`, extracts the DETERMINISTIC counters (getTypeOfExpression calls, narrowWalks, spineNodes, typeNode cacheable/bypassed, globals lookups) and diffs them against `docs/perf/cost-counters.txt`, failing above ±2%. Counters, never wall time — a loaded box swings ±13% and cannot see a 70 ms regression (round 713 added +11.5% getTypeOfExpression calls for one diagnostic and no gate noticed). An increase is not a veto but an accounting demand: justify it in the session note and `--update` the baseline in the SAME commit. Needs `build/bench/tsc-project-*` (create once with `scripts/bench-compile-tsc.sh --project compiler --no-emit --no-log`), and must NEVER run while `jvmTest` is in flight.
- **A `<clinit>` CAN BE OVER THE 8,000-BYTECODE LIMIT, AND ONLY ITS *COLLECTION* CONSTANTS ARE IN IT (round 820).** A Kotlin `private const val` of a primitive/`String` is emitted with a `ConstantValue` attribute and costs the static initializer NOTHING, so ~200 dispatch tags in `Checker`'s companion were free while 50 `setOf`/`mapOf` literals held all 10,339 bytecodes. It shrinks ONLY by hoisting those literals into functions it calls, and they must be **TOP-LEVEL** (`CheckerKt`, an `invokestatic`) — a companion `private fun` is an instance method on `Checker$Companion`, reached through the very static field `<clinit>` is installing. **The constraint that decides what can move: a `private` companion member is INVISIBLE to a top-level function in the same file**, so a literal whose initializer reads one (e.g. `LIB_MIN_TARGET`'s `+ TYPED_ARRAY_NAMES.flatMap { … }` tail) can only move in part. **And the hoist's whole risk is a WRONG-SET SUBSTITUTION**: same-typed builders (five return `Set<String>`) are interchangeable to the type checker, so a mixed-up call site compiles clean and silently changes which names the checker believes exist — `ClinitConstantHoistTest` is the only thing that sees it. Nothing here is priceable: a static initializer runs once, at class load.
- **THE COLD A/B IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO A STEADY-STATE-ONLY EFFECT, AND (JIT.1) IS THE MEASURED PROOF: the huge-method arc reads `+0.08%, B wins 3/5, NOISE-DOMINATED` cold and **-33.6%, 4/4 pairs, 1.505x** WARM (round 845, r802's parent vs r821's close, arms verified at 19-over-limit vs 0 in the bytecode).** So a method crossing 8,000 is a **warm-path** defect: `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` protects the daemon/`--serve`/AOT artifact far more than the CLI, and any change that grows a hot method must be A/B'd with `ab-warm.sh`, never `ab-interleaved.sh`. Corollary for the ~1% quiet-box sd rule: it is calibrated for the +-1% regime and may be overridden EXPLICITLY when the effect is tens of times the arm sd - say so, never silently.
- **A `java.util.regex` SCAN OF WHOLE SOURCE TEXT DOES NOT WARM UP (~1.5x) WHILE OUR OWN HAND-WRITTEN SCANNERS WARM 3.3x — SO AN UNGATED WHOLE-PROGRAM REGEX IS A *WARM-PATH* DEFECT THAT A COLD TABLE RANKS AS UNREMARKABLE (round 859).** Two passes running the same `export as namespace` pattern over all 9,977,097 chars are 0.38% of a cold compile and **1.30% of a warm one**, warming 0.85x and 1.05x against a tail median of 2.90x — and the pattern matches ZERO times on tsc's own sources. The fix shape is already in `Checker.kt`: `checkExportAsNamespaceSelfCycle` runs essentially the same pattern for **0.0 ms** because cheap guards sit ABOVE its `findAll`. Gate before you scan, and never rank a text-scanning pass from a cold table (`docs/perf/warm-tail-attribution.md` § 4).
- **A METHOD OVER 8,000 BYTECODES IS NEVER JIT-COMPILED AND NOTHING IN THIS REPO EXCEPT `scripts/huge_methods.py` CAN SEE IT (rounds 802/803).** HotSpot's `DontCompileHugeMethods` is a product flag defaulting to true, so such a method runs INTERPRETED for the whole process: the corpus does not measure cost, `cost_gate.py`'s counters do not move, and `-XX:+PrintCompilation` prints no "too large" line (the compile is never *proposed*). Splitting `forEachChild` (9,750 -> 4,353/2,728/2,175) measured **-3.93%, 5/5 pairs** on its own. **So: before "just adding an arm" to a big `when`, run the census — and split by a CONTIGUOUS key range** (each part stays one tableswitch), **keeping the hottest keys in the ENTRY function** and dispatching continuations with one compare, never a fall-through chain. **JIT GATE (JIT.1)(f), round 817 — a round that touches compiled code runs `python3 scripts/huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` next to `cost_gate.py`, and `HugeMethodLimitTest` runs the same whole-program census inside the suite.** **The census is 0 as of round 821 and (JIT.1) is CLOSED** — so the gate's whole job now is to fail the moment a NEW method crosses 8,000, which is exactly how this family grew unnoticed for 800 rounds. **The suite test is the SECOND instrument on purpose, and it paid for itself on its first run: `javap` prints the static initializer as `static {};` with no `(`, so the script's header regex skipped it and charged 10,339 bytecodes to the 16-byte access bridge that precedes it — the queue carried `access$checkBigintPropertyNames$emit` as a split target for fourteen rounds and the real offender, `Checker.<clinit>`, was never seen.** Read `Code` attribute lengths from the class file when the answer matters; a `javap` rendering is a parse away from the truth. **Never raise it to make a red gate green; split the method — and note the suite test also FAILS on a STALE `KNOWN_OVER_LIMIT` entry, so a landed split cannot leave the ratchet loose. **AND THE CENSUS IS `-core`-ONLY BY DEFAULT (`--classes` defaults to the core module's class dir), SO IT IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO EVERY OTHER MODULE — a green run on a round that added compiled code OUTSIDE core is a control, not a gate (round 909, whose new module was invisible until it was passed `--classes <module>/build/classes/kotlin/jvm/main` explicitly; the tell is a `classes scanned` count identical to the previous round's).**** `--fail-over 0` is the end state. (Wiring it into Gradle's `check` is a build-system change, owner-gated as (JIT.3).)
- **EXTRACTING A REGION THAT `continue`s THE CALLER'S LOOP: WRAP IT IN A ONE-ITERATION FRAME, `for (x in listOf(xIn)) { <the region, verbatim> }` — naming the frame variable what the region already called it (round 819, `Transformer.transformToCommonJS`).** A `continue` cannot survive extraction into a member function, and rewriting it to `return <holder>` edits the moved text at exactly the sites hardest to verify; a single-element loop makes `continue` mean what it meant before (abandon the rest of THIS item) and leaves the control-flow token census unchanged on both sides. **Decide WHICH regions need it with a brace-depth scan** (`scripts/tcjs_split_verify.outer_continues`), never by eye — a by-eye census of one arm said four and the scan said five — and assert both directions (every region with a caller-loop `continue` is framed, no other one is). The frame is load-bearing: `listOf(xIn, xIn)` type-checks and processes every item twice.
- **A RECORDED COUNTER BASELINE IS A CLAIM ABOUT A BUILD, NOT ABOUT A COMMIT — when the gate fails with no source change, REBUILD THE COMMIT THAT RECORDED THE BASELINE before believing either number (round 776).** Round 760's rebaseline recorded `typeNode.bypassed 104,162 -> 95,909`; its own commit, rebuilt, measures 104,162, and ablating the veto it credited moves the counter by ONE resolution — five later rounds re-measured against that unreproducible state and inherited it. A recorded delta is not evidence; a rebuild is.
- **A JVM CONSTRUCTOR CANNOT DELEGATE ITS PROPERTY INITIALIZERS, SO `Checker.<init>`'s REMAINING HEADROOM IS SPENT BY ADDING *FIELDS*, NOT PASSES (round 814, (JIT.1)(d)).** After the split, **5,538** of its 8,000-byte budget is the class's **494 `private val` initializers** and only ~34 is the dispatch sequence — every `pass("name") { … }` line now lives in an `initSetupPasses`/`initDeclarationOnlyPasses`/`initCheckPasses1..8` helper (and its lambda is already its own method, because `pass` is non-inline). ~200 more fields put `<init>` back over HotSpot's `HugeMethodLimit`, where **nothing but `scripts/huge_methods.py` can see it**. Two invariants the split adds: those ten calls must stay IN SOURCE ORDER inside their `declarationOnly` guard (run 8 ends with two diagnostic RETRACTIONS that only work after run 1 emitted), and `HugeMethodLimitTest.methodCodeSizes` must keep the LARGEST `Code` length per method NAME — Kotlin emits a 72-byte synthetic `<init>` bridge for default arguments, so a last-wins map reports `<init>` as 72 and passes the limit pin vacuously.
- **PROGRAM ORDER IS THE CRAWL'S ORDER, AND `typeNode.bypassed` / `mapped.keyed` ARE A FUNCTION OF IT (round 776) — `ProjectCompiler.walk` SORTS, and removing the sort silently makes those two counters a property of the filesystem.** Which file first touches a shared type node decides whether INV.5(c) calls the resolution own-file or foreign: three orders of the same 78 files move them ~1% with the AST and every diagnostic bit-identical, so an order change looks exactly like a cost regression and shows up in NO output diff.
- **UNDER `--workers N` A LAZILY-INITIALIZED *SHARED* SINGLETON IS MINTED FROM WHICHEVER WORKER TOUCHES IT FIRST — i.e. AFTER that worker rebased — so its Symbol/Type id lands INSIDE the other workers' id space and the id-keyed relation caches silently confuse it with a worker-local type (round 825; this WAS the `--workers` race).** Nothing in the sequential prefix (crawl/parse/Binder) reads an intrinsic, so `TypeKt.<clinit>` ran in a worker and `anyType.id` measured **1,000,000,005**; the guard is now a DISJOINT id slice per worker plus a pre-touch, both in `runInDeepStackWorkers` (DeepStack.jvm.kt), pinned by `ParallelWorkerIdSpaceTest`. **Any process-wide mutable cache a `Checker` reaches must also be warmed before the workers spawn** (`RealLibSnapshots.prewarmParsedLibFiles`) — a plain `HashMap.getOrPut` from N worker threads is a data race with no exception to find it by.
- **A `--workers` WORKER RUNS ITS OWN ASSIGNED WORK ~50% SLOWER THAN THE SAME WORK RUNS SEQUENTIALLY, AND *THAT* — NOT DUPLICATION — IS THE LARGEST LOSS IN THE PARALLEL PATH (round 879).** The spine is essentially fully partition-scoped (w0 and w1 spend 10.5 and 7.7 s of non-bind work on 3,151 k and 2,275 k assigned chars = a 1.36 ratio against a 1.385 char ratio), yet at the sequential rate of 2.02 ms per k-char w0's share plus its bind should be ~8.2 s against **12.3 s measured**. It is what makes the fitted R rise with N in every ladder here. **Round 878's "D = 9.4-11.2 s, each worker redoes 40-45% of the compile" is RETRACTED** — that fit drew its points from DIFFERENT worker levels, where contention differs (the same single file costs 13,503 ms at w4 and 15,885 ms at w6), so the slope absorbed the contention and dumped it into the intercept; fitting WITHIN a level gives D ~ 3.7 s. **Do not spend workers past 4** (RSS +5% at w4, +75% at w6, and the critical worker gets SLOWER).
- **THE ALIASING IN `mergeSingleSymbol` IS *LOAD-BEARING*: BECAUSE `globals[name]` **IS** THE BINDER'S OBJECT, EVERY READER REACHING A SYMBOL VIA `BinderResult.locals` / `nodeToSymbol` SEES THE MERGED DECLARATIONS FOR FREE — COPY IT AND THEY GET THE UN-MERGED ORIGINAL (round 884, corpus: `extendGenericArray`, `extendGenericArray2`, `jsExportMemberMergedWithModuleAugmentation`).** So tsc's `cloneSymbol` alone is NOT portable here; what makes it work there is the FORWARDING TABLE beside it (`mergedSymbols[source.mergeId]` + a `getMergedSymbol` every reader passes through), and **tsgo's variant is the one to copy — `map[*ast.Symbol]*ast.Symbol` PER-CHECKER, keyed by identity, because tsc writes `mergeId` onto the shared symbol and that is the one thing a shared bind cannot allow.** Ours should be id-keyed (`IntKeyMap`; the clone carries the original's id deliberately, so a fresh id never shifts the sequence). Priced: an extra probe on all **748,522** `globals.lookups` is ~0.24-0.35% of a warm rebuild against the **-5%** that unconditional `--shareBind` is worth. `--mergeClone` has the clone half, OFF by default; the three failing tests are the cheapest map of the read sites that need forwarding. **Both reference implementations are ON THE BOX** — tsc at `build/bench/tsc-project-*/src/compiler/checker.ts`, tsgo at `typescript-go-repo/internal/checker/checker.go` (tag `typescript/v7.0.2`).
- **A MERGED `interface`'s SAME-NAMED MEMBER IS ONE `Symbol` CARRYING ONLY THE *LAST* BLOCK'S DECLARATION — `mergeSingleSymbol` ADOPTS — SO `Symbol.declarations` IS NOT A MEMBER'S WHOLE DECLARATION LIST, WHERE tsc's IS (round 928, measured: `both` declared in two `interface Merged` blocks answers `[decl2]`).** Overloads and an accessor pair are fine (one container), so the defect only shows across BLOCKS. Anything needing every declaration of a member must reconstruct it from the OWNER symbol's own `declarations`, each of which is a container to scan (`Checker.typeCaptureMemberDeclarations`); the failure is silent and in the dangerous direction — a rename that looks complete and rewrites one block.
- **TypeScript 7.0 SHIPS AS THE `typescript` PACKAGE ITSELF (`latest = 7.0.2`), NOT AS `@typescript/native-preview` — THAT CHANNEL IS RETIRED AND STOPPED AT `7.0.0-dev.20260707.2`; THE BIN IS NAMED `tsc`, NOT `tsgo` (round 884).** The two bench references therefore share a package NAME (`typescript@6` vs `typescript@7`) and **cannot coexist in one `node_modules`** — `bench.yml` installs them into separate npm roots and resolves whichever bin the spec provided. Switching the column is series-safe: 7.0.2 measures **1,774 ms** against the dev build's 1,788 on the compiler profile with the same 65 diagnostics. Local copy: `tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc` (gitignored).
- **ON AN ALL-MODULE PROGRAM THE CHECKER MUTATES *ZERO* BINDER-OWNED `Symbol`s — `--bindMutationCheck` reads `checked 15580, changed 0` on the compiler profile, IN THE SAME RUN as `mergeSingleSymbol: adopts 406, mutates 175` (round 882).** Both hold because those merges land on **LIB** symbols (`bindRealLibs` / `init:mergeLibGlobals`), which are in no `BinderResult` — so `PartitionCheck`'s "a worker must never reuse an already-checked bind" is true of the LIB tables, not of the program's, and **a bind is shareable across workers for an all-module program as-is**. **THE SCOPE IS THE CAVEAT**: the zero holds only because INV.3(d) keeps a module's locals out of `globals`; a program with global SCRIPT files does mutate binder output (the pin's positive control drives exactly that and asserts the arm sees it — round 849, a zero from a blind instrument reads like a real negative). The condition is cheap to test at runtime, so **gate a shared-bind path on the program's SHAPE rather than blocking it on the immutability refactor**. Instrument note: fingerprint identity-keyed on `Symbol`, which is sound because `Symbol` is a plain class and NOT a `data class` (round 471's deep-`hashCode` hazard is about AST nodes).
- **THE BLOCKER TO SHARING ONE BIND ACROSS `--workers` CHECKERS IS **406 OBJECTS**, NOT A SYMBOL GRAPH — `--mergeCensus` reads `adopts 406, mutates 175 (164 reaching an adopted symbol)` against the ~105 k symbols a worker mints (round 881).** `Checker.mergeSingleSymbol`'s `else` branch **adopts** — `target[name] = symbol` puts the BINDER's own `Symbol` into `globals`, which is why a bind is CONSUMED by a checker rather than read by one — and its `if` branch mutates in place (`declarations.addAll` is NOT idempotent, so a second checker over the same tables appends duplicates). The population is tiny because **INV.3(d) already retired the merge for module-only names**. Do not read "small" as "easy": those 406 are exactly the objects whose identity `globals` hands out, so copying them moves id allocation (id drift reshuffles ~350 boundary tests) and collides with INV.3(c)'s merged-instance invariant and `canonicalEnumSymbol`'s frozen verdicts. And the census watches ONE site — a shared bind also needs "the checker mutates a binder `Symbol` nowhere else" answered. Staging, price and obligations: `docs/parallel-bind-sharing.md`.
- **THE PARALLEL PATH IS `wall(N) = 1,447 ms + 7,717/N` WARM, AND THE 1,447 IS A PER-WORKER FIXED COST **SHARING CANNOT REDUCE** — SO `docs/parallel-caching.md` PHASE 1 ("resolve the lib slice once, freeze it, share it read-only") BUYS **ZERO WALL**, however redundant the N copies look (round 880).** The fit is `worker = fixed + r x assigned chars` over the WARM per-worker rows (w0 3,892 ms for 1 file / 3,151 k chars, w1-w3 3,157-3,310 for 25-26 files / 2,275 k): `r` = 0.776 ms/k-char against a sequential 0.565, i.e. **+37% contention**, and `fixed` = 1,447 ms = the re-bind (515) plus ~930 ms of program-wide checker work. N=4 predicts 3,376 against 3,035-3,232 measured; N -> infinity gives a **4.35x ceiling** contention keeps out of reach. **GC and the JIT are both measured and both acquitted**: GC pauses are 734 ms at w1 / 1,338 at w4 and **heap-INSENSITIVE** (`-Xmx4g` 14,896 vs `-Xmx8g` 14,865 ms), and the effect persists WARM, where the JIT ramp is over. Only two things move the floor — making the work partition-scoped, or doing less of it, and the latter is the (WARM.*) single-thread arc, not a concurrency lever.
- **…AND THAT LAW HAS A PRECONDITION IT DOES NOT STATE: "CONCURRENT DUPLICATION IS FREE" ASSUMES *CORES ARE ABUNDANT*, WHICH ON THIS BOX THEY ARE NOT — `--shareBind` (bind ONCE, hand every worker the same `BinderResult`s) measures **-5% WARM at w4**, replicated in two ABBA batches with the rotation reversed, all four `on` runs faster than all four `off` runs (round 883).** Four binder threads run beside four JIT compiler threads on 8 cores, so four simultaneous whole-program binds cost MORE wall than the 515 ms one bind costs, and collapsing them returns the difference — i.e. this is the +37% per-worker contention term being collected, not a bind saving. **The prediction was written into the KDoc BEFORE measuring and said zero; state the prior first, because that is what makes a refutation legible.** Sharing is OPT-IN because soundness is a property of the PROGRAM, not the compiler: safe while nothing merges a program symbol into `globals` (INV.3(d) gives that for an all-module program and not otherwise). Id safety is structural — the shared bind runs on the CALLER thread, so its symbols sit below every worker's rebased slice (>= 1e9). **A shape gate that would make it default must REUSE the checker's own merge predicate rather than re-derive it**; a re-derivation wrong in the permissive direction corrupts silently.
- **HOISTING CONCURRENT DUPLICATED WORK INTO THE SERIAL PREFIX CANNOT WIN, AND USUALLY LOSES — `wall = D + A/N` WHETHER D RUNS IN ONE WORKER OR IN ALL N, SO MOVING IT TO THE PREFIX LEAVES `D + A/N` AT BEST (round 879, which retracted its own queue item on this arithmetic before building it).** Measured: the ~445 tail passes are **4,342 ms** of a sequential compile but only ~1.3-2.7 s of w4 wall, because they are largely partition-scoped ALREADY (~1.1 s duplicated per worker + ~98 ms per ASSIGNED FILE — with the tail off, w0 with 1 file drops 1,194 ms while w1 with 26 drops 3,651), so hoisting them would have added 4.3 s of serial time to save ~1.9 s per worker: **a net ~2.4 s LOSS**. **A count of `binderResults` loops (321 of them) is a fact about the source and says nothing about what removing them returns** — price the prize first (round 758). The PassLab (`build/pass-lab.txt`, `disable <pass>`) answers this class of question with NO recompile.
- **AND VERIFY A `--workers` CLAIM IN *EMIT* MODE, NOT ONLY `--noEmit` — every `--workers` capture taken in this project before round 878 was check-only, which round 738's `skipEmitOutputs` gate makes structurally blind to transform/emit.** The check is one `--outDir` build per arm plus `diff -r` (w1 vs w4: 78 files each, clean).
- **THE WALL OF A PARALLEL PHASE IS THE SLOWEST WORKER, SO A PARTITION'S max/mean IS A *CEILING*, NOT A TAX — AND `checker.ts` IS **31.6% OF THE COMPILER PROFILE**, WHICH IS WHY `i % workers == w` CAPPED `--workers` AT 2.10x / 2.63x / 2.41x AT 4 / 6 / 8 BEFORE ANY CHECKER WORK (round 877).** That is also the whole explanation of "w8 is worse than w4", recorded three times (round 826 cold: 1.242x vs 1.361x) and never explained: at w8 one bucket drew the giant AND its ordinary share. `balancedFilePartition` is longest-processing-time-first and takes the ceiling to **3.16x at every level >= 4** — which is then the REAL ceiling of FILE-level parallelism on this program, since one file being 31.6% of the input means no assignment beats 1/0.316; raising it further needs sub-file parallelism, not a better scheduler. **Its weight is source LENGTH, an explicit proxy for checking cost** (exact, free, and known before any checking — a true cost measure is not); a wrong proxy costs balance, never correctness. **Its order must stay TOTAL (size desc, ties on `fileName`)** or the partition stops being a function of the program and diagnostics start depending on scheduling.
- **EVERY `--workers` NUMBER IN THIS REPO IS *COLD* AND EVERY WARM NUMBER IS *SEQUENTIAL* — THE TWO REGIMES FIRST MET IN ROUND 876, AND THEY DISAGREE: w4 IS **1.531x** WARM AGAINST ROUND 826's **1.361x** COLD.** Measure a parallel change with `BenchMain <proj> <warmup> <iters> <tiers> <emit> workers<N>` (or `WORKERS=N scripts/ab-warm.sh`), never by inferring a warm answer from a cold ladder: cold, worker threads compete for the very cores the JIT ramp is running on, and a cold A/B can show the removal of real work as a wash. **Set the level ONCE per process** — round 867's shared-branch-profile law is sharper for worker levels than for anything it was written about (two levels in one JVM share every compiled method in the binder and the checker), so comparing LEVELS is two invocations of the harness, never two arms of one run.
- **A REDUNDANT COMPUTATION WHOSE RESULT IS *DROPPED* IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY GATE HERE — NO DIAGNOSTIC, NO EMITTED BYTE, NO `cost_gate.py` COUNTER MOVES, ONLY THE WALL (round 876: `cpcBindAndCheck` bound the whole program on the SEQUENTIAL prefix of every `--workers` compile and read the result nowhere — 515 ms warm, ~11% of a parallel rebuild).** Two consequences. **The pin for such a thing must be a COUNT, not a time** (`FrontEnd.sequentialFileBinds`, written only from the caller thread, asserted exactly 0 under workers) — 515 ms sits under the run-to-run spread of a parallel compile, and round 868 already says a timed assertion is a coin flip. **And `+0.00%` from `cost_gate.py` is the EXPECTED answer, not a green light**: the gate exercises the sequential path, where a parallel-only change is a strict no-op — read it as a control that the OTHER path is untouched, and get the actual verdict from `--frontEnd`'s phase rows.
- **A `--workers N` CLAIM NEEDS A DIAGNOSTIC-COUNT DISTRIBUTION OVER >=5 RUNS PER LEVEL — one `--listAll` capture is a coin flip and is how rounds 754 and 824 each shipped a false green.** The detector is `grep -c 'error TS'` over repeated fixed-worker-level runs (~25 s each); `--partitionCheck` is a STATIC partition model and cannot see a race by construction.


- **THE JDK 25 AOT CACHE HAS *NO* INVALIDATION — A STALE ONE SILENTLY RUNS THE PREVIOUS BUILD'S BYTECODE (exit 0, `OK — 0 errors`, where the uncached run gives `NoClassDefFoundError`), SO `scripts/xtsc`'s FAIL-SAFE CHECK IS THE ONLY THING BETWEEN A REBUILT JAR AND A WRONG ANSWER (rounds 828/832).** The cache is passed to the JVM only when a recomputed manifest — sha-256 of every classpath entry IN ORDER, the JDK build, OS/arch, and the cache file's own digest — matches byte for byte; **mtime is deliberately absent from the fingerprint** (round 828's decisive experiment used a FRESH mtime, and a rewrite that preserves one is what a stat check waves through). Do not "simplify" that to a size/mtime compare, do not add an opt-out flag, and do not use `-XX:AOTMode=on` (auto mode is what degrades a bad cache to a normal run). `AotCacheGuardTest` fails on exactly that mistake; contract in `docs/perf/aot-cache.md`.
- **`build/libs/*.jar` IS NOT BYTE-REPRODUCIBLE, SO *ANY* BUILD — INCLUDING A NO-OP ONE AND INCLUDING `./gradlew jvmTest` — INVALIDATES THE AOT CACHE, AND A CACHED ARM MEASURED AFTERWARDS IS SILENTLY UNCACHED (round 842).** `jvmJar --rerun` with zero source change yields a different sha256 AND size (5,639,210 -> 5,639,200 B), and every classpath entry is content-hashed into the fingerprint, so the guard correctly reads `SKIP no-cache-file` — fail-safe, never a wrong answer, but a "cached" arm then runs at plain speed and its ladder still looks plausible (round 842's first `--serve` A/B was an A/A this way). **Train AFTER the last build, and NEVER measure a cached arm without `XTSC_AOT_VERBOSE=1` printing `aot USE …`** — that line is the only thing separating the two.
- **…AND A SECOND BATCH REPLICATES THE *MEASUREMENT* DRAW, NEVER THE *TRAINING* ONE — AN A/B ON AN AOT TRAINING CONFIGURATION NEEDS >=2 INDEPENDENTLY TRAINED CACHES PER ARM (round 842).** Two caches trained by an IDENTICAL `xtsc-aot train` differ by up to **+322 ms (2.4%), 10/12 paired**, and the ordering REPLICATES across batches hours apart, so it is a persistent property of the cache FILE that re-running can never average out; rounds 840(c)/(d) each cited two batches as evidence while re-using the same two cache files. No observable predicts it — five identical-command draws span 212,992 B and the LARGEST cache is second-slowest, which is why 840(d) § 10.3's "+82 KB" reading is struck. Harness: `scripts/aot-draw-variance.sh`.
- **TWO ARMS THAT SHARE ONE COMPILED METHOD ARE NOT INDEPENDENT ARMS — THE ONE THAT RUNS FIRST WRITES THE BRANCH PROFILE THE OTHER IS COMPILED AGAINST, AND A BRANCH PROFILED AS *NEVER TAKEN* COSTS AN UNCOMMON TRAP WHEN THE OTHER ARM TAKES IT (round 867: per-arm sd 16-38%, one process's control arm the batch's cheapest and its treated arm the dearest, by 2x).** It bites any A/B whose two arms are the same code driven by different DATA (a mask, a mode field, a flag), which is exactly how `BenchMain`'s tiers are built. **Give each arm its own JVM** — sd fell to 2-5% with nothing else changed — and remember the first instrumented rebuild in a process is also the one that WARMS a method the uninstrumented loop never touches, so discard a leading throwaway. `scripts/round867-warm-amp2.sh`.
- **`PostCheckerPartitionTest`'s `residue * 4 < post` is TIMING-FLAKY UNDER LOAD and is the one pin in this suite that reddens without a regression** — it bounds a residue over a **272 us** region by four timestamp pairs, and a single scheduler hiccup blows it. Re-run the class alone before believing it (round 867: red in the full suite, green in isolation, from a change it cannot reach).
- **A SIGN-CONSISTENT PAIRED BATCH IS NOT A RESULT — REPLICATE IT IN A SECOND BATCH, BECAUSE A ~5% PER-ARM SPREAD MANUFACTURES 5/5 SWEEPS (round 840(c)).** Batch 1 read an AOT arm as 5/5 wins, median −1,161 ms (−6.6%) on the check-only path, in a rotated interleave with the winner in the *disadvantaged* position 4 times; batch 2 read 3/6 wins and +192 ms, and a third batch 2/4 — the effect was zero. The genuine effect in the same runs (emit, −1,132 ms) replicated in all three batches. Rotation and within-rep pairing control the drift; only replication controls the *draw*, and a second batch of the two arms costs one more run block.
- **A KOTLIN-DAEMON DEATH ANNOUNCES ITSELF AS `Using fallback strategy: Compile without Kotlin daemon` AND THEN BURNS MINUTES FAILING (round 812: 3m56s, inside a 683 MiB Gradle daemon).** The upstream line is `Connection to the Kotlin daemon has been unexpectedly lost` (round 811's `GC overhead limit exceeded` in a second costume). Kill the run when you see the fallback line; recovery is `./gradlew --stop` plus a graceful bracket-pattern `pkill -f 'KotlinCompile[D]aemon'` (never `-9`), then rebuild.
- **`open(path, 'w').write(<expr>)` TRUNCATES THE FILE BEFORE `<expr>` IS EVALUATED, SO A RAISING EXPRESSION LEAVES IT AT ZERO BYTES (round 879 emptied `PLAN-PHASE-5.md` this way; recovered by `git checkout --` because it was committed).** Python evaluates the receiver first, and the scripted doc edits in this repo are exactly the shape that bites — a long f-string with an `s.index(...)` inside it. Build the new content into a VARIABLE first, then open and write it; or use the Edit tool, which cannot half-apply. And it is a second argument for the standing rule that a round's docs are committed before the next scripted rewrite touches them.
- **THE SCRATCHPAD DIRECTORY IS SHARED BETWEEN SESSIONS, SO A WAIT LOOP KEYED ON A FILE'S *EXISTENCE* (`until [[ -f out.json ]]`) CAN MATCH A PREVIOUS SESSION'S FILE AND RETURN INSTANTLY WHILE THE REAL JOB IS STILL RUNNING (round 946, ~40 min).** The stale `pristine_sweep.py` output found that way was three rounds old and read as a plausible before-arm (334/776 instead of 297/773), i.e. it failed in the reassuring direction — a stale measurement looks exactly like a fresh one, and single-fixture spot checks on the SAME binary disagreeing with the full run is the only tell. **Delete the output file before launching, or wait on the PROCESS (`tail --pid=<pid> -f /dev/null`), never on a marker that could already exist.**
- **NEVER write `nohup ./gradlew … &` INSIDE a `run_in_background` Bash call — the double-detach makes the tool report success immediately while the run lives on, and the next command's `rm -rf build/test-results/jvmTest` then deletes the results dir out from under it (round 831, ~40 min).** The victim run dies with `NoSuchFileException: …/binary/in-progress-results-generic.bin` **after its tests have already completed**, so an empty results dir plus a `BUILD FAILED` looks exactly like a build that never ran — while the log in fact carries the full `N tests completed, M failed`. **Read the log before believing an empty results dir**, and pass the command to `run_in_background` bare.
- **…AND A `| tail`/`| grep` ON THE GRADLE RUN DESTROYS THAT LOG IN EXACTLY THE KILLED-RUN CASE THE RULE ABOVE EXISTS FOR (round 840b) — the filter buffers until the pipeline EXITS, so a run that dies mid-flight leaves a ZERO-BYTE log: no `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, no `tests completed`, no `fallback strategy` line, nothing to tell a killed run from one that never started.** Note this file's own "How to run tests" incantation is `… | grep -a "tests completed"` — that form is for a FOREGROUND run you watch; for a backgrounded one redirect the FULL output and grep the FILE afterwards.
- **Do NOT poll for a Gradle run with a `while kill -0 $(pgrep -f "GradleWrapperMain.*jvmTest") ...; do sleep N; done` wait-loop — it never exits.** `pgrep -f` matches against the FULL command line, and the wait-loop's OWN command line literally contains the string `GradleWrapperMain.*jvmTest` (inside the pgrep pattern), so it matches ITSELF → `kill -0 <self-pid>` always succeeds → the loop sleeps forever, orphaned (harmless/idle, but never terminates and clutters the process list; clean up with `kill <pids>`). There is no need to poll at all: launch the suite with the Bash tool's `run_in_background: true` and react to the `<task-notification>` that fires when it completes (the harness re-invokes you). If you must poll for something, pick a pattern that cannot match the poller itself (e.g. a marker file's existence). Two long-lived JVMs (the Gradle daemon + `KotlinCompileDaemon`, ~hours uptime) are NORMAL idle build daemons — leave them; they speed up builds and auto-shut-down after their idle timeout. **The pkill variant is WORSE (round 466): `pkill -9 -f KotlinCompileDaemon` KILLS THE INVOKING SHELL ITSELF (the -f pattern matches the bash -c command line containing the literal), so everything after it in a compound command silently never runs — the tell is a Bash call that "completes" with no output and no side effects. Use a bracket pattern that cannot match itself: `pkill -9 -f 'KotlinCompile[D]aemon'`.**
- **A `when` ARM THAT IS `options.copy(field = …)` ON A WIDE DATA CLASS COSTS ~160 BYTECODES AT ITS CALL SITE, so a dispatch table's size is the ARM COUNT times the DATA CLASS's FIELD COUNT (round 815, `applyDirective`: 85 one-line arms = 13,694 bytecodes, over HotSpot's 8,000 `HugeMethodLimit`).** Kotlin compiles a named-argument `copy` into a `copy$default` call carrying the whole argument vector plus the default bitmasks, so source that reads as one line is not one line of bytecode — **adding directives to `applyDirective` walks it back over the limit at ~160 each** (per-run headroom is in `docs/perf/setup-phase-and-huge-methods.md` § 19). Check with `scripts/huge_methods.py`, never by eye.
- **POST-MODULE-SPLIT, A BARE `./gradlew jvmTest --tests '*Foo*'` FAILS THE BUILD EVEN WHEN THE TEST PASSES — every module runs `jvmTest`, and the four that do not contain `Foo` each die with `No tests found for given includes` (round 886).** The core module compiles and its tests run GREEN in the same invocation, so the `BUILD FAILED` is pure module-routing noise and reads exactly like a real failure. Qualify the task: `./gradlew :xemantic-typescript-compiler-core:jvmTest --tests '*Foo*'`. (The unfiltered whole-suite form is unaffected — that one must stay unqualified to cover all four modules, and its XMLs must be summed with the `*/build/test-results/jvmTest/*.xml` glob.)
- `project.exec {}` is not available in Gradle 9 Kotlin DSL task `doLast` blocks — use `ProcessBuilder` directly instead.
- **A POSIX `fork`/`exec` SPAWN MUST `dup2` /dev/null ONTO fds 0/1/2 BEFORE `execvp`, OR THE DAEMON HOLDS ITS CLIENT'S STDOUT OPEN AND `xtsc … | tail` NEVER RETURNS (round MOD.5, measured).** The client exits, the compile finishes, and the shell waits forever on a pipe whose write end the detached daemon still owns — `--serve` works, the output is correct, and only running the client THROUGH A PIPE shows it (a unit test on `spawnDetached` passes). The JVM twin gets this free from `Redirect.DISCARD`; the native one is hand-written in `Platform.native.kt` and needs the double fork (`setsid` between them, `waitpid` the intermediate, `_exit` not `exit`) as well.
- **A CLIENT'S FIXED COST IS THE JVM, NOT ITS CLASSPATH — the `-api`-only thin client is 278 ms against the fat dispatcher's 287 ms, and the Kotlin/Native one is 7.0 ms against a 0.9 ms fork+exec floor (round 872).** Class loading is lazy, so the 5.6 MB compiler jar a daemon-served request never touches costs nothing; the module split's value is that it makes a NATIVE binary affordable, never that it shrinks a JVM start. `scripts/xtsc` routes `--daemon` to the native client and falls back to the JVM **on exit code 3 only** — that code means "the request never ran", so falling back on any other non-zero code compiles and reports twice.
- **…AND SWAPPING A CLIENT ARM CHANGES SEMANTICS UNLESS YOU STOP IT: the thin client STARTS a daemon when it finds none, so the launcher must pass `--no-spawn` or `--daemon` quietly replaces an in-process compile with a long-lived JVM nobody asked for — measured, it spawned one from inside the 14,000-test suite and left it running (round 872).** `AotCacheGuardTest`'s dispatcher case is what caught it; a client arm added later needs the same guard.
- **A PIN ON THE VALUE ONE LAYER PRODUCES CANNOT SEE WHAT THE NEXT LAYER DOES WITH IT (round 872).** `ExitCodeParityTest` pinned the exit code the daemon puts IN its response — always correct — while `runAsClient` propagated only `XTSC_REFUSED` and dropped the rest, so a daemon-served FAILING compile exited **0** where the CLI and the in-process fallback exit 1, and CI branching on it read a failure as a pass. Silent by construction: the diagnostics print either way and nothing prints the code. When a value crosses a boundary, pin it where it is CONSUMED.
- **A CONNECT-AND-CLOSE IS THE CLIENT'S REACHABILITY PROBE, NOT A FAILED REQUEST — the daemon must recognise a frameless connection and stay silent (round MOD.5).** `isDaemonReachable` is polled every 25-500 ms while a client waits for a daemon it just spawned, and logging each one fills the daemon's log with alarming lines describing its own health check.
- **A DAEMON REQUEST CARRIES THE CLIENT'S *WORKING DIRECTORY* AND THE COMPILER RESOLVES AGAINST IT — DO NOT RE-ADD PATH REWRITING TO EITHER CLIENT (round 873, protocol version 2).** A JVM cannot change its own cwd, so `CompileServer` installs `CompileRequest.workingDirectory` into `SystemVfs.workingDirectory` for the duration of one request (install-and-restore on the one compile thread, exactly like the round-848 ledger); that single variable is the funnel, because `ProjectCompiler.build` absolutizes both the project path and a `--outDir` override through `Vfs.resolveAbsolute` before doing anything with either. **The rule both clients used to carry — "rewrite an argument that names something existing here" — is UNFIXABLE at that layer and its two holes were CI-grade**: a client does not parse the compiler's options, so it cannot tell a project path from the `4` in `--workers 4`, and it never sees the path a user did not type — `CliArgs.project` defaults to `"."`, so `xtsc --daemon --noEmit` in a failing project compiled the DAEMON's directory and exited **0**, while `--outDir out` (not yet existing, so "not a path") emitted the user's JavaScript into it. The version bump is load-bearing: a v1 daemon answers a v2 request against its own cwd, and `protocolProblem` is what refuses it — **but only since 2026-08-09; before that its answer was printed and discarded, see the next entry.** Sweep with `scripts/serve_parity.py`; contract in `docs/serve-parity.md`.
- **CONSULTING A GUARD IS NOT ENFORCING IT — `CompileServer` called `protocolProblem` on every request, PRINTED the answer, and then compiled anyway (2026-08-09).** So a v1 request was in fact served against the DAEMON's cwd (the round-873 hazard the version bump exists to stop), and since a mismatched client's documented answer is to fall back and compile in-process, every `xtsc --daemon` through a stale native client cost **TWO full compiles** — 7 mismatches to 14 served requests, 14.6 s against an honest 7.5 s. `respondTo` now answers `XTSC_REFUSED` with `elapsedMs = 0`. **Why it survived: every server pin built its `CompileRequest` POSITIONALLY, i.e. at the UNVERSIONED default, so the suite pinned the opposite behaviour** — daemon tests now go through `clientRequest(...)`, and a mismatch message printed by BOTH peers must be peer-neutral (it said "the daemon speaks protocol N, this client speaks M", which inverts every noun in a daemon log).
- **GraalVM PGO IS THE BIGGEST SINGLE LEVER MEASURED IN THIS ARC — -21.2% CHECK-ONLY AND -19.1% EMIT, 5/5 PAIRED IN BOTH MODES, 46 DIAGNOSTICS AND ALL 78 EMITTED `.js` BYTE-IDENTICAL (2026-08-10, `docs/perf/aot-native-image.md` § 10).** It needs **Oracle GraalVM** (`-graal` in SDKMAN); Community Edition (`-graalce`) has no PGO and its `native-image --help` does not mention the word. On this box, one session: CE 25.2.4 11,481 ms -> Oracle 25.0.4 10,222 -> **Oracle+PGO 8,057**, i.e. **~1.93x FASTER than tsc 6.0.3** where CI's CE arm is at 1.02x parity. **Train the `.iprof` on BOTH modes** - a check-only-only profile leaves the Transformer/Emitter on static heuristics, which is round 840(c)'s -932 ms trap one artifact over. The CE-vs-Oracle row CONFOUNDS distribution with Graal version (25.2 innovation vs 25.0 LTS, same JDK base 25.0.4, and Oracle ships no innovation build) - do not read it as a statement about either alone.
- **`javaTarget`'s CEILING IS GRAALVM, NOT KOTLIN — GraalVM ships NO JDK 26 build in EITHER distribution (Oracle's `graalvm-jdk-26` 404s; CE's newest is `graal-25.2.4` / JDK 25.0.4), and `native-image` cannot read a class file its own JVM rejects (2026-08-10).** Kotlin 2.4.10 reaches `JVM_26`, so the bump compiles and then silently costs the `nativeImage` task and both CI native workflows — i.e. the fastest artifact shipped. 25 is the pin; raise it only together with a GraalVM that has 26.
- **AZUL ZULU IS *NOT* FASTER THAN TEMURIN, AND THE DEV BOX'S DEFAULT `java` IS ZULU — SO A *COLD* NUMBER TAKEN LOCALLY RUNS ~13% PESSIMISTIC AGAINST CI (2026-08-10, `docs/perf/jdk-jit-vendor-comparison.md` § 1).** Same upstream build both sides (26.0.2+10, so vendor is the only variable): Temurin wins 3 of 4 cells, cold check-only by **+13.1%** (0/2); the one Zulu-favouring cell is mixed 2/3 with a range crossing zero. Warm numbers are close enough to compare across the two; cold ones are not.
- **THE GRAAL JIT IS A ~5-10% *WARM* LEVER AND AN 8-16% *COLD* PENALTY, WHICH IS EXACTLY WHAT `bench.yml`'s `JAVA_HOME`-ORDER COMMENT IS GUARDING (2026-08-10, § 2 of the same doc).** Against Temurin: Oracle GraalVM's JVM is **-5.4% check-only / -10.2% emit warm, 3/3 in every cell**, and **+8.2% / +4.6% cold**. Confounded with JDK 25-vs-26 (GraalVM has nothing on 26) in the direction that UNDERSTATES Graal. Free ~5-10% for the DAEMON; keep the bench on Temurin as the conservative reference.
- **THE BENCH'S PRIMARY xtsc COLUMN IS A *WARM* REBUILD SINCE 2026-08-10 AND THE SERIES RESTARTED AT `BENCH-ROWS-V2` — never compare a V2 row's xtsc cell to a V1 one (cold vs warm is ~4x on the same commit, so the boundary alone looks like a 4x win).** **Since the same date it publishes TWO warm arms — `temurin26` (primary) and `graal-oracle25` — on identical bytecode in one run, so the series never has to pick a JVM**; the second arm's delta IS the within-round Graal-vs-HotSpot number, and a GraalVM-only primary would have frozen the series on JDK 25. The AOT column re-baselined on the same date, CE -> Oracle BASE — **not PGO, which lives in `native.yml` (the shipped artifact) because bench.yml runs per push**, so that column reads ~14%/~20% slow against what ships. What this immediately showed: **warm xtsc is 2.05x FASTER than tsc check-only and 2.20x on emit** — tsc parity is behind us in the warm regime and the open gap is tsgo (3.09x / 2.71x); the cold arm's "2.00x SLOWER than tsc" is a JVM-startup story, not a compiler one.
- **A CRaC CHECKPOINT OF A WARMED JVM RESTORES IN ~30 ms AND ITS FIRST COMPILE RUNS AT FULL WARM SPEED — 6.8-7.3 s AGAINST 24-25 s COLD, SAME BINARY, SAME SESSION (3.4x, against the AOT cache's 1.64x), OUTPUT BYTE-IDENTICAL BAR THE `time:` LINE (2026-08-10, `docs/perf/crac-checkpoint.md`).** Three features get called "AOT/snapshot" and only one stores COMPILED CODE: the shipped JDK 25 AOT cache is JEP 483+515 (classes + method PROFILES, which is why it is worth nothing warm), Leyden's AOT code caching is NOT in this JDK (`-XX:+AOTCodeCaching` unrecognized), and CRaC needs a CRaC build (Zulu 25 CRaC; the default `warp` engine checkpointed with **no root and CapEff 0**, where the CRIU engine would have needed capabilities). **It is not shippable: the restored process keeps the CHECKPOINT's working directory** (measured - checkpoint in the repo, restore from `/tmp`, `user.dir` unchanged), i.e. round 873's defect one layer down, so a CRaC CLI must re-install the real cwd through `SystemVfs.workingDirectory` in an `afterRestore` hook. The 340 MB image was page-cache-hot in every restore here and a cold-disk one is UNMEASURED (dropping caches needs root). Suite on that JDK: 14,234 / 0.
- **A WARM A/B's DEFAULT WARM-UP WAS *2* AND THAT ALONE SPENT MORE THAN THE WHOLE +-1.0% BAND — MEASURED A/A, TWO IDENTICAL ARMS SIT **3.3%** APART AT `WARMUP=2`, 2.0% AT 3, **0.8%** AT 6 AND 0.9% AT 8 (2026-08-10; defaults now 6 in `ab-warm.sh` AND `BenchMain`).** The inflated median is NOT the problem - the ramp is common-mode and cancels in a paired delta - but it lands on the one quantity the verdict gates on, the spread BETWEEN process medians. Method worth reusing: run ONE ladder of 16 measured iterations per process (`BenchMain <proj> 0 16`) and re-slice it offline for every candidate setting, which makes the arms identical by construction and costs two runs instead of ten. **And no setting stabilises an ABSOLUTE number: the ladder still drifts -3.4% between iterations 9-12 and 13-16, sign-consistent in both runs**, so warm-up is not over at 16 and only within-round paired deltas are quotable.
- **THE WARM-UP LADDER IS HotSpot AND ESSENTIALLY NOTHING ELSE — 80,056 ms of JIT CPU during request 1, 28,995 during 2, 9,927 during 3, against a plateau of ~2-3 s (2026-08-09, `jcmd PerfCounter.print` between requests).** The ladder 25.1 / 11.2 / 7.6 / 7.3 / 7.2 / 7.0 s is bought by C1+C2 compiling **5,069 code blobs in the first request alone**; GC is flat at ~120 ms/request and the one cross-request cache that is NOT the JIT — `CrawlParseCache` — is worth **270 ms** at plateau (`--parseCacheOff`, blocked arms). Two consequences: **the plateau is reached at request 6-8, not 4** (request 4 is still ~5% high), and JIT never actually stops (127 fresh blobs at request 10), it just moves off the critical path onto the other cores.
- **A PRISTINE FIXTURE HAS TWO HALVES THAT LIVE IN DIFFERENT FILES, AND TAKING BOTH FROM ONE OF THEM IS A SILENT WHOLE-FILE LINE SHIFT: the `.js`/`.errors.txt` BASELINE echoes the source verbatim but with the `// @key: value` HARNESS DIRECTIVES REMOVED (tsc strips them before compiling), while `tests/cases` keeps them (round 941).** So an offline reconstruction must take the SOURCE from a baseline and the DIRECTIVES from the case — a source taken from the case is the baseline's line numbering plus the directive count, and every `(line, code)` row then reads as a divergence in BOTH directions, which is indistinguishable from a real finding (30.5% of round 940's sweep). `tests/cases` is also incomplete in this clone, so neither half is always present; a missing case leaves the baseline's `(target=es2015)` VARIATION SUFFIX as the only surviving record of the options. **The guard is an ALIGNMENT ORACLE** — compare the reconstruction line-for-line against the baseline's own `==== file ====` body — and `scripts/pristine_sweep.py` records `aligned`/`misaligned`/`unknown` per fixture for exactly this reason. Directives are then passed into the scratch tsconfig VERBATIM, because `TsConfigLoader` routes every `compilerOptions` key through the same `applyDirective` the corpus harness uses; re-deriving that mapping in Python is what loses `@jsx` and `@experimentalDecorators`.
- **AN ABSENT DIRECTIVE IS EVIDENCE ONLY WHERE THE *CASE FILE* IS PRESENT — A MISSING ONE SAYS NOTHING, AND `pristine_sweep.py --tsc-strict-default` DELETED FOUR GENUINE FALSE POSITIVES BEFORE IT WAS GUARDED ON THAT (round 943).** That arm injects tsc's own `strict: false` default wherever a fixture names no strict-family directive, to size how much of a sweep is the (CHK.13) convention rather than a modelling gap; `strictPropertyInitialization` has NO case file in this clone and **20 TS2564 in its own baseline**, i.e. pristine plainly had the flag ON. Round 941's defect (c) one directive over. **Second control, needed whenever the case file is absent: does pristine's own baseline carry that CODE anywhere in the fixture** — zero over seventeen uninitialised class fields is conclusive, seven is a refusal. `docs/pristine-divergences.md` § 0b.
- **A PROJECT PATH THAT DOES NOT EXIST USED TO CRAWL THE WHOLE FILESYSTEM, AND UNDER `--serve` THAT WEDGES THE DAEMON PERMANENTLY (round 873).** `resolveConfigPath` takes `dirname` of a config nobody confirmed — for `xtsc /nonexistent` that is **`/`** — and an unreadable config used to fall back to the default everything-glob: >30 minutes of CPU, after emitting TS5083 and going walking anyway. **The general trap is the server one, and it is unchanged: `CompileServer` serves requests SEQUENTIALLY on one thread by design and neither peer has a timeout, so ANY request that does not return holds the daemon for good and every later client blocks with no diagnosis.** A config that does not EXIST now includes nothing; one that exists and does not PARSE keeps the default (its dir is real). When a parity/latency harness reports "the daemon stopped serving", look for a wedged compile before suspecting the transport.
- **A PARITY/ABLATION DRIVER NEEDS A *REACHED-THE-THING-UNDER-TEST* CONTROL, AND FOR THE DAEMON THAT IS THE SERVER'S OWN `request N served` COUNT (round 873).** `--daemon` falls back to compiling IN-PROCESS when no server answers, so a dead daemon makes both arms run the same code and agree — a perfect green sweep that tested nothing, the same class as round 853's frozen classpath. The driver requires the count to advance by exactly one per step AND the `no compile server on` message to be absent. Related and cheap: **a "this cell is allowed to differ" marker is an escape hatch that WILL absorb an unrelated defect** — round 873's ablation caught one swallowing an exit-code divergence into a documented cwd excuse.
- **A CLI FLAG THAT WRITES A PROCESS-GLOBAL MODE MUST GO THROUGH `modes.set(Obj::field, v)` IN `parseCliArgs` — A BARE `Obj.field = v` THERE COMPILES, WORKS IN THE ONE-SHOT CLI, AND SILENTLY RECONFIGURES EVERY LATER REQUEST ON A `--serve` DAEMON (round 848).** `runCli` restores the ledger in a `finally`, to the value the field HELD (`ArgNarrowGate.mode` defaults to ON, not to its zero value); `CliModeRestoreTest` reflects over every declared field of the 16 mode objects and is what sees the omission. **And do NOT read a green corpus or a green profile as "that arm is equivalent": all 14 probe arms are byte-identical on the compiler profile INCLUDING `--flowScanBogus`, whose job is to corrupt the scanner, and defaulting `FlowScan.bogus` true leaves the 13,902-test suite with exactly ONE failure — its own dedicated equivalence pin.**
- **THE COMPILE SERVER MUST RUN EVERY REQUEST ON *THE SAME* THREAD, AND `Dispatchers.IO` SILENTLY BREAKS IT WITH BYTE-IDENTICAL OUTPUT (round MOD.4b).** Symbol/Type id sequences are thread-local and `runWithDeepStack` hands them off from the CALLER's counters (INV.6(6c0)), so a coroutine that lands on a different carrier thread hands off from counters still at zero, restarts ids at 1 and collides with the singleton intrinsics — invisible to the corpus, to a `--listAll` diff and to `cost_gate.py` alike. `CompileServer.onCompileThread` (a single-thread executor) is the guard and `CompileThreadInvariantTest` the pin; its load-bearing case is the CONCURRENT one, which a pooled dispatcher fails while passing every same-caller assertion. Never `launch { }` per connection.
- **THE LAUNCHER'S DEV CLASSPATH IS `…-daemon/build/install/lib` (Gradle's `xtscLib` Sync), NOT a hand-listed set of jars (round MOD.4b).** The old form pasted known jar names plus `build/bench/cp.txt` — the COMPILER's dependency tail — so the commit that gave the daemon its own dependencies (`-api`, ktor, coroutines) omitted them and died with `ClassNotFoundException` at RUN time with a green build. Add a module dependency freely; never re-hand-list the classpath.
- **A GRADLE `-I` INIT SCRIPT APPLIES TO *EVERY* BUILD IN THE INVOCATION — INCLUDING `build-logic` — AND AN UNQUALIFIED TASK NAME NOW RUNS IN ALL THREE PROJECTS (round MOD.3).** The classpath-dump init script used by `bench-compile-tsc.sh` / `cost_gate.py` / `ab-*.sh` registered its task under `allprojects`: post-split the root throws (no `jvmRuntimeClasspath`) and **`-api` prints a SECOND, WRONG `XTSC_CLASSPATH=` line that the readers' `head -1` may pick** — a silently wrong benchmark, not an error. It must be `rootProject { findProject(":xemantic-typescript-compiler-core")?.tasks?.register(…) }` (`findProject`, since `build-logic` has no such project and a hard `project(":…")` fails the run during plugin resolution), invoked QUALIFIED, with the reader asserting exactly one line. `ab-interleaved.sh`/`ab-warm.sh` re-use the generated file and refuse a pre-split one by grepping it for the module name.
- **…AND THE CACHED `build/bench/xtsc-classpath.txt` OUTLIVES THE THING IT DESCRIBES: A HARNESS THAT READS IT WITHOUT THAT GUARD RUNS AN OLD COMPILER AT EXIT 0 WITH A PLAUSIBLE ERROR COUNT AND NO TELL (round 852).** The file found in the tree was PRE-MODULE-SPLIT — its only class directory was the ROOT project's `build/classes/kotlin/jvm/main`, which the split left behind but which A/B harnesses still WRITE (so its mtime is recent and a staleness check by date proves nothing): 582 classes against the core module's 649, missing `ModeLedger`/`LibTypeCensus`/`CcetSections`, i.e. a pre-round-848 binary. `scripts/grid838.sh` had no guard and every dashboard capture it produced since the split is unverified. **Grep any cached classpath for `xemantic-typescript-compiler-core/build/classes` before believing a capture**, and treat the root `build/classes` as an A/B scratch area, never as the compiler.
- **…AND THE FRESHNESS GUARD THAT *DOES* EXIST IS USUALLY POINTED AT THE WRONG FILE: A CACHED CLASSPATH IS A CLAIM ABOUT A BUILD, AND THE INPUT THAT INVALIDATES IT IS `gradle/libs.versions.toml` — NOT THE MODULE'S `build.gradle.kts`, WHICH A VERSION BUMP LEAVES UNTOUCHED (round 858).** `ab-warm.sh` guarded its `cp-warm.txt` cache with exactly that wrong comparison, which is how `build/bench/cp.txt` came to name kotlin-stdlib 2.4.0 / kotlinx-io 0.9.0 / serialization 1.9.0 a month after the build moved to 2.4.10 / 0.9.1 / 1.11.0 — **every jar it named still existed and the compiler still linked against them, so there was no error, no crash and no wrong answer, only a measurement of something other than what ships.** A guard aimed at the wrong input is indistinguishable from no guard; the ablation shows it, since dropping `libs.versions.toml` from the watched set reddens only the libs-versions pin while the module-build-file pin stays GREEN. Everything now resolves through `scripts/lib/dep-classpath.sh` (non-empty + newer than EVERY build-definition input + every named entry exists), `build/bench/cp.txt` has ZERO readers and `DepClasspathGuardTest` keeps it that way. **Also from that round: the documented "pre-split refusal" greps the INIT SCRIPT, not the cache, and sits in the resolve branch — so a cache HIT skips it entirely. Check that a refusal FIRES, never that it exists.** `docs/perf/classpath-readers-audit.md`.
- **A DEPENDENCY-TAIL DIFFERENCE IS *BEHAVIOURALLY* NIL AND ITS TIMING EFFECT IS UNDEMONSTRATED — BUT MEASURING IT PRODUCED A TEXTBOOK 3/3 SWEEP THAT DID NOT REPLICATE, SO USE IT AS THE REFERENCE CASE FOR ROUND 840(c) (round 858).** Same binary, same profile, only the tail swapped: batch 1 read **+3.63%, fresh slower 3/3, both arm sds under 1%**; batch 2 read **+0.33%, 2/3** with deltas straddling zero, because the *fresh* arm itself moved **−2.7% between batches on a byte-identical configuration**. One batch would have been written up as real and used to "correct" two earlier rounds. All four `--listAll` digests were `59d930db…` and all 12 compiles `46 errors`, so behaviour never moved. **A sign-consistent paired batch is not a result; the second batch costs one run block and is the only thing that separates drift landing on an arm from an effect.**
- **THE JAR FILE NAME IS A CONTRACT: core's `base { archivesName }` is pinned to `"xemantic-typescript-compiler"`, NOT `project.name` (round MOD.3).** `scripts/aot-draw-variance.sh` and `scripts/aot-corpus-suite.sh` glob `xemantic-typescript-compiler-jvm-*.jar`; letting the jar become `…-core-jvm-*.jar` makes those globs match nothing. `-core` is an internal module boundary the distribution never learns about. (The LAUNCHER no longer globs by name — post-MOD.4b it takes the whole staged lib dir, see the entry below.)
- **THE THREE SHIPPED ENTRY POINTS DELIBERATELY DISAGREE, AND "MAKING THEM AGREE" IS A REGRESSION IN WHICHEVER DIRECTION YOU DO IT (MOD.7, 2026-08-10).** `scripts/xtsc` + `scripts/xtsc-aot` run `…compiler.server.XtscMainKt` (they have a daemon to reach); the **GraalVM image is built by `:xemantic-typescript-compiler-cli`** — `./gradlew :xemantic-typescript-compiler-cli:nativeImage`, binary at that module's `build/native/xtsc` — from `…compiler.cli.MainKt`, which REFUSES `--serve`/`--daemon`/`--socket` with exit 2; and `…compiler.MainKt` may be neither, because the compiler's argument loop ends in `else -> if (!a.startsWith("-")) o.project = a`, so it adopts a socket path as the PROJECT and exits 0 (round 840, measured, 173 files emitted). Pointing the image back at the dispatcher re-adds ktor-network + slf4j + `-api` to a binary that can never open a socket (23 → 15 runtime modules); `AotCacheGuardTest` refuses a `nativeImage` in the daemon module and `NativeImageEntryPointTest` refuses the other two classes. **kotlinx-serialization is NOT removed by that split and never can be at the entry point** — the core parses tsconfig.json with it. PGO passthroughs on the task: `-PnativeImageArgs="…"` and `-PnativeImageOutput=name` (Oracle GraalVM only; CE has no PGO).
- **THE AOT LAUNCHER'S DEV CLASSPATH IS A *STAGED* DIR THAT ONLY `assemble` CREATES, SO A FRESH CHECKOUT OR A `clean` LEAVES `scripts/xtsc` UNABLE TO START AT ALL — and post-split nothing had run it, so the launcher was dead for the whole MOD window (round 857).** `xtsc_resolve_classpath`'s dev fallback is `…-daemon/build/install/lib` (the `xtscLib` Sync); with it absent the script dies `cannot resolve a classpath … or build this repo first`. Fail-safe, but it means **`./gradlew assemble` is a prerequisite of every AOT measurement**, and a harness that assembles its own classpath instead (the old `<core jar>:$(cat build/bench/cp.txt)` form) is no longer a PREFIX of the trained one — the JVM then declines the cache in `AOTMode=auto` and **both arms run uncached, agree, and prove nothing**. Any harness carrying an AOT arm must read that staged dir with the launcher's own `find -maxdepth 1 -name '*.jar' | LC_ALL=C sort` AND assert a non-zero `shared objects file` class-load count.
- **THE AOT FINGERPRINT RECORDS EACH CLASSPATH ENTRY'S *BASENAME*, NEVER ITS PATH (`${entry##*/}`), SO A BYTE-IDENTICAL COPY OF THE LIB DIR AT ANOTHER LOCATION VERIFIES `USE` AGAINST THE SAME CACHE (round 857).** The guard binds the ARTIFACT, not the install location — which is what lets a distribution be relocated without retraining, and what makes a mutate-a-copied-lib-dir experiment a valid arm rather than an automatic miss. Do not "strengthen" it to full paths: that would refuse every relocated install for no correctness gain.
- **`xemantic { applyAllConventions() }` IS SINGLE-MODULE-ONLY AND MUST NOT BE RESTORED — its `applyJarManifests()` breaks any subproject two ways (round MOD.2).** Its `allprojects` callback calls `archiveBaseName.get()` while a Jar is being CREATED (for a subproject that runs BEFORE the Kotlin plugin sets it — callbacks fire in registration order and the root always evaluates first, so a `base { archivesName = … }` in the module does NOT fix it), and it reads `project.xemantic`, an extension that exists only on the root. The replacement is root build.gradle.kts's `afterEvaluate` jar-manifest block; the pin that a jar keeps its five `Implementation-*` attributes + `META-INF/LICENSE` is a manual `unzip -p build/libs/*jvm*.jar META-INF/MANIFEST.MF`.
- **A `types: ["node"]` bench measurement on a FRESHLY created profile dir is GARBAGE until `node_modules/@types/node` is copied in (round 730).** Only `build/bench/tsc-project-*` (the compiler profile) carries the real `@types/node` from an earlier online session; `bench-compile-tsc.sh` `rm -rf`s it from every profile it materializes without `--node-stub`. The tell is TS2591 staying at 43 instead of falling to 13 (plus a TS2688) — it looks exactly like a regression. Copy the dir across before measuring.
- **THE 8-PROFILE GRID'S TWO ARMS MUST BE PRODUCED BY THE SAME COMMAND — a `--listAll` patched into `bench-compile-tsc.sh`'s `NOEMIT_ARGS` is OVERWRITTEN by the next line (`[[ $NO_EMIT -eq 1 ]] && NOEMIT_ARGS=(--noEmit)`), so that arm's capture is TRUNCATED to the first 30 diagnostics and the diff reports a regression that does not exist (round 811 read `0 added / 16 removed` on all eight profiles from a byte-identical compiler).** The tell is `... and N more error(s)` in the capture; run both arms as a direct `java … --noEmit --listAll <profileDir>` and have the differ REFUSE any capture containing that string, alongside round 804's non-empty check.
- **…AND EVERY COMMITTED "8-PROFILE GRID" HARNESS IN THIS REPO WAS IN FACT A *ONE*-PROFILE GRID UNTIL ROUND 895 — THE COMPILER PROFILE IS `build/bench/tsc-project-<commit8>` (a historical name) WHILE THE OTHER SEVEN ARE `build/bench/tsc-<name>-<commit8>`, SO THE `tsc-project-*` GLOB THEY ALL USED MATCHES EXACTLY ONE DIRECTORY.** It fails SILENTLY and in the reassuring direction: the loop runs, both arms capture, the differ prints `added=0 removed=0` and the round records a green 8-profile grid it never ran — which is exactly what makes profile-unique files (harness/server/services, round 782's own lesson one entry up) invisible. **Enumerate profiles by the presence of a `tsconfig.json` and REFUSE a run that finds fewer than 8** (`scripts/round895-grid.sh` is the reference); treat any pre-895 round's grid claim as covering the compiler profile only.
- **A CAPTURE md5 IS A PROPERTY OF (OUTPUT x RECIPE), SO IT IS ONLY COMPARABLE ACROSS ROUNDS THAT USED THE SAME RECIPE — *FOUR* LIVE LINEAGES EXIST FOR THE SAME COMPILER-PROFILE OUTPUT, AND ROUND 853 REPRODUCED THEM ALL FROM ONE VERIFIED CAPTURE (round 841 + round 853).** The fourth is round 848's `4090b73e…` (`grep 'error TS' | sed 's#.*/src/#src/#' | sort`), which read like a stale-binary artifact for a whole round and was not one. `59d930db…` = `grep 'error TS' | sort`, 46 lines, absolute paths (rounds 826, 836-840); `4caacf24…` = the whole `--listAll` stdout minus `[`/`time:` lines, sorted, 54 lines (rounds 828-835); and **`scripts/grid838.sh`, the committed harness a next round will reach for, additionally `sed`s the project prefix away and prints `84bbe7f0…`, which NO round note records** — so its first user will read a `sed` as a regression. Quote the recipe with the digest, prefer `added=0 removed=0` against a rebuilt before-arm for anything cross-round, and keep the md5 for the within-round many-runs check. `docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 11.
- **`scripts/bench-compile-tsc.sh` stat parsing uses GNU `grep -oP` — on macOS (BSD grep) every parsed stat (self_ms, errors, files, emitted) silently logs as 0 in the bench TSV; `wall_ms` is real.** A TSV row with self_ms=0/errors=0 is a macOS parse artifact, not a 0-error run — recover the numbers from the run log it points at (or install GNU grep).
- **AN ABLATION IS EVIDENCE ONLY IF YOU COUNT HOW OFTEN THE DISABLED CODE ACTUALLY RAN (round 753 proved both directions in one session).** "Cut it out and the profile stayed byte-identical" is the standard deletion test and it is silently unsound: the objlit annotation fallback (`discriminantKeysOfMember`'s AST leg) ablates byte-identical on the compiler profile and is LOAD-BEARING off it — the profile simply carries no `p: typeof X` shape — while the `.kind` DOMAIN veto ablated byte-identical having FIRED 11,667 times, which is what made deleting it safe. So instrument the ablation to count suppressed verdicts (or classify per SIGHTING) before reading a green run as deletion evidence; a zero count means the run tested nothing. Same failure class as round 744's "100% artifacts, deletable" inventory and round 750's frozen-memo probe.
- TypeScript compiler tests are **generated** by `./gradlew generateTypeScriptTests` into `build/generated/typescript-tests/`; this task requires the TypeScript repo to be cloned first (done automatically via `cloneTypeScriptRepo` dependency). Never edit generated files manually.
- **The test suite is C = A − B, mirroring tsgo's EXACT harness (build.gradle.kts) — the full-suite count is NOT the raw tsc corpus.** **(A) set A is PINNED to the PRISTINE mainline tsc commit tsgo tracks, NOT the tsgo-port branch tip.** `cloneTypeScriptRepo` pins `typescript-repo` to `typeScriptCommit` = the `main`-parent of tsgo's `_submodules/TypeScript` merge. **LOAD-BEARING: do NOT pin to the tsgo submodule sha directly — the `tsgo-port` branch REGENERATES its reference baselines to the Go compiler's DIVERGENT output** (e.g. type-id-based union-member ordering: tsgo emits `'boolean' | 'number'` / `Cover[] | Cover` where pristine tsc — and we — emit `'number' | 'boolean'` / `Cover | Cover[]`). We diff char-by-char against ORIGINAL tsc (owner directive: don't chase tsgo divergences), so pin to the pristine `main` commit (the 2nd parent of the "Merge branch 'main' into tsgo-port" commit tsgo's submodule points at). **(B) set B is tsgo's own two-part skip** in `generateTypeScriptTests`: (1) `tsgoSkippedTests` — whole-FILE skip (tsgo `compiler_runner.go skippedTests`): removed-option parse-failures (`verbatimModuleSyntaxCompat*`, `preserveValueImports*`, `importsNotUsedAsValues*`, `keyofStringsOnly`, `noStrictGenericChecks`, `moduleNone*`, `noImplicitUseStrict_*`, …) + `typescript.d.ts`-dependent `APISample_*`; (2) `usesUnsupportedOption` — whole-CONFIG skip (BOTH the `.errors.txt` AND `.js`/`.d.ts` subtest) when a config's directives include a tsgo-removed feature (tsgo `SkipUnsupportedCompilerOptions`): target es3/es5, module amd/umd/system, moduleResolution node/node10/classic, `outFile`, `baseUrl`, `esModuleInterop:false`, `allowSyntheticDefaultImports:false`, `alwaysStrict:false`. Faithful because tsgo runs the filter on the harness-PARSED options (explicit directives only; unset stays zero-valued → never matches). **PLUS (2026-07-02, user-approved, DELIBERATELY beyond tsgo's own harness): `tsconfigInTestUsesRemovedFeature` — a whole-FILE skip when a tsconfig.json EMBEDDED in the test sets `module: amd/umd/system` or `outFile` (those bypass the directive filter; tsgo runs such tests but its compiler rejects the option, so our pristine-tsc baselines would pin removed-feature behavior). Deliberately NARROW: tsconfig-in-test `target: es5` / `moduleResolution: node10` / `baseUrl` are NOT skipped (~55 active tests use them incidentally while pinning still-relevant paths/suffix/decl-emit behavior).** tsgo's `skippedEmitTests` (Go parallel-emit nondeterminism) is deliberately NOT mirrored (not a removed feature; our harness is deterministic). Prior heuristic (drop only es3/es5/amd/system/umd JS-emit, keep error-baseline) is REPLACED by this. `scripts/tsgo_relevance.py` is a retained safety-net reporter. `effectiveTarget` still maps an EXPLICIT ES3/ES5 → ES2015. See TSGO-RELEVANCE.md.
- **`usesUnsupportedOption` IS A PER-*CONFIG* SKIP, SO A FILE WHOSE BARE DIRECTIVES ARE UNSUPPORTED STILL GENERATES TESTS FROM ITS VARIATIONS — `@target: es5, es2015` drops the bare subtests and KEEPS the `(target=es2015)` ones (round 836).** Any offline replication of the generator (the M3.0 per-case survey in `docs/conformance-worklist.md` is one) that reads it as a whole-FILE skip silently loses ~10% of the corpus and reports the lost cases as absent rather than failing — 13 of 125 conformance cases on the first pass, including every `labeledStatements` `NoCrash` fixture. Only `tsgoSkippedTests` and `tsconfigInTestUsesRemovedFeature` are file-level.
- **An UNSET `@target` maps to `effectiveTarget = ES2024` (tsc `getEmitScriptTarget`: unset → LatestStandard/ES2025), NOT ES2015 (CompilerOptions.kt `effectiveTarget`).** So a no-`@target` test KEEPS native class fields / async / spread (`useDefineForClassFields` ≥ ES2022 → true) — the corpus baselines were generated by a tsc/tsgo whose unset-target default is high, so they are internally consistent (`classPropertyInferenceFromBroaderTypeConst`'s baseline keeps `D = DEFAULT;`). **THERE ARE THREE TARGET NOTIONS AND A NEW READ MUST PICK ONE DELIBERATELY (rounds 944/945):** `effectiveTarget` is EMIT ONLY (45 Transformer/Emitter sites; explicit es3/es5 -> ES2015); **`defaultedTarget` (named `libTarget` for one round) is EVERY CHECKER QUESTION** — tsc's own `getEmitScriptTarget`, the single `languageVersion` its whole checker reads — covering BOTH lib availability (`libFeatureAvailable`/`libProvidesGlobalAt`/`bindRealLibs`) and the 23 `target < ES2015` DOWNLEVEL gates; the RAW `options.target` survives at exactly THREE sites, all documented in `CompilerOptions.defaultedTarget`'s KDoc (the two `target >= ES2015 || …` strict-mode determinations, which a flip would make unconditionally strict, and one per-fixture baseline pin). **`effectiveTarget` is NEVER right for a checker question** — it maps an explicit `es5` UP, which deletes that program's genuine TS2550/TS2583 (lib direction, round 941/944) and OPENS every downlevel gate tsc keeps shut for it (round 945). **Both halves were recorded here as deliberate and both were merely INVISIBLE**: the corpus never tested either, because **6,436 of its 6,573 case files name an explicit `@target`** (TypeScript pinned them when it dropped ES5) and no case file reaching a lib-availability mechanism omits `@target`/`@lib` (0 of 55 / 0 of 30+ / 0 of 26). Round 944's own "the downlevel gates are UNCHANGED and that is a measured decision" is **RETRACTED**: its evidence — 4 pristine-only TS2488 rows — was misattributed (we are silent for those shapes at `esnext` too, so no gate suppresses them; they are (CHK.22), an unimplemented iterability check), and the real family has the OPPOSITE sign — a tsconfig with no `target` collected TS1250/TS1501/TS1503/TS2659/TS2737/TS18045 from us and nothing from tsc. **A pin about a downlevel gate must name its target explicitly** (`CompilerTestSupport.DOWNLEVEL_ES5`); 21 pre-945 pins relied on the ES3 default to open a gate for them.
