- **…AND THAT RAW READ IS RIGHT FOR *LIB AVAILABILITY* AND WRONG FOR A *SYNTAX-SUPPORT* GATE, BECAUSE `options.target` DEFAULTS TO **ES3** WHILE tsc's UNSET DEFAULT IS THE LATEST STANDARD (round 941, TS18028).** `if (options.target <= ScriptTarget.ES5)` made every `#private` field in a project with NO `target` an error; the predicate a syntax gate wants is the target the user ASKED FOR — `options.targetExplicitlySet && options.target <= ES5` — and NOT `effectiveTarget`, which maps an explicit ES5 up to ES2015 and would drop the true positive. **The corpus cannot see either side**: `usesUnsupportedOption` skips every explicit es3/es5 config, so no ACTIVE baseline exercises such a gate at all. The sibling `target < ES2015` dispatch gates (e.g. `checkBlockScopedFunctionDeclarations`, TS1250) are unaudited and inherit the same question.
- **Disabled test category**: `.errors.txt` error baseline tests are commented out in `build.gradle.kts` `generateTypeScriptTests` task — search for `TODO: Re-enable when type checker is implemented`. Re-enabling adds ~9,055 tests (see docs/history/PLAN.md item 4c).
- Kotlin 2.x disallows `.` in backtick-quoted JVM method names (error: "Name contains illegal characters: .."); sanitize test function names by replacing dots with underscores (e.g. `foo_ts` not `foo.ts`). Some TypeScript test base names contain dots beyond the extension (e.g. `accessors_spec_section-4.5_error-cases`), so the entire `nameWithoutExtension` must have dots replaced, not just the `.ts`/`.js` suffix.
- **Write `src/commonTest` so it would compile for Kotlin/Native — NOTHING CHECKS THIS ANY MORE (all native targets commented out 2026-07-25 for loop speed), so violations accumulate SILENTLY and are paid for in a cleanup session whenever a native target returns.** Kotlin/Native is stricter than the JVM on three axes, and round 682 cleared 169 errors accrued since round 610 — while `./gradlew jvmTest` stayed 100% green throughout, because it is a JVM-only gate. (1) Backtick names additionally reject `(`, `)`, `,`, `&`, `@` ("Name contains illegal characters") — keep common test names to letters/digits/spaces/`-`/`_`/`'`, rendering a parenthetical aside as ` - aside` (`src/jvmTest` names are unaffected — that asymmetry is exactly why the drift goes unnoticed). (2) `kotlin.assert` is `@ExperimentalNativeApi` there and unusable from common code (and `@OptIn` for a native-only annotation cannot be written in common) — use `com.xemantic.kotlin.test.assert(cond)`, which is multiplatform AND power-assert-enabled (build.gradle.kts `powerAssert.functions`), so it takes NO message: the failure diagram renders every subexpression value, which is why the library's `message` parameter is not meant to be used. It also always evaluates, unlike `kotlin.assert` (a silent no-op without `-ea`). (3) JVM-only stdlib extensions (`String.format`) don't exist. To CHECK: `./gradlew -PenableNativeTargets=true compileTestKotlinLinuxX64` is now runnable LOCALLY under the memory protocol below (~1m40s); CI still runs `.github/workflows/native.yml`. **Round 822 re-measured the drift after ~10 dark rounds and found only 17 violations, ALL of axis 1 and ALL the same character — `,`** — because axes 2 and 3 are structurally closed by the one-idiom `assert` rule and the no-JVM-API rule, which agents inherit by copying neighbouring code, while a COMMA is the one illegal character that occurs naturally in English prose and this entry's actionable advice is about parentheses. Expect the same distribution next time: grep for `,` in backtick names first.
- **THERE IS A FOURTH KOTLIN/NATIVE DRIFT AXIS AND IT IS A *RUNTIME* ONE, SO `compileTestKotlinLinuxX64` BEING GREEN PROVES NOTHING ABOUT IT: a `commonTest` that drives multi-thousand-frame RECURSION compiles clean and then HARD-CRASHES the test process** (`runWithDeepStack` is a pass-through on native and a native stack overflow is uncatchable, so `StackOverflowError` there is a never-thrown stub). Gradle reports `Test running process exited unexpectedly. Current test: <name>` and every alphabetically-LATER class then silently never runs — one crasher masks all the rest, so finding them is iterative (round 822 lost 4,414 of 13,689 tests to a single test). **Depth alone is not the predicate**: 30,000-term chains, a 3,000-statement flow chain and the corpus's 6,452-term `binderBinaryExpressionStress` all PASS because the walkers are iterative; what crashes is true deep recursion (a >2,000-level flow walk) or any pin asserting the JVM boundary guard's TS2589. **ROUND 827 MOVED THE BUDGET, NOT THE AXIS: `runWithDeepStack` now runs the native pipeline on a `pthread_attr_setstacksize(256 MB)` thread, so `CfaTooLargeBailTest` + `Inv4SpineAccessorModifierDeepChainTest` are back in `commonTest` and green — but an overflow is still UNCATCHABLE there, so a deep enough shape still kills the process and a TS2589-asserting pin is still unexpressible (`DeepExpressionChainTest` stays `src/jvmTest`).** A `none { code == 2589 }` assertion that DOES ride along is vacuous on native — say so in the KDoc rather than reading it as coverage.

- **`--noEmit` means the COMPILE CORE must not transform/emit either (round 738) — and the gate is `CompilerOptions.skipEmitOutputs`, NEVER `options.noEmit`.** `noEmit` is also a corpus DIRECTIVE that **440 generated tests set**, and their baselines were produced by a core that still emits; `skipEmitOutputs` is set only by `ProjectCompiler` from its own `noEmit` parameter. Do not "simplify" the Phase-3 gate to `options.noEmit` — `SkipEmitOutputsTest`'s negative control exists for exactly that. Round 738's follow-on benchmark claim ("every pre-738 xtsc-vs-tsc ratio compared our check+emit against tsc's check-only; the gap is ~2.15×") is **RETRACTED — round 739 read the scripts: the CI 3-way runs EVERY compiler with emit, so the 2.4× was already like-for-like and this gate does not move it.**
- **A PERF RATIO IS MODE-SCOPED, AND THE TWO MODES ARE DIFFERENT COMPILES (round 739, `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md` § 0.2 has the table).** `bench-3way.sh` (the published `bench-history/` ratio) runs xtsc, tsc AND tsgo **with emit**; `ab-interleaved.sh` and `cost_gate.py` — i.e. every perf number this arc produces — run `--noEmit`. Measured: the emit work is **8.5% of a check-only compile**, so the two are not interchangeable and **the check-only ratio was never measured on either side** (the bench never ran tsc with `--noEmit`; it does now). Always name the mode with the ratio, and never read ONE CI row as the ratio — xtsc is a single cold JVM run per row and the ratio ranges 1.87×–2.72× across 340 rows of an essentially unchanged compiler.
- **A SECTION-PROBE RUN ON A MEMORY-STARVED BOX INFLATES EVERY ROW BY ~270x WHILE THE RATIO BETWEEN ARMS SURVIVES, SO NO INTERNAL CONSISTENCY CHECK CAN CATCH IT (round 800).** A batch that built and then immediately measured — the build's own Kotlin daemon still resident, BUILD.1's trap — read 6,483 ms for a row whose true value is 5-11 ms and took 9.6 min per compile instead of 26 s. **The only tell is the ABSOLUTE ns/call against the previous round's table**; put `./gradlew --stop` plus a graceful bracket-pattern kotlin-daemon kill INSIDE the script, between the build and the runs, and note that DETERMINISTIC counters are immune and can still carry the round.
- **WATCHING A BENCHMARK RUN IS PART OF THE BENCHMARK — round 774's A/A read −6.70% on the SAME binary while the agent `tail`ed its log every few seconds, and −0.36% / −0.54% / +1.02% when left alone (process-median sd 278 → 48 ms).** One xtsc run already takes ~3.15 of this box's 4 cores, so the spare capacity your shell commands eat is the capacity the measurement needs: start a run in the background, then END THE TURN and react to the notification — do not poll. There are TWO A/B protocols with different bands (COLD `scripts/ab-interleaved.sh` ±2.0%, WARM `scripts/ab-warm.sh` ±1.0%, `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md` § 6); a warm run whose printed per-arm sd exceeds ~1% was not measured on a quiet box and its verdict must be discarded, however clean the median looks.
- **`bench/` is GITIGNORED (.gitignore line 62) — `bench/*.tsv` is a LOCAL dashboard, not a shared baseline.** "Re-baseline the dashboard" cannot be done by committing TSV rows; the shared record is `bench-history/` (CI-written), STATUS.md and the round notes. A TSV row you add is invisible to the next agent on another machine.
- **The crawl's per-file read/pre-parse spans are ELAPSED-WITH-SUSPENSION, not CPU (round 738).** They bracket a `withContext(...)`, so a file's "parse" includes waiting for a `Dispatchers.Default` slot — which is why the pre-parse sum is ~10.7× the crawl's WALL. **Only the crawl WALL is a wall-clock price**; never add those sub-sums into a total. Race-freedom there is by construction (each element carries its own nanos back; the SINGLE-THREADED collector sums them) — a `+=` from the workers races exactly as `PassTiming.nodeKindHistogram` does.

### Measured dead-ends (full detail archived — do NOT re-attempt without reading the archive entry)

- **`BinderResult.flowGraph` IS BUILT ON FIRST ASK ((INC.9)), SO A NEW `binderResults` LOOP THAT TOUCHES IT SILENTLY RESTORES A 126 ms WHOLE-PROGRAM COST — AND NO GATE HERE CAN SEE IT (2026-08-22).** The corpus, `cost_gate.py` and every whole-program wall are UNCHANGED by construction (a full build asks for every checked file's graph anyway); only the incremental floor moves, and only `scripts/partition-equivalence.sh`'s floor/median arms report it. The receipt is a COUNT, not a ms: flow graphs built per compile are **123 -> 0** on the floor arm and **123 -> 78** on a FULL build, the 45 that vanish being the real-lib `.d.ts` files that were bound with a graph on every compile and read by nobody. Touch it inside a program-wide pass only behind the guard that pass already has (`init:evolvingArrayUseSiteWalks` reads it after `if (autoArrayNames.isEmpty()) continue`), and keep the `lazy` — `CheckerPool` and `--shareBind` hand ONE `BinderResult` set to several threads, so a plain nullable field publishes a half-built graph.
- **…AND THE LAW IT MEASURES IS THAT A COST PRIOR DOES NOT TRANSFER ACROSS *REGIMES* EITHER: the identical candidate is 0.3% of a FULL build and 24% of an incremental FLOOR (2026-08-22, (INC.9)).** `docs/perf/warm-flow-graph-attribution.md` § 9.3 priced "skip a FILE whose graph is never read" at **52 of 123 files, 885 nodes, 0.3% of the mints** and refused it — correct for a build that checks every file; under a `recheckOnly` partition the same rule reaches **122 of 123**. Round 789's "a cost prior from one family of sites does not transfer to another" now has a second axis: **before re-reading any refusal in this repo, ask which REGIME it was measured in** (full vs partitioned, cold vs warm, sequential vs `--workers`).
- **EVERY `BinderResult` FROM ONE `Binder` SHARES THAT BINDER'S `nodeToSymbol` AND `moduleInstanceStates` MAPS, WHOSE KEYS ARE `(pos, end)` PACKED — SO THEY COLLIDE ACROSS FILES AND ARE LAST-WINS IN BIND ORDER (2026-08-22).** That is what makes per-file bind REUSE unsound as a drop-in (a re-bind of one file cannot reproduce the program-wide table), and it is invisible from `bind()`'s signature, which reads as if each call produced its own tables. The other half of the refusal: `Checker.mergeSingleSymbol` ADOPTS binder-owned symbols into `globals` and `declarations.addAll` is not idempotent (round 881: adopts 406, mutates 175), so a second consumer over reused tables appends duplicates.
- **THE INCREMENTAL FLOOR IS 75% PROGRAM-WIDE TAIL WALKERS AND ONLY 20% BIND, WHICH INVERTS THE RANKING EVERY EARLIER ROUND IMPLIED (2026-08-22, (INC.3), compiler profile, one process, palindrome-drawn).** Of a 1,219 ms floor: the 400 tail walkers **806.7 ms (66.2%)**, the 16 `init:*` setup passes 112.2 (9.2%), BIND **240.6 (19.7%)**, crawl 27.4 (2.2%), `checkSpine` **0.1**. **Round 880's "bind 515 ms" DOES NOT TRANSFER — it is a per-WORKER term measured under `--workers 4` with +37% contention in the same fit** (round 883's `--shareBind` −5% is that contention being collected), and the crawl's "~138 ms" is gone because parses are fully content-cached (`78 reused / 0 fresh`, PARSE row zero calls). **Only 24 of the 400 tail walkers narrow at all** (those iterating `checkedResults`); the other 376 iterate `binderResults`, drive their own whole-tree walk, and cost the same whether the checker checks 78 files or none. The tail is FLAT — largest single pass 26.3 ms, 100 passes at a mean 7.9 ms carrying 98% — so "make one cheaper" has no one to make cheaper. Instrument: `scripts/floor-decomposition.sh`. Two more figures it retires: `init:buildFileLocalTypeMaps` is **1.4%** of a warm compile, not 3.56% (rounds 829/859/861 landed levers in it), and the two never-warming whole-program regex passes are **0.44 ms** between them, gated by rounds 859/862.
- **A SOURCE-SCANNING ANALYZER OVER `Checker.kt` WILL PARSE ITS OWN *KDoc EXAMPLE* AS REAL CODE, AND THE FAILURE IS TOTAL AND REASSURING: the `pass("name") { … }` sample at `Checker.kt:9537` is indistinguishable from a registration in raw source, and its brace match runs away over the rest of the file — so EVERY walker's private closure came out empty and every one read "no hazard" (2026-08-22, (INC.7) batch 2).** Its sibling that round: `\badd\s*\(` matched `xs.add(` as a call to a file-level `add`, wiring every walker into everything. **Both failed toward "safe to change", which is the only direction that matters for a classifier, and neither is visible without controls** — the working set was: a count of `fun` names found, a list of known functions that must be present, a NON-EMPTY closure for a walker known to call helpers, a balanced mode stack at EOF, and at least one KDoc match REFUSED. Round 809's stripper entry is necessary and nowhere near sufficient.
- **GATING A TAIL WALKER ONTO THE PARTITION BANKS ~79% OF ITS ROW, NOT 100% — ROUND 788's LAW ON A THIRD INSTRUMENT (2026-08-22, (INC.7) batch 1, measured).** Eight walkers whose floor rows summed to 162.66 ms went to 0.01, and the floor fell **128.4**: **+34.2 ms reappeared on OTHER passes**, led by `checkArrayIsArrayCompoundElseIndex` (1.05 -> 19.91 ms). The gated walkers were driving shared type resolutions that are MEMOIZED, so the first later asker pays. **So the 806.7 ms tail is an UPPER BOUND on what gating all 376 returns**, and any per-pass sum quoted as a prize needs the same discount until re-measured. **BATCH 2 CONFIRMED THE PREDICTION: 221.3 ms of rows banked 189.1 = 85.5%, leaking the SAME ABSOLUTE ~32 ms as batch 1 did from a 36% smaller batch** — relocation lands on ungated tail walkers, so the more are gated the more of the moved work is never asked for at all. Corollary for the classification: the two candidates DISQUALIFIED in batch 1 are the two whose loop bodies look identical to the eight that qualified — they differ only in ambient installs above and below the `for`, which is invisible unless you read past it.
- **AND `checkedResults` IS `binderResults` *EXACTLY* WHEN `assignedFileNames == null`, SO MOVING A PASS LOOP ONTO IT IS A STRICT NO-OP FOR EVERY FULL BUILD AND FOR THE WHOLE CORPUS — IT CAN ONLY CHANGE A PARTITION (`Checker.kt:110`).** That is what makes (INC.7) attemptable, and it is the exact edge round 609 fell off: gating a COLLECTOR that way starves the partition of program-wide suppression context and INVENTS 1,174 TS2339 false positives. A pure EMITTER (loop body does nothing but `diagnostics.add`) is safe, because a partition reports nothing about unassigned files anyway; anything that writes a side set, retracts, installs ambient, reads `diagnostics` back, or needs file B walked to judge file A is not. **The detector is `scripts/partition-equivalence.sh`** — a starved partition ADDS rows, which is what it compares — and the corpus is the second gate, where a moved baseline means the change reached a full build and the no-op reasoning is wrong somewhere.
- **A LANGUAGE-SERVICE CACHE MUST BE SERVED BY *CONTAINMENT OF WHAT WAS ASKED*, NEVER BY MEMBERSHIP OF THE FILE — AN ANSWER THAT WAS NEVER ASKED FOR IS **ABSENT**, AND AN ABSENT CAPTURE RENDERS NOTHING WITH NO ERROR ANYWHERE (2026-08-23, (INC.14)).** `Project.prepare` retains one build's answers for a working set, and the tempting lookup ("is this file prepared?") is wrong for every caret on a node the file-wide request does not carry — a call expression, a literal, a `this`. Decide it against the stored REQUEST's own spans, so the test is a property of what happened rather than of two call sites deriving their span lists the same way today; the pin is a caret on a NON-occurrence in a prepared file, and it is the only thing that reddens when the test is weakened. Same silent-failure direction as `captureIn`'s derived partition one layer down.
- **AND A CAPTURE BUILD'S DIAGNOSTICS ARE NOT A PLAIN BUILD'S, SO NO CAPTURE CACHE MAY EVER ANSWER `diagnostics`/`diagnosticsOf` (standing rule, `docs/language-service.md` § 3; re-checked when (INC.14) added a second capture cache).** A capture types nodes the checker had no reason to type. That is why `prepare` is documented as preparing SEMANTIC queries and why the error-reporting half is a separate mechanism — `diagnosticsOf`'s memo keyed by the PARTITION the build walked, which serves any SUBSET of it by filtering.
- **NARROWING THE *CHECK* FOR AN EDITOR QUERY IS DONE AND CLOSED — A MEDIAN FILE'S OWN CHECKING IS **15 ms** AGAINST A **1,092 ms** FLOOR, i.e. THE FLOOR IS **99%** OF A NARROWED QUERY (2026-08-22, (INC.1), compiler profile warm).** `recheckOnly = {file}` takes a query from 4,818 to 1,107 ms and every one of the 78 files agrees row for row with the full build — so the partition is sound AND essentially exhausted; a further round spent partitioning the checker is chasing 15 ms. **The floor is free to measure and needs no probe: a partition naming a file the program does not contain checks nothing per-file, so what it costs is crawl + parse + bind + the program-wide passes.** Everything left in the incremental arc is inside it ((INC.3)). Two corollaries: an editor's question needs NO reverse-dependency closure (it claims nothing about the other files, which is why round 772's "closure buys nothing on a barrel-exporting codebase" does not apply to it), and a partition's result must NEVER be adopted as a project's build — its diagnostics are a subset and would be reported as the whole program's, silently.
- **THE *CHECK* PATH AND THE *CAPTURE* PATH DISAGREE UNDER A PARTITION, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS SOUND — A TYPE REFERENCE INSIDE A **FOREIGN FILE'S ANONYMOUS OBJECT TYPE LITERAL** RENDERS `any` IN A CAPTURED TYPE WHILE THE SAME BUILD STILL REJECTS THE ASSIGNMENT THAT DEPENDS ON IT (2026-08-22, (INC.2), measured over 381,666 spans: 45 diverge in 11 of 76 files, types 45, definitions 0).** `{ program?: any }` is reported for `{ program?: Program }` — the outer signature survives and the literal's MEMBERS collapse. **It is first-touch cache order and not the partition, which the census proves rather than assumes: in 5 of the 45 the FULL build is the one rendering `any` where the narrowed renders `T`.** **The cause is a LAZY MEMBER TABLE and not name resolution across files, which the divergent rows themselves prove: one loses a KEYWORD type (`fileName: string` -> `any`) and another a mapped-type modifier (`Required<{ f(): void }>` -> `Required<{ f?: any | undefined }>`) — names do not fail that way, an unresolved member table does.** It is round 833's hazard one layer up (*a target type's member table is lazy, so a verdict depends on whether an earlier line happened to resolve it*), except the reader is `typeToString` and the earlier line is A DIFFERENT FILE'S CHECK, which a whole-program build always happens to perform and a partition removes. `definitions=0` across 360,152 captures says only the RENDERING half is affected; the fix is to force `resolveStructuredTypeMembers` before rendering, so the string is a function of the type rather than of who walked first. **Consequences: do NOT infer a capture's correctness from a green diagnostics sweep — they are different resolvers**, and the full-vs-narrow pair is a differential ORACLE for the underlying order-dependence, needing no baseline because the two arms must agree ((INC.5), `scripts/capture-equivalence.sh`).
- **A `Checker` REUSED ACROSS k QUERIES ***IS*** A CHECKER WHOSE PARTITION IS THOSE k FILES, SO THE ORDER-DEPENDENCE OF A RE-ENTRANT CHECKER CAN BE CENSUSED WITH **NO** CHECKER SURGERY (2026-08-23, (INC.14): 1 divergent row in 741,864, and the shared arm is the one that is right).** `recheckOnly` is a SET and the spine walks it in program order either way, so `recheckOnly = {file}` per query versus `recheckOnly = group` once are the two arms, they must agree, and no baseline is recorded — the wall ratio of the same run is the prize (1.79x/3.19x/3.82x at k = 2/8/26). `scripts/checker-reuse-differential.sh`. Vary k to REPLICATE: it re-groups every file, so it is not one draw repeated.
- **AND A FIXTURE PINNING CROSS-FILE CAPTURE ORDER-DEPENDENCE IS HALF BLIND BY CONSTRUCTION — a capture is recorded DURING the walk, so a file walked LATER cannot influence an earlier file's captured answers in EITHER arm (2026-08-23).** Only the second-walked file's rows are sensitive; write the fixture so the file you are testing is the one the crawl sorts LAST, or the pin can only ever see half of what it claims. Three ablations were run against such a pin and all three read green, one of them demonstrably a DEAD arm (`buildFileLocalTypeMaps` resolves only Function/Class/Interface/Enum/TypeAlias/Alias symbols, and a two-file fixture of `const`s holds none).
- **AND THE FIXTURE FOR "DOES NARROWING LOSE A DIAGNOSTIC" IS VACUOUS BY DEFAULT: AN ARGUMENT-POSITION ERROR (`use({ program: 1 })` AGAINST A FOREIGN TYPE LITERAL PARAMETER) IS NOT REPORTED BY THIS COMPILER AT ALL, SO BOTH ARMS AGREE ON AN EMPTY LIST AND THE PIN PASSES HAVING MEASURED NOTHING (2026-08-22).** The shape that DOES report is a read of the literal's member (`const n: number = make().program` -> TS2322). Any equivalence pin over two compilers, two modes or two arms needs a control asserting the reference arm is NON-EMPTY — round 790's "a verifier reads 0 both when the skip is sound and when the instrument is dead", one instrument over.
- **AN IMPOSSIBLE *ORDERING* IS THE CHEAPEST TELL THAT A WARM-UP RAMP IS BEING MEASURED, AND IT IS SHARPER THAN ANY SPREAD STATISTIC (2026-08-22).** A "floor" arm measured 1,632 ms where the median partition it is a strict SUBSET of the work of measured 1,107 — impossible, and the cause was position: the floor sat at slot 3 of the process while the partitions ran at slots 4-81, and the same process read a full build at 9,421 ms against a warm 4,818. **Put timing arms after whatever warms the JVM, rotate them, and check subset-vs-superset before quoting anything**; four rotated draws then spanned 0.7%. Round 869's law with a free consistency check attached.


- **52.3% OF THE FLOW NODES THIS COMPILER MINTS ARE NEVER READ BY ANY CONSUMER, AND IT IS WORTH 0.63% — DO NOT RE-OPEN "the flow graph builds too much" (round 865, `--flowCensus`, `docs/perf/warm-flow-graph-attribution.md` § 9).** Only **22.0% of the minting WALK** sits in function-like containers nothing reads (43 ms of a 196 ms row); 0.3% is in files nothing reads, and the rest is interleaved with read nodes on antecedent chains that cannot be skipped one at a time — what is left over is one allocation each, which round 801 priced at 0 ms. **And the asymmetry is INVERTED from round 864's side table: a missing side-table entry degrades to a correct fallback, a missing FLOW NODE makes `flowAt` answer `null`, nothing narrows, and the compiler emits a FALSE POSITIVE** — so any laziness here must defer-and-build, never omit.
- **THE FLOW-NODE CLASSES MUST STAY PLAIN CLASSES — `FlowCensus` (and any future side table) keys an IDENTITY `HashSet<FlowNode>` on them, which is sound only while they are not `data` classes (round 865).** Adding `data` to `FlowBranchLabel` & co. turns every such set into round 471's deep-`hashCode()` hazard over a mutable antecedent graph, silently. `FlowNode.id` is NOT an alternative key: it restarts at 0 in every file, exactly as `nodeId` does (round 787).
- **THE INV.4 REACH MACHINERY IS CENSUSED AND CLOSED: 3.32 M EDGE EVALUATIONS AND 1.91 M CONSULTATIONS PER WARM REBUILD, AND EVERY MECHANISM IN IT PRICES BELOW 1% (round 875, `docs/perf/reach-machinery.md`).** Three traps a next agent will otherwise re-derive: (1) **the edge predicates' `when (parent) { is X -> }` chains of 41-119 arms are NOT the cost** — amplification measures **13.3 ns per evaluation and 106 arms costs the same as 49**, so the whole population is 44 ms = 0.67% and a `kindId` tableswitch conversion (the M0.2 shape, tempting because round 803 got -3.93% converting `forEachChild`) can take almost none of it; (2) **a push-based status maintained by the walk is 11.1x MORE work**, because it must compute a status for all 43 classifiers at every node while the pull scheme folds only the ancestors of the kinds each is consulted at (16 of the 43 are consulted under 1,000 times, one ONCE); (3) **a memo hit rate of 8%, with 23 classifiers at exactly 0%, is CORRECT, not a cache to fix** — a classifier is consulted at most once per node, so the memo can never answer its own query and exists only to terminate an ascent. Only the memo TRANSPOSITION (43 per-file `nodeId`-keyed arrays into one row per node) is still open, at <= ~0.8%.
- **A PER-FILE, NAME-KEYED "could this root ever be narrowed" SET IS VACUOUS — it refuses 0 of 14,117 openings and costs 150-211 ms to do it (round 855, `docs/perf/narrowed-any-opening-price.md` § 4b).** Every `VariableDeclaration`/`Parameter`/`BindingElement` mints a `FlowAssignment` whose subtree contains the declared name, so **every root DECLARED in a file is in that file's narrowable set by construction** — the set can only refuse a root with no declaration in the file (an import), and real `any` receivers are locals and parameters. Skipping a narrowing walk needs a *path-and-position* oracle, which is the walk; do not re-propose the name-keyed form. Corollary for pinning it: `diagnose()` is SINGLE-FILE, an unresolved import is not `anyType`, so the refusing population cannot be reached from a fixture at all (`openings=0`) — pin such a predicate at the SET level (`FlowGraphBuilder().build(…)`), not through a compile.

- **AND IT IS CLOSED FOR A SECOND, LARGER REASON THAT ITS OWN CENSUS CANNOT SEE: THE PASS'S REAL PRODUCT IS THE PROGRAM'S FIRST-TOUCH *ORDER*, AND DEFERRING IT MOVES THE CAPTURE CHANNEL FROM **5 DIVERGENT SPANS TO 2,722 IN 46 OF 76 FILES** (2026-08-22, (INC.10), built and reverted).** Resolving every file's declarations up front makes type DISPLAY a function of the program rather than of who walked first; 83% of the divergence is `aliasDisplayMap` (an alias name attaches to an interned type at its FIRST mint — `ModuleName` vs `ModuleExportName`) and the residual is UNDIAGNOSED and runs the other way (full `Extract<…>` vs narrow `ClassLikeDeclaration`), so it may be two different `Type` instances rather than two renderings. **Measured trade-off, and it is almost exactly one-for-one: fully deferred = 0.01 ms / 2,722 spans; `TypeAlias` symbols kept eager = 6.81 ms / 462 spans; the whole DECLARATION branch kept eager = 64.94 ms / 5 spans (baseline). The deferrable part is 1.13 ms of 66.** So round 829's "1,499 of 4,161 entries are ever READ" is the wrong question — read-ness of the ENTRY says nothing about the ORDER — and `scripts/capture-equivalence.sh` is the ONLY gate that sees it: `partition-equivalence.sh`, `cost_gate.py` and all ~13k corpus baselines are green on the fully-deferred build. (INC.2)'s "do NOT infer a capture's correctness from a green diagnostics sweep — they are different resolvers", collected.
- **A PASS WHOSE PRODUCT HAS ONE READER MAY STILL BE UNDEFERRABLE, AND A PASS THAT LOOKS LIKE A COLLECTOR MAY BE PURE EMIT-ONLY WORK — CHECK THE READER'S *CALLER*, NOT THE PASS'S NAME (2026-08-22, (INC.10), one of each in the same round).** `trackAllImportReferences` reads as a program-wide collector and is 100% emit-only: `referencedAliases` is read by `isReferencedAliasDeclaration` alone, whose ONE caller is a single line of `Transformer` reached only by `import x = require(…)` under `module: preserve` without `verbatimModuleSyntax` — and round 738's `skipEmitOutputs` gate means a `--noEmit` build never constructs a transformer, so the walk ran **78 times per checker** to fill a set nothing could read. **Two consequences for pinning such a deferral.** Prefer deferring to a static `if (options.skipEmitOutputs) return`: the corpus EMITS, so a deferred path is exercised by thousands of `.js` baselines while a statically gated one is never tested at all. And **a pin written on the mechanism's OBVIOUS shape can be vacuous** — an ESM `import { a } from "./x"` fixture answers correctly with `referencedAliases` EMPTY, so both elision assertions passed against a binary with the ask deleted, and only the ablation said so.
- **`buildFileLocalTypeMaps` IS CENSUSED AND CLOSED, AND ITS BIGGEST-LOOKING PRIZE IS A *DETECTOR*: the `typealias` branch's resolutions exist to trip `deepInstantiationBailed` — the bail is observable ONLY while `getTypeOfSymbol` runs — so not resolving them DELETES a TS2589/TS2615 rather than deferring it (round 829, `docs/perf/setup-phase-and-huge-methods.md` § 27).** Measured with `--fltmCensus`: 12,738 direct resolves produce 4,161 entries of which **1,499** are ever read (read site: `calls=16,043 / distinct=1,499`, **278,355 misses**), and the deletable part — never read AND never asked again — is 47.1% = **0.8-1.0% of the compile**, below the cold A/B band against a program-wide name-resolution blast radius.
- **…AND IT MUST BE KEYED ON A BOUNDARY THE CALLER DOES NOT ALREADY SHORT-CIRCUIT — a hook on an inner `if (cached) return` whose guard is DUPLICATED in the wrapper counts ZERO consumptions and reports it as a finding (round 849).** `resolveStructuredTypeMembersCore`'s guard is repeated one frame up in `resolveStructuredTypeMembers`, so the census read "nothing ever reads a derived lib type back" where the truth is **133.9 reads per production**; the zero is indistinguishable from a real negative, and only a pin built to FAIL if the probe were inert caught it. Counter-form of round 786's "open on the WRAPPER" law — grep the callers for a copy of your guard before believing a zero.
- **…AND ITS "NEVER ASKED AGAIN" VERDICT IS ONLY AS STRONG AS THE GRANULARITY IT IS KEYED AT: `buildFileLocalTypeMaps`'s MOVE test is keyed on SYMBOLS, and its 12,738 direct `getTypeOfSymbol` calls make only **1,842** nested symbol asks between them, reaching **434** symbols they did not start from (round 861).** So ~all of the pass's 225 ms is `getTypeFromTypeNode` / member resolution / type interning — none of it symbol-keyed, none of it visible to the `askedLater` hook — and "deletable" there means *not re-asked at the one granularity carrying almost none of the work*. **Any deletable-ms from that census is an UPPER bound; only a REPLAY ablation (record the deletable `file|name` keys, skip exactly those next rebuild, demand byte-identical output) can separate deletion from relocation.** The tell is `setupAsks` barely exceeding `directResolves` while the row is large.
- **A `java.util.regex` PATTERN THAT BEGINS WITH A ZERO-WIDTH ASSERTION GETS NO BOYER-MOORE PREFIX SEARCH AND IS ATTEMPTED AT *EVERY* POSITION OF THE INPUT — over the 9,977,097-character compiler profile that is 121 ms for a pattern that matches NOTHING, against a seventh as much for its literal-prefixed sibling in the same loop over the same text (round 862, `docs/perf/warm-tail-attribution.md` § 13).** `Pattern.compile` hands the head node to `BnM.optimize` only when it is a literal `Slice`, so `\bdeclare\s+…` pays for the leading `\b` and `import\s*\(…` does not. **This was the whole post-checker region** — round 861's entry here priced `cpcRequireOnlyOrphans` at 130.4 ms = 1.72% and blamed its AST walk; the walk is **0.37 ms** and the region is now **2.84 ms** after an exact literal-anchored rewrite plus deferring its `import("…")` pass behind the candidate sets (139.1 ms = 1.82% of a warm rebuild, **both modes**; the `skipEmitOutputs` gate on top is only 0.03%). Two of the last three rounds found their region's cost to be a whole-program regex matching nothing, so **before designing anything around a whole-text scan, check whether its pattern starts with a literal** — and note no gate in this repo sees such a cost: the corpus runs these rules on 2-file fixtures and `cost_gate.py` counts only checker work.
- **A POPULATION *COUNT* SHARE MAY NOT BE PROJECTED ONTO A ms ROW — round 859 projected 47.1% x 3.56% = "~1.68% warm" and the measurement is **1.09%**, a 1.54x overshoot in three compounding deflations (round 861).** (i) a count share is not a ms share (47.1% of the resolves are 40.5% of their wall); (ii) the sub-region the census measures is not the ROW (the direct-resolve wall is 94.3% of `init:buildFileLocalTypeMaps`); (iii) part of the "deletable" ms is a DETECTOR that deletion would delete. Measure the sub-population in the SAME rebuild as the row — which is why `BenchMain`'s `fltm` tier arms two probes — or the ratio is a cross-draw quotient against a 41%-spread row.
- **A PRODUCED-vs-CONSUMED CENSUS MUST BE KEYED ON THE *CALLS*, NEVER ON WHAT WAS STORED (round 829, a 4x under-read).** Keying the deletable population on map ENTRIES missed the largest population in the pass — **67.3% of its resolutions store nothing at all** (they answer `any`/`errorType`), so they can never be "read" and were structurally invisible; the tell is a classified population that does not sum to its own denominator.
- **A FALSIFIER CONTROL CAN BE DEAD ON THE VERY PROFILE THE PRIZE WAS MEASURED ON — RUN IT WHERE THE THING IT WATCHES ACTUALLY HAPPENS (round 793).** `--ccetPreGateBogus` (the gate refuses everything) reports 14 on harness and **0 on the compiler profile**, because nothing in tsc's own compiler sources ever reaches a call-prologue emission; a "0 skipped calls emitted" column read only there proves nothing. Check the control fires on the SAME run that reports the zero, and if it cannot, say so and move the falsification to a fixture (an in-process probe pin) or another profile.
- **REMOVING A SECTION ALSO REMOVES ITS PROBE BOUNDARIES, SO A BEFORE/AFTER PARTITION DIFF OVERSTATES THE SAVING BY N x BOUNDARY (round 793).** Gating the 7-walker call prologue moved its rows 261 -> 66 ms raw, but 6 of the 7 boundaries (52,413 invocations each) went with them — 28 ms at the ~90 ns differential cost, 96 ms at the 299 ns in-situ read. Subtract the boundaries production never executed before quoting a row delta as a prize, and prefer the ONE-SPAN sub-measure, whose count does not change.


- **WHILE enum -> MEMBER IS DECIDED VACUOUSLY, NO TARGET CAN SEPARATE AN ENUM FROM A SUB-UNION OF ITS OWN MEMBERS — so ANY measurement of "does this enum narrow change a verdict" reads 0 by construction, not empirically (round 765, measured at the consumer).** A consumer-side differential over round 764's subtractive narrow found 4 relation consults on the compiler profile and 10 on services, **0 differing**, with EVERY consultation's target being the enum itself (`SyntaxKind` -> `SyntaxKind`, …) — a sub-union relates by union membership. Do NOT read such a zero as "the narrowing is dead weight, delete it": it becomes load-bearing the moment (REL.2)'s global rule lands. The falsification that makes a zero here trustworthy is to record a deliberately BOGUS baseline and confirm the counter reports differences; a perturbation that replaces the tracked VALUE instead destroys the tracking and can only ever report 0.
- **A CALL WHOSE CALLEE IS A MEMBER OF AN IMPORTED NAMESPACE IS NEVER ARGUMENT-CHECKED — `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` does not run for it at all (round 766, isolated with three probes).** A same-file `namespace Debug` reaches the argument gate and a plain exported function imported from another file reaches it; an exported `namespace Debug` imported from another file does NOT, in both the direct-import and `export * from` barrel forms. That is **1,108 `Debug.<member>(…)` calls on the compiler profile and 1,518 on services** whose arguments no argument-position check ever sees. **So a call-argument fix that measures ZERO firings on the profiles is probably not inert — it is probably invisible**: price it against a probe that uses a plain imported function before concluding anything (rounds 764-766's arms all read 0 for this reason). It is NOT a general resolution failure — the flow-walk assert path resolves a namespace member fine.
- **A PER-KIND SPINE DISPATCH TABLE IS CLOSED *COLD* AND IS A **~1% LEVER WARM** — PRICED ROUND 867 AND NO LONGER OPEN: `s_p`, the production cost of ONE consultation that is entered and immediately declines, is **2.286 ns [2.148-2.512]**, so **`R` = 32.0 M x `s_p` = 73.2 ms [68.7-80.4] = 1.06% of a warm rebuild [1.00-1.17%], 1.47% of the `checkSpine` row** (`docs/perf/dispatch-table.md` § 9). Round 866's `[0, 352] ms` and its 187 ms / 2.7% point estimate are SUPERSEDED — that corner was 2.6x too high. Two batches, three `r`-pairs each, agreeing to 1%; measured by round 759's AMPLIFICATION (`--spineAmp N`, `BenchMain`'s `amp<N>`/`ampc<N>`), because at 2 ns the quantity is two orders of magnitude below a warm probe boundary. **Three consequences a next agent needs.** (i) **Do NOT re-derive it and do NOT re-run `--dispatchGated` to decide it**: that arm measures `G - R`, its own machinery's price MINUS the prize, one equation in two unknowns, and the machinery alone is **+715 ms = +14.4% of the warm `checkSpine` row** (round 866's `gatedfull`), enough to hide the whole thing. (ii) **The prize is REAL but one band**, i.e. below what `ab-warm.sh` (+-1.0%) can confirm — a landed table would have to be defended on `cost_gate.py` counters plus § 9's decomposition, never on a warm A/B alone. (iii) **The implementation shape is decided and it is a per-kind `Long` BITMASK, not a `when (kindId)`**: the amplifier's CONTROL arm directly measures that skeleton — a mask load plus 59 register-resident bit tests — at **13.16 ns per node = ~11.3 ms whole-compile, 15% of the prize** (net ~62-73 ms), while a dense `when (kindId)` of 138 arms x ~20 calls is far over the 8,000-bytecode cliff (round 845's -33.6% warm) and would have to be split by contiguous key range (round 802). `SpineDispatch.enterSkipMask`/`leaveSkipMask` are the masks, complement-exact and pinned. Queue item: (WARM.13b). That machinery is now priced by a third arm (`gatedfull` — the same dispatch over a table that skips NOTHING) at **+715 ms = +14.4% of the warm `checkSpine` row, 8/8 sign-consistent**, which is large enough to hide the whole prize and is why round 732's cold GATED run came out slower. Warm, the prize is bounded in **0-352 ms (0-5.1% of a rebuild)**, point estimate 187 ms = 2.7% under a stated assumption; the single number that decides it is `s_p`, the production cost of ONE rejecting consultation (`R = 32.0 M x s_p`, so the 1% floor is cleared at **2.2 ns**), and at 2-14 ns that is an order of magnitude under the warm boundary cost, so the instrument is round 759's amplification and not a timestamp pair — queued as (WARM.14). **A dense per-kind `when (kindId)` is ALSO not free: 138 arms x ~20 calls is far over the 8,000-bytecode cliff** (round 845's -33.6% warm), so any implementation must be split by contiguous key range (round 802) or use a per-kind `Long` bitmask, and must beat ~10 ns per KEPT consultation or it repeats GATED's outcome. The COLD figures below stand as the cold record: **(round 732, measured and corpus-verified) it removes 64% of the handler consultations and 4.8% of the time (883 ms upper bound of an 18.5 s spine; ~100-300 ms realistically — that discount is REASONING, never measurement, and round 847's warm 352 ms carries the same caveat).** The instrumentation (`SpineDispatch`, opt-in `--dispatchProbe`/`--dispatchGated`, behaviour-free when off) and the derived table STAY — re-derive from them, do not re-guess. **The law this adds to the caching one: a COUNT of avoidable work is not a MEASURE of it.** Round 716 sized this at 1.0-2.5 s from "IDENTIFIER is 44.5% of nodes at 2,746 ns and almost no handler wants an identifier"; in fact 22 of the 59 handlers ACT at an identifier, because the handlers keyed on PARENT edges, FRAME-owner identity (`frames.last().owner === node`) and nodeId REGISTRIES (`ctaM3NarrowThen`, `ccetRestores`, `cpaLoopVarRestores`) cannot be closed by the node's own kind AND are the expensive ones. **The spine's cost is SIX handlers holding ~61-63% of it** — WARM (round 847, and this is the list to use): `ccetSpineLeave` 876 ms, `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor` 853, `cpaSpineLeave` 617, `ctaSpineEnter` 359, `spineIanyEnterNode` 171, `spineArithEnterNode` 153; that is cta/cpa/ccet legacy-parity frame bookkeeping, i.e. a per-handler problem (queue item (SPINE.1)). **Round 732's own figures (`cpaSpineLeave` 4,366 / `ccetSpineLeave` 3,046 / `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor` 2,900 / `spineIanyEnterNode` 1,025 / `ccetSpineEnter` 920 / `ctaSpineEnter` 586, "71%") ARE STALE — on today's binary `cpaSpineLeave` is 2,060 ms cold, DOWN 53%, and is THIRD, not first** (rounds 788-795 landed levers in it). Two handlers LOOK own-kind-closed and are not: `spineArithLeaveNode` (a frame pop follows its `when (kindId)`) and `spineNpLeaveNode` (its `else` arm reaches every Expression kind) — never read only a handler's first three lines.
- **A SECTION-PROBE'S OWN BOUNDARY COST MUST BE PRICED DIFFERENTIALLY — an in-situ EMPTY-SPAN calibration over-reads by 3.5-4.4x and has now misled three consecutive rounds (733/734/735).** Back-to-back empty `at()` spans read 306 ns (unrolled) and 391 ns while the true cost is ~86-92 ns: a `repeat` loop puts a back-edge SAFEPOINT POLL inside every empty span, an unrolled sequence still attributes stop-the-world pauses, and a span across a non-inlinable call reads ~1 us (a cold transition, not a timestamp pair). **The only trustworthy figure is a DIFFERENTIAL: run the SAME code at N boundaries and at 1, divide by the extra boundary count.** `ArgSections` implements that as a MODE (`--argSectionsCoarse` keeps only the partition anchors, so a non-anchor boundary costs a static read and a not-taken branch); copy that shape rather than the empty-span loop. Per-section nanos are sound for RELATIVE attribution only, never as a production cost model.
- **When the thing being priced is SMALLER than a timestamp pair, AMPLIFY THE SIGNAL instead of shrinking the instrument (round 759, `docs/perf/claim-audit-round758.md` § 11).** A ~89 ns pair around a ~37 ns `globals[name]` probe would BE the measurement, and counters (round 736's escape) cannot price a `HashMap` get. `--globalsAmp r` brackets **r** reads of the same key under ONE pair, so `p(r) = cold + (r-1)*warm + boundary` and **two values of `r` cancel the boundary algebraically** — no calibration needed for the slope. Falsify it by ARITHMETIC, not timing: the sink must be an exact multiple of the hit count at every `r` (that is what rules out JIT elision), and the slopes from three independent pairs must agree. The in-situ empty bracket (`r < 0`) is used ONLY to split `cold` from the boundary, and its answer carries the 3.5-4.4x over-read caveat above.
- **…AND AN AMPLIFICATION LADDER READ OFF THE *WHOLE-REBUILD WALL* MUST BE ABBA-ROTATED INSIDE EACH PROCESS, BECAUSE THE FIRST INSTRUMENTED REBUILD IS THE SLOWEST DRAW AND IS WORTH UP TO 15% (round 869).** An ascending `r0,r8,r16` ladder puts that bias entirely on the `r = 0` arm and FLATTENS the slope; the same `r = 0` arm read 8,082 ms in one cycle and 6,872 ms in the next of one process. Run `r16,r8,r0,r0,r8,r16` and average per arm, and put >=8 rebuilds before the first tier. This is a 5-for-5 law in this arc; it is only invisible when every arm is measured once. **AND ROTATION INSIDE ONE PROCESS IS NOT ENOUGH AT TWO DRAWS PER ARM — RUN THE MIRROR ROTATION AS A SECOND BATCH (round 891).** `r16,…,r16` and `r0,…,r0` over the SAME binary and the same family read **53.6 and 14.1 ms/rep**, a 4x disagreement, because the leading draw's 15% lands wholly on whichever arm ran first and a 6-point fit cannot absorb it; pooling the two rotations (or discarding the leading draw from each) gives 33.9/32.6 ms with the two sub-intervals agreeing to 2.5%. A single 6-draw ladder is a batch, not a result.
- **THE REVERSE-REPLAY SCOPE MECHANISM HAS ONE HOME — `MapScopeStack<V>` / `SetScopeStack` (`ScopeStack.kt`) — AND A THIRD COPY FAMILY MUST INSTANTIATE IT, NEVER HAND-ROLL A FOURTH (round 892 retired round 891's `VarScopeStack` onto it for exactly this reason).** Two facts a new instantiation needs: the scope is a `MutableMap`/`MutableSet` **FACADE** because these maps are handed OUT to legacy helpers that write through them, and `clear()` THROWS (it cannot be recorded in O(1), and a silent one drops the ENCLOSING scopes' entries). A frame that SHARES its parent's map is "open no scope" — a flag the pop mirrors, so push and pop are paired by construction.
- **A REFUSAL'S REASONS MUST EACH NAME A COST, NOT A FIELD — round 891 refused the cta local family partly because its sibling `ambiguousNames` RESETS rather than shadows (O(size) for an undo log), and that sibling was never COPIED at all: a fn frame gives it a fresh EMPTY set, which is O(1) (round 892).** A field that lives beside the expensive ones, is installed by the same sandwich and is named in the same census row can still be outside the family; check whether it appears in the COPY accounting before letting it block a conversion. The same round's other two reasons were an un-instrumented zero and a real one — so one of three refusal grounds was a category error that cost a round.
- **A PER-SCOPE WHOLE-MAP COPY IS REPLACEABLE BY ONE LIVE MAP + AN UNDO LOG *EXACTLY* WHEN THE STACK IS STRICTLY LIFO, NO KEY IS EVER REMOVED, AND NO READER MUTATES OR RETAINS THE MAP — AND WHAT DECIDES WHETHER IT IS WORTH IT IS `writes/entries`, NEVER THE COPY COUNT (round 869, `docs/perf/warm-leaf-profile.md` §§ 9-13).** **Round 892 adds the two audit refinements that decided the hardest case: a local `val saved = <the map>` pointer swap is NOT a retention (the object identity is stable, so the restore puts the same object back — only a FIELD assignment is), and an ad-hoc `EpochMap(currentLocalTypes)` install is NOT a disqualifier (it is a DETACHED snapshot, written into and then discarded by the swap-back, and a copy that never merges back cannot observe the difference between a copy chain and a live map).** A REMOVAL is fine too, as long as it is recorded — round 869's "no key is ever removed" is about a first-write-only scheme, which this is not. The pop must replay its frame's slice in REVERSE, which is what makes a repeated write to one key correct with no per-frame "already shadowed" set. **The two annotation families (`spineOs`/`spinePd`) satisfy all three; `currentLocalTypes`, `CtaFrame.localTypes` and `CtaFrame.varTypes` DO NOT — do not widen `AnnScopeStack` to them without new pins**: a wrong scope there does not crash, it silently resolves a name to an outer binding (the `applyBodyLocalShadowing` FP class), and `varTypes` is deliberately SHARED at some frames and copied at others, so "share" becomes a replay FLAG rather than a structural fact. `AnnScopeStack.view` is the LIVE map, not a snapshot — retaining it past its frame is the one way to break it silently.
- **…AND ROUND 891 TOOK `CtaFrame.varTypes` (1,145,523 entries -> 16,182 undo records, ~33.9 ms = 0.59% warm), WHICH ADDS THE ONE MECHANISM `AnnScopeStack` DOES NOT HAVE: WHEN THE SCOPE'S MAP IS HANDED *OUT* TO HELPERS THAT WRITE INTO IT, THE SCOPE MUST BE A `MutableMap` **FACADE** (`put`/`putAll`/`remove` routed through the log), NOT A PRIVATE MAP WITH A `put` METHOD — AND THE FACADE IS THEN THE ONLY PLACE THE FAMILY'S WRITES CAN BE COUNTED.** That is not bookkeeping: the pre-891 census hooked **2 of the 3** write paths into that family (`ctaTypeParamsIntoLocals`' parameter writes and the `extraVarTypes` `putAll` were invisible), so its recorded ratio was 6.3x optimistic and nothing said so. `clear()` on such a facade must THROW — it cannot be recorded in O(1), and a silent one drops the enclosing scopes' entries with no way to restore them. **The remaining three families are REFUSED with reasons, not unstarted** (`docs/perf/cta-frame-copy-families.md` § 3): the two `Epoch*` ones are not ONE stack at all — three spine stacks plus >=12 ad-hoc `currentLocalTypes = EpochMap(currentLocalTypes)` install/restore sites whose restore is a POINTER SWAP — and `CtaFrame.localTypes`, though the bigger prize at 0.8-1.0%, still has the round-849 un-instrumented `writes 0` and a sibling (`ambiguousNames`) that RESETS rather than shadows, which an undo log expresses in O(size).
- **…AND WHEN IT IS SMALLER THAN THE RUN-TO-RUN NOISE OF THE AGGREGATE ROW IT LIVES IN, TAKE THE SPAN AT THE SITE — NEVER DIFFERENCE TWO ARMS' ROW (round 854).** `--passTiming`'s `narrowWalks` row reads **1,423 / 1,460 / 1,602 ms on ONE binary** (±6–9%), so the queue's own instruction ("difference the row between an ablated build and HEAD") would have spent two builds producing noise; one timestamp pair at the call site plus the **delta of `PassTiming.narrowWalkNanos` across it** answered in one run, ±1.7% on the ratio. Quote the SHARE of the row (instrument-internal, steady) rather than the absolute, and record BOTH a wall span and a walk-only sub-span — the tier-3 shadow bookkeeping lives inside the first and in no production run, making it an upper bound (here 435 vs 381 ms cold). Reference: `PassTiming.cmamAny*`, `docs/perf/narrowed-any-opening-price.md`.
- **TWO WALKERS THAT ARE LINE-BY-LINE MIRRORS MAKE THE SECOND ONE A PURE *REPEAT* WHENEVER THEIR ONE DIVERGENT ARM WENT UNREACHED — AND THAT, NOT "same result", IS WHAT KILLS THE CACHE-MUTATION-ORDER HAZARD (round 790).** `narrowTypeFromFlow` vs `narrowTypeFromFlowFollowLoopEntry` differ ONLY in the `FlowLoopLabel` arm, so a walk that arrived at no loop label makes the traversal its mirror would, and every resolution the mirror would drive was already driven and cached moments earlier — so skipping it mutates nothing new and round 754's order argument does not apply. To decide it at a call site use MONOTONE, never-reset counters bracketed IMMEDIATELY around the walk (a boolean reset before the call is clobbered by re-entrant walks; the fold after the call re-enters the checker and poisons a late bracket), and treat as UNKNOWN every case where the walk did not traverse: flow disabled, no reference path, no flow node, a serve from the round-664 INTER-walk memo, or any truncation. The round-736 INTRA-walk `NarrowFlowMemo` is NOT such a case — it is constructed fresh per outermost entry, so anything it serves was walked earlier in the SAME walk.
- **A SKIP'S POSITIVE CONTROL IS ITS COMPLEMENT POPULATION, WHICH COSTS NOTHING TO SHIP (round 790).** A verifier that keeps the OLD behaviour and counts violations over the skipped population (round 788's protocol) reads 0 both when the skip is sound and when the instrument is dead. Running the SAME comparison over the population the gate never skips answers that for free — there it must diverge — so no deliberately bogus baseline has to be injected. `--verifyLoopRetry` / `--verifyLoopRetryAll` are the reference pair.
- **THE FLOW-NARROWING TAIL IS EXTREME AND ITS THREE OBVIOUS CAUSES ARE ALL MEASURED FALSE (round 735, compiler profile): 394 of 70,037 walks (0.56%) cost 1,485 ms = 47% of all narrowing and 4.9% of the compile.** Before proposing anything there, know that the tail does NOT trip (`narrowWalk tripped: 0` — so the "tripped walks are never memoized, hence re-run in full" theory is dead), does NOT exhaust the 1,000,000-visit budget (630,641 arrivals across all 394; 1,601 each, max 19,515), and is NOT memoizable (`walkMiss cold=69,968` of 70,037 — essentially every launched walk is a first sighting, on top of the 40,709 the LIVE memo already serves). The residue is arithmetic: the tail runs at 2,354 ns per flow-node arrival against a 372 ns all-walk mean, i.e. 13x more arrivals AND 6.3x costlier arrivals. Diagnose with `--passTiming` (grep `narrowWalk`); full derivation in `docs/perf/argument-check-attribution.md`.
- **CACHING IN FRONT OF A RESOLUTION IS A CLOSED DIRECTION (round 716, the THIRD independent confirmation — see docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md § 0). The law: in xtsc the cacheable population is the CHEAP TAIL, so a keyed probe costs more than the work it avoids.** Priced three ways in one session: the whole context-bypassed `getTypeFromTypeNode` population is **68 ms** (31,571 outermost calls @ 2.2 µs = 0.35% of the compile), so INV.5(c) exists for a third of one percent; WIDENING its round-548 checking-file gate lifts hits 23% → 46% and measures **+28% wall** (6 interleaved pairs), and memoizing the fingerprint (builds 53,765 → 13,293) still measures **+11.9%**; pure IDENTITY keying (tsc's mapper-object shape) gets **4.1%** hits because the context maps are re-allocated per install, not reused per region. Prior confirmations: round 665 (`getTypeOfExpression` memo servable population = 30 ms, a 35× over-estimate) and round 659 (75% of a migrated pass's cost reappears in checkSpine). **tsc is not fast because it caches** — `NodeLinks.resolvedType` is a field read on the node, with no key to build. **Before proposing any new cache, measure the PRIZE first** (time the outermost calls of the population you intend to serve); every over-estimate in this codebase came from hits × mean-call-cost.
- **The INV.5(c) context fingerprint is INCOMPLETE, and the round-548 checking-file gate is what hides it (round 716).** Widening the gate produced 1,269 shape-different serves — every one a lib generic signature (`(value: T, …)` served where `(value: Declaration, …)` was correct) under an IDENTICAL fingerprint — so the substitution input is ambient state captured by none of `inferenceNamespaceStack`/`currentTypeParamScope`/`currentTypeAliasArgs`. Any future widening must close that first; the prize for doing so is the 68 ms above, so it is almost certainly not worth it. Diagnose with `--verifyMappedCache` (recomputes every served hit and splits shape-different from id-only).
- **CUTTING THE `getTypeOfExpression` RECOMPUTE FACTOR IS CLOSED (round 737, `docs/perf/type-of-expression-attribution.md`) — the ×2.76 is REAL and its mechanism is exactly the one ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK § 0.1 stage 3 named, but the whole prize is 823 ms (2.9%) and the largest actionable handler-pair merge is 166 ms (0.58%).** The factor decomposes as **2.05× cross-handler × 1.34× recursion**: 177 sites initiate typing and 45.2% of typed nodes carry >1 origin, yet per-caller factors are 1.00–1.11, so no handler re-types alone and there is nothing for one handler to fix. § 0's law in a cache-free shape: the four biggest co-occurrence pairs by COUNT are 141,388 repeats worth 71 ms (0.5 µs each) while the biggest by TIME is 2,603 repeats worth 166 ms (64 µs) — **what is redundantly recomputed is what was already cheap.** Two numbers to stop mis-quoting: `typeOfExprNanos` ("total incl. nested") DOUBLE-COUNTS a subtree once per nesting level — the true cost of all expression typing is **2,439 ms = 8.5% of checker-init** — and **74.4% of the calls are OUTERMOST**, so "the factor is just recursion" is false. Attribute with `--typeOfExprCallers` before proposing anything here.
- **SKIPPING A *CACHED* RESOLUTION MOVES THE WORK, IT DOES NOT DELETE IT — so a SKIPPABLE population is not a RECOVERABLE one (round 788, measured on the `cpaComputeArgCtxTypes` pre-gate).** 96% of its calls produced a value nothing could read, priced at 265 ms; gating them out skipped 94.3% of the calls and dropped four cost counters 2.75-5.09%, yet the timed row fell only **109 ms** and the downstream engine row rose by about as much — because the callee type it resolved is memoized, so the first later asker pays instead. **Before pricing any skip, ask which of the skipped callees CACHE their result: what is recoverable is only the part with no other consumer.** This is the dual of the closed caching direction below, and the same law: what looks free to remove is usually what was already going to be paid.
- **A WARM A/B IS MORE SENSITIVE IN ms AND *LESS* SENSITIVE IN PERCENT (round 788).** The warm rebuild is ~11.9 s against a ~27 s cold compile, so a saving whose code C2 has already compiled is a SMALLER fraction of the warm run — a change worth ~1.4% cold can read as ~0.5% warm and land inside the +-1.0% band. Quote the driver's own verdict line (round 788 got `NOISE-DOMINATED`: per-pair spread 270 ms against an 80 ms median delta, with both arm sds under 1%), never a median alone, and fall back to `cost_gate.py` counters — which is what the driver itself tells you to do.
- **POPULATION vs FREQUENCY — the arc's most expensive recurring error, now with three measured instances (round-758 audit of 57 claims, `docs/perf/claim-audit-round758.md`).** "N% of the nodes / N of the calls / N sightings" answers *how often a path is entered*, NEVER *how much work is behind it*, and here the two differ by 5–146×: `IDENTIFIER` is 44.5% of the nodes and **8.4%** of the spine's time (5.3×); `getCalleeType`'s "half its results are thrown away" is 50.6% of the calls and **8–10%** of the time (6.2×); 874 expression-bodied arrows held **six** block bodies (146×). So: **price the population before designing the fix; never multiply a count by a mean cost.** Corollary: a RESIDUAL (a time obtained by SUBTRACTION) is not a measurement of whatever you then name it — § 0's "dispatch + handler machinery, 42%" is the migrated passes' own checking work, and real dispatch is 100–300 ms.
- **BUT THE DIRECTION IS NOT ALWAYS "SMALLER", AND ASSUMING IT COST TWO ROUNDS THEIR PREDICTIONS (round 759, `docs/perf/claim-audit-round758.md` § 10).** "What you could skip cheaply is what was already cheap" **holds only when the exit predicate and the cost share a CAUSE** — a `getCalleeType` resolution is discarded *because* it failed fast. Inside `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` it is FALSE: the 72% of arguments that never reach the assignability check carry **89%** of the argument-typing time (**22,604 ns vs 7,134 ns**), because the predicate reads the PARAMETER (`isSimpleCheckableType`, foreign TPs) while the cost belongs to the ARGUMENT, and complex parameters attract complex arguments. Rounds 758 and 759 both predicted "< 40%" from the prior and were out by ~2.5×. **Ask what SELECTS the population and what DRIVES its cost before invoking the law.** The CACHE form (line above, "stop proposing caches") is unaffected. Also from that round: *paying for a value is not wasting it* — the 89% is consumed by eleven downstream blocks, so a big share is not automatically a prize.
- **The live map (round-758 re-measurement; `--passTiming`, compiler profile, ~26.5 s probe-free): `checkSpine` is 84% of checker-init and 74% of the compile, the ~400 tail passes are 10.4%, the front end is 11%.** Per-kind the concentration is the five STATEMENT-ANCHOR kinds (VARIABLE_STATEMENT / EXPRESSION_STATEMENT / RETURN_STATEMENT / IF_STATEMENT / BLOCK): **10% of the nodes, 40% of the spine** — that is a LOCATION, not a lever (the same inference from the same table produced the dead (DISPATCH.1)). The absolute figures in `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md` § 0 go stale fast: **re-measure a number before spending a round inside it** (round 755's target had halved while it sat in the queue).
- **CONSULTATION IS NOT THE EXPENSE, AND A HANDLER'S NANOS ARE ITS WORK, NOT ITS SCAFFOLDING (rounds 732+733, two independent derivations; `docs/perf/dispatch-table.md` + `docs/perf/spine-leave-attribution.md`).** (1) A per-kind handler table removes 64% of the 50.6 M handler consultations and **4.8% of the time** (883 ms upper bound, ~100–300 ms realistic) — round 716's 1.0–2.5 s came from reading "IDENTIFIER is 44.5% of nodes and almost no handler wants one", but 22 of the 59 handlers genuinely ACT there, and handlers keyed on PARENT edges / FRAME identity / nodeId REGISTRIES are both unclosable and the expensive ones. (2) One level down, in the two hottest handlers, **88.4% of the time is the migrated passes' own checking work** and the ancestor climbs that looked like the target are 176 ms of 8,195. **So: never infer a scaffolding cost from a handler-level total — attribute INSIDE first**, and price the population before building anything (every over-estimate in this codebase came from `hits × mean-call-cost`). Corollary for any probe: calibrate its timestamp-pair overhead IN SITU, never in a startup loop — a startup loop measures the cold interpreter (40,573 ns/pair vs 42 ns in situ) and silently turns every net figure negative.
- **The flow-walk memo (round 664) is LIVE and DEPENDENCY-KEYED: key = (reference nodeId, fileHash, walkKind, inputId), value = the FlowGraph instance + the Type instances bound to the reference's ROOT NAME in currentLocalTypes / narrowedDeclaredTypes; it serves only while all three match.** Three invariants a change must preserve: a walk that TRIPPED (`flowDepthTripped`) is NEVER stored (its result is truncated and its TS2563 side effect must keep firing; a served hit runs no walk, so it consumes no visit budget and trips can only get rarer); the value is `Any?` + an unchecked cast, sound ONLY because `walkKind` is in the key (Boolean-returning `isAssignedAtFlow` must never share a key with the Type-returning walks); and a NEW `flowWalkWithTripCheck` call site must pass a `kind`, an `inputId` folding its starting type id with its path hash, and the path's ROOT NAME — omitting any of them silently mixes distinct walks into one entry (round 662 measured that collision as 165 wrong serves).
- **There are TWO narrowing memos at DIFFERENT scopes and they fail differently — the entry above is the INTER-walk one; `NarrowFlowMemo` is the INTRA-walk one, and round 736 found its serve condition was costing 631,585 recomputed flow-node arrivals per compiler-profile compile.** Its `served(id, depth)` guards ONE thing: a deeper entry has less budget before `NARROW_MAX_DEPTH` and might truncate where the stored computation did not. That is decidable, not approximable — `depth` reaches the result through no other channel, and only NON-truncated subtrees are ever stored — so an entry carries `hi` (the max depth its own subtree reached) and also serves when `depth + (hi - storedDepth) < maxDepth`. **THE NON-OBVIOUS INVARIANT: a served hit MUST fold `depth + memo.lastHitHeight` into the caller's `narrowWalkHiDepth`** — otherwise an ancestor records the memo shortcut's height instead of a fresh recomputation's and the disjunct goes unsound under nesting. Both walker mirrors carry it; `NarrowMemoDepthTest` + `IntKeyMapTest` pin it. Corollary for future rounds: when a cache measures as "cannot reach this population", check whether a DIFFERENT cache at a different scope is the one failing.
- **The expression-memo epoch (`spineExprEpoch`) is PROBE-ONLY — it is read only by the two `--passTiming` shadow memos, so a fence change cannot alter compiler output (round 660).** Its churn is attributed: of the narrowing walks' 80 k memo misses, 57% are COLD (first sighting — unrecoverable by any fence), and of the 43% epoch-invalidated repeats 99.6% recompute IDENTICALLY, but `meanEpochDelta` is ~154–218 with 96% of blame on genuine `currentLocalTypes`/`currentFlowGraph` swaps — so **a finer per-map fence does NOT work; memo validity must be DEPENDENCY-KEYED (per reference-root-name generation)**. Fenced setters skip the bump when the value is unchanged (`if (field !== v)`) — 28% of bumps were no-op save/restore round-trips; keep that guard on any new fenced field.
- **`--workers N` WAS A RACE AND IS FIXED (round 825, mechanism at line 54); (PERF.HW.a) IS CLOSED ON 72 POST-FIX RUNS — 48 in round 825 plus round 826's 24, EVERY sorted capture a single md5 and identical to sequential.** Round 824's flicker (w2/w4 46/47, w8 46/56, against sequential 46x8) is the pre-fix behaviour and must not be quoted as current. What survives as a permanent rule: **any `--workers` correctness claim needs a DISTRIBUTION over >= 5 runs per level** — one `--listAll` capture is a coin flip (round 754 and round 824 each shipped a false green from a single draw) and `--partitionCheck` is a static model that cannot see a race by construction. The error count alone is a sufficient, nearly-free detector.
- **Parallel `--workers N` scaling on 8 cores, RE-TAKEN ON THE FIXED BINARY (round 826, n=6 rotated interleave, `docs/perf/worker-scaling-round826.md`): w2 1.305× / w4 **1.361×** / w8 1.242×, every level 6/6 sign-consistent wins — so the old 1.25× cap is refuted TWICE, and the race was DEFLATING the parallel arms, not flattering them** (paired deltas moved w2 −19.05 → −22.54%, w4 −23.84 → −27.10%; round 824's numbers were conservative). **w4 is the optimum and worker count alone is EXHAUSTED there — w8 regresses to 1.242×.** Only the seq/w4 Amdahl fit reproduces across rounds (34.0 → 35.3% divisible, floor 1.52 → 1.55×); the seq/w2 fit does NOT (36.9 → 46.7%), so round 824's "the w2 and w4 fits agree, the model is coherent through w4" is retracted — the fitted P share falls monotonically with N (46.7 → 35.3 → 22.3%), which is the signature of an N-growing overhead a 2-parameter model cannot express. **A 3-parameter `R + P/N + C·N` fit is UNIDENTIFIABLE from a 3-point ladder (per-rep R ranged 1.6–14.9 s) — do not quote one.** What did not change: each worker does a FULL re-bind and runs ALL ~318 program-wide collectors, but **that duplication is concurrent, so it costs CPU and RSS, not WALL** — cores peak at 6.63 of 8 (never saturated) while CPU goes seq 98 → w2 92 → w4 103 → w8 125 s and peak RSS 808 → 2,234 MB. Hence **(M2) step (b) "shrink R" is priced SMALL for wall time: ~1.36× → at most 1.55× in the unreachable limit, realistically ~1.45× (≈1.0–1.6 s of a 23.2 s compile); its real product would be CPU and memory.**
- **THE SEQUENTIAL SELF-COMPILE ANCHOR IS NOT STABLE ACROSS ROUNDS AT THE ~10% LEVEL, WITH IDENTICAL CODE AND AN IDENTICAL PROFILE — 23,183 (r826) / 25,299 (r823) / 26,145 (r824) / 26,518 ms (bench row, 2026-07-28) = a 12.8% spread, all four the same sequential path on the same 78-file profile.** Round 824 reconciled its seq arm with round 823's as "3.3% apart, well inside either arm's band"; the third point shows that was luck. **Only WITHIN-round paired deltas are quotable** — never compare an absolute ms figure to another round's.
- **A "SINGLE-THREADED" xtsc RUN CONSUMES ~4.17 OF THIS BOX'S 8 CORES (round 824; it was ~3.15 of 4 on the old box, round 740) — AND THAT TAX IS NOT A CONSTANT: IT SCALES WITH `nproc`, BECAUSE `CICompilerCountPerCPU` IS TRUE BY DEFAULT.** Doubling the cores took `CICompilerCount` 3 → 4 and `ParallelGCThreads` 4 → 8, and the JIT's CPU from ~21.7 s to ~43.8 s (`-XX:CICompilerCount=2` drops the run 4.20 → 2.55 cores). **So never express a core budget as "N cores NET of a fixed ~3.2"** — round 740's ">= 12 cores" unpark condition did exactly that and was miscalibrated in the conservative direction (the answer arrived at 8; free cores went 0.85 → 3.83, not → 4.85). Two consequences that did NOT change: **never run two compiles/benchmarks concurrently** (one copy already occupies half the box), and **this is not a single-thread lever** — self time is flat across baseline / JIT-starved / GC-starved (round 618's "JVM flag hunting is DEAD" stands; the JIT threads occupy cores a WORKER would want, they do not steal from the compile thread).
- **Migrating a tail pass onto the check spine recovered only 25% of its cost when measured WITHOUT a dispatch table (round 659) — that verdict is SUPERSEDED as MIS-ORDERED, not wrong (round 716).** The measurement stands (35 passes: deleted rows summed 3,146 ms, checkSpine grew +2,358 ms; interleaved A/B +0.24%), but its cause was that "one walk" still consults every handler at every node, so migration converted "N walks over the tree" into "N consultations per node". With (DISPATCH.1)'s per-kind table a migrated pass costs only its own kinds. **Re-measure one migrated pass after DISPATCH.1 before judging the arc again** — do NOT cite round 659 as a reason not to migrate.
- **PRICE A TIMING PROBE'S OWN BOUNDARY *DIFFERENTIALLY* — an empty back-to-back span over-reads by 3–10× (round 734, same safepoint bias class as the JFR entry below).** An empty `t()`/`close()` span read **922 ns** across a method call, **360 ns** inside a `repeat(n)` loop (whose back-edge safepoint poll attracts stop-the-world attribution) and **306 ns** unrolled — against a true **86 ns**, obtained by measuring the SAME code twice in one run, as N sections and as 1 span, and dividing the difference by the N−1 extra boundaries. Cross-check with round 733's technique (`--passTiming` with vs without the probe flag: the whole probe moved `checkSpine` by +29 ms). The tell of an over-read boundary is small sections going NEGATIVE in the `net` column.
- **…AND WHEN THE PARTITION IS *LAYERED*, THE ON-MINUS-COARSE DIFFERENTIAL MUST DIVIDE THE ***OUTERMOST*** LEVEL'S Δms BY ***ALL*** EXTRA BOUNDARIES — summing every level's Δ over every level's own Δboundaries OVERSTATES the price by 1.7–2.2× (215 vs 97 ns, round 850), because a deep boundary executes inside every level above it and is charged once per level.** `CtaSections`' B/C/D/E open inside level A's rows and `CpaSections` is a chain P ⊃ Q ⊃ R, so this bites all three intra-handler probes; the check that the answer is right is that the ON and COARSE arms — boundary counts 2.5× apart — then agree on the INNER levels too (0–5%), which the outermost-level fit does not force. Warm the boundary is **97–202 ns** against **501 ns cold**, so a cold table's `net` column is over-subtracted 2.5–5× and must be recomputed from raw nanos, never inherited.
- **A `*coarse` SECTION TABLE PRINTS `mode: ON` (`CpaSections`: `mode=OFF`) — `BenchMain` clears each probe's `mode` right after the instrumented rebuild and BEFORE dumping, so `report()`'s label is always wrong for a COARSE arm (round 850).** The data is unaffected; identify the arm by its PAYLOAD — a COARSE run prints only the anchor/wrapper rows and carries ~2.5× fewer boundaries — never by the label.
- **THE `--passTiming` PROBE LANDS ~100% INSIDE `checkSpine` AND NOWHERE ELSE, SO A `full`-TIER TABLE OVERSTATES THAT ONE ROW AND NOTHING ELSE — USE `--passTimingRows`, WHICH KEEPS THE WHOLE PER-PASS TABLE FOR +0.25% COLD AND 0.0% WARM (round 846, `docs/perf/warm-jvm-attribution.md` § 10).** Measured both regimes: of the probe's ~2.6 s, **99.5-99.8% is inside checker-init and 101-109% inside `checkSpine`**, while the other 416 pass rows and the front end are probe-FREE (top-20 `full`/`rows` ratios 0.63-1.35, no sign). Two consequences a reader of any older table must carry: **a warm-up RATIO taken from a `full` table is compressed for the row the probe lands on** — `checkSpine` really warms **3.46x** and the tail passes **2.59x**, where § 4 reads 2.24x vs 2.38x — and **the probe's OWN cost warms up** (first instrumented rebuild in a process 3,457 ms, second 1,856 ms, sign-consistent in two processes), so pricing it needs >=2 instrumented rebuilds per process, never one. `BenchMain <proj> <warmup> <iters> rows,spine,full,rows,spine,full` is the differential; the tier flags default to the pre-846 behaviour, so `cost_gate.py` and every counter pin are unchanged, and a reduced tier's zeros come with a header saying they are ABSENT measurements.
- **`jfr print` SILENTLY TRUNCATES EVERY STACK TO ITS TOP 5 FRAMES, AND `scripts/aggregate_jfr.py` DOES NOT PASS `--stack-depth` — so its "INCLUSIVE time by method" table is, by default, the inclusive time *within five frames of the leaf* (round 868).** The tell is arithmetic that cannot be reconciled with this arc's own attribution (`checkSpine` read **15.6%** inclusive against the known ~74%); the printed stack merely ends in `...`. Pass `--stack-depth 512` and assert `max(len(stack))` is below the cap before believing any inclusive number or caller attribution. LEAF (frame-0) numbers are unaffected — but they are **not stable across processes**: `HashMap.getNode` reads 9.66% in one JVM and 3.70% in another on the SAME binary because C2 inlined it in the second, so attribute stdlib work to its nearest non-stdlib CALLER (that replicates to a few tenths of a percent) and filter samples to the compile thread (`xtsc-deep-stack`), since ~3% belong to the JFR recorder and the crawl's coroutine workers.
- **A `Checker.kt` `LineNumberTable` line needs +65,536 OR +131,072 (the file is 178,013 lines and the field is u2), and INLINED stdlib appears at SYNTHETIC lines PAST EOF — so a per-line JFR/`javap` attribution takes three guesses to decode and one of them is unfalsifiable (round 868).** The largest single line bucket in that round's raw data was a synthetic one. Use line data to SHAPE a hypothesis, never to report a number, and assert that the corrected span actually contains the function (as `scripts/tisp_split_analyze.py --bytes` does).
- A JFR leaf-frame self-% is NOT a wall-clock price (round 623): `computeLineStarts` showed 5.3% of samples; eliminating it (lazy) measured −0.3% = neutral — counted-loop safepoint bias inflates tight-loop self samples, and parallel-crawl worker savings don't move the serial-dominated wall. Price any JFR self entry by interleaved A/B before acting on it (the round-618 JFR shares this bias class).
- JVM GC/heap flag hunting on the self-compile: DEAD (round 618) — G1 vs ParallelGC vs +Xms4g vs +UseCompactObjectHeaders (JDK 25, 3 interleaved rotations) all land within the drift band (medians 30.2–30.8 s); the workload is not GC-bound at -Xmx4g. Perf levers are structural (docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md), not flags.
- Deleting the legacy pass tail for wall time: DEAD as a lever (rounds 619/620) — the corpus emission census (PassLab censusMode) measured 417/440 tail passes emitting, and the round-620 HONEST disable experiment showed 20 of the 23 census-silent passes are ALSO corpus-pinned (26 test failures) — only 3 pure adders were deletable. Tail wall recovery is spine MIGRATION (M0.4). **Census-silence is deletion evidence ONLY for pure `diagnostics.add` passes: `censusByPass` records net-POSITIVE deltas (`c1 > c0`), so wipe-and-pin walkers (`removeAll` + `pinDiag`, net 0), rewriters (`diagnostics[i] = copy`), retractors (net < 0), and collectors (side-set producers) are census-silent while fully load-bearing.** Round 619's "23 deletion-ready" verdict was a FALSE GREEN manufactured by Inv0PassTimingTest's cleanup assigning `PassTiming.disabledPasses = emptySet()` — a test touching fork-global PassLab state (`disabledPasses`/`censusMode`) must SAVE-AND-RESTORE, never assign the default back (the wipe re-enables the lab's disables for every alphabetically-later test class = the whole generated corpus). Also: `build/pass-lab.txt` is NOT a Gradle task input — a lab-file change alone does not invalidate an up-to-date `jvmTest`; force `--rerun` for any lab experiment.
- Deleting the `checkGeneric*` pin-walker family as "superseded by the open generic gate": DEAD — 15/16 are still load-bearing (round 600 sweep: 21 corpus failures; their shapes are inference/constraint semantics the structural relation cannot decide). Only checkGenericFnTypeBipartition was deletable (its noStrictGenericChecks pin family is tsgo-skipped). Re-attempt a per-walker deletion only after generic INFERENCE (M3.1) lands, one walker at a time, suite-gated.

- A LIVE narrowing-walk fold at the two Type-returning entries (epoch-fenced + declaredType-identity + confirm-once): DEAD — gate-green but NOISE in the interleaved A/B (round 599; the confirm-once tax + 80k/111k fresh-epoch misses cap the served fraction); savableNanos measured 1.4s but unrealizable under this policy. Revive with INV.5 canonical types.
- A LIVE per-node getTypeOfExpression memo (whitelisted instance-stable results + confirm-once + the round-595 epoch): DEAD — gate-green but 1-3% SLOWER measured interleaved (round 596); **MECHANISM MEASURED round 665: the whole servable population is worth 30 ms** (71,310 outermost served calls at ~0.42 µs each) because the servable calls are the CHEAP TAIL — trivial identifiers/literals whose resolution is already cached — while the expensive calls (fresh minting, narrowing, relation work) are precisely the non-instance-stable ones the whitelist excludes. Any future estimate of a memo's value must price the SERVABLE population, never hits × a mean call cost (that error inflated this by 35×); the servable hits are the cheap calls, the expensive recompute is the non-memoizable fresh-minting/narrowing kinds, and the per-mutation epoch bookkeeping exceeds the savings. The epoch infrastructure + the --passTiming shadow memo REMAIN for future policy evaluation.
- Global variance analysis wired into the relation engine: DEAD — ~263 regressions (round 336); strictFunctionTypes variance lives in dedicated walkers. Revisit only as part of a full M3.1 relation redesign.
- A general PropertyAccess-receiver TS2339 fallback via resolved interface members (B153): NOT viable — merged/cross-file interface member resolution is incomplete; only the class-AST-chain path + two tightly-gated exceptions (B527/B330) work.
- The parser strips a tuple element's `?` and label from the element NODE (no `NamedTupleMember`/`OptionalType` is produced — `[number?]`'s `?` is consumed by parseType's trailing-`?` recovery), but SINCE round 452 records per-element optionality on `TupleType.elementOptional` (named `?` in the labeled branch; unnamed `?` bridged via the `tupleElementConsumedOptionalMarker` flag). `getTupleType` reads it → marks optional members in `optionalTupleMemberIds` (consulted by `isOptionalProperty`, since tuple member symbols carry no declaration) AND sets an optional-containing tuple's `length` to the union `minLength..maxLength` (so `[] : [a?, b?]` relates). Element optionality is thus available in the relation now; there is still NO rest/minLength-beyond-length modeling (see the getTupleType-collapse gotcha). `tupleHasOptionalElement` (source-text scan) remains for the boolean "has any optional" question. **Round 459: AST-based tuple checks reach ASSIGNMENT targets via `currentLocalDeclTypeNodes`** (annotation NODES for function-body locals, populated first-decl-wins alongside `currentLocalTypes` in checkVarDeclAssignability, saved/restored in checkFunctionBody) — `checkAssignmentExpression` feeds it (or the globals annotation) to `arrayLiteralSatisfiesTupleTarget` for an array-literal RHS; a NEW body-local AST-shape check should read the node from this map, not re-derive it.
- **A MAPPED-TYPE member's `readonly`/optional state is NOT on its declaration — a homomorphic member CARRIES ITS SOURCE PROPERTY'S declaration (for the "declared here" related info), so `isOptionalProperty`/readonly readers see the SOURCE's modifiers.** All four mapped modifiers therefore need their own recording at the materializer (`getTypeFromMappedTypeNode`): `readonly` → `mappedReadonlyMemberIds`, `-readonly` → `mappedMutableMemberIds`, `-?` → `mappedRequiredMemberIds`, `?` → the `SymbolFlags.MappedOptional` BIT (round 728 — a bit, not a set, because the arm consulting it is `isOptionalProperty`'s declared-REQUIRED branch, which every property in the program reaches).
