A new modifier or a new consumer that forgets this reads the source's state and is silently inverted; `Partial<T>`/`Required<T>` were each broken this way for months, in opposite directions.
- **An enum-MEMBER type annotation (`SK.A`) resolves to `anyType`, and `TypeFlags.EnumLiteral` is SET NOWHERE (round 740, (REL.1)).** `getTypeFromTypeReference` reduces `SK.A` to the BARE name `"A"`, resolves it to a `SymbolFlags.EnumMember` symbol, and `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker` has no branch for that flag → `else -> anyType`. So `SK.A` and `SK.B` are the SAME `Type` instance and every enum-member comparison is vacuously true both ways — the whole `enumMemberKeysOfTypeNode` / `kindDomain*` / `discriminantPropAnnotation` AST key-space ecology exists to answer around it. **Do NOT build on `TypeFlags.EnumLiteral`**: nothing sets it, so all ~11 read sites (including the `EnumLiteral → Enum` widening rule at Checker.kt:143100) are dead code that a grep makes look live.
- **`TypeFlags.Primitive` OMITS EVERY *LITERAL* BIT (no StringLiteral/NumberLiteral/BooleanLiteral/BigIntLiteral/EnumLiteral/UniqueESSymbol), SO `!hasAny(Primitive …)` IS NOT THE COMPLEMENT OF `hasAny(Object)` AND A RULE WRITTEN AS THE NEGATION SILENTLY ACCEPTS LITERALS, BARE TYPE PARAMS, `unknown` AND `keyof`/indexed-access TYPES (round 834).** That was the `object`/NonPrimitive relation leg for its whole life; write the POSITIVE test tsc writes (`s & Object && t & NonPrimitive`) and remember a `false` from `isSimpleTypeRelatedTo` is not a rejection — it falls through to the structural engine, which is where a `Type.TypeParam` source must be decided by its CONSTRAINT.
- **`isSimpleCheckableType` IS NOT A FREE PREDICATE TO WIDEN — IT HAS ~45 CONSUMERS AND SEVERAL ARE A DEDICATED WALKER'S ENTIRE FP FIREWALL, so widening it makes those walkers CO-EMIT with the general path (round 834).** Add a sibling predicate (`isArgCheckableType`) and name the call sites instead; the same round's `checkSymbolAsWeakTypeArg` shows the other direction — a walker KDoc claiming "purely ADDITIVE **because** the general check skips X" is a claim that expires the moment X is opened, so grep for such wording before opening one.
- **A TYPE'S `baseTypes` ARE WRITTEN IN ITS OWN TYPE-PARAMETER SCOPE, so any walk that descends from a `Type.Reference` to `ref.target.baseTypes` MUST first instantiate them through `createTypeMapper(target.typeParameters, ref.resolvedTypeArguments)` (round 761).** `interface Fwd<T> extends Box<T>` stores `Box<T>` — Fwd's OWN `T` — so an unmapped descent answers `Box<T>` and the property resolves to a bare type PARAMETER. `resolveGenericPropertyTypeWorker` composes correctly; `findInheritedBaseRef` did not. Mapping mints fresh type ids, so an id-keyed `visited` set no longer bounds a self-expanding circular base — keep a step cap.
- **`getTypeOfSymbol` PERSISTS INTO `symbolTypes` ONLY WHEN THE *CALLER'S* INSTANTIATION CONTEXT IS EMPTY (`symbolTypeContextIsEmpty`, round 778) — the same three dimensions `getTypeFromTypeNodeCore`'s `cacheable` gate has always used.** Without it a member typed by a type parameter froze globally as `T` or as `any` depending on which file touched it first, i.e. on program order — invisible in every output diff until the crawl order changes. A context the worker installs FOR ITSELF (namespace push, own type params) is a function of the symbol and stays cacheable, so the gate must be read at ENTRY. **Any NEW global, id-keyed checker cache written during a resolution that consults ambient state needs the same gate**, or it is a program-order dependency with no diff to find it by.
- **A DELIBERATE EXCLUSION IS A DEBT WITH A NAMED CREDITOR — RE-TEST IT WHEN THE CREDITOR IS PAID (round 783).** (WIDEN.1)(a) kept widening a `const`'s enum member solely because not doing so exposed one profile line; that line had an unrelated cause, and once it was closed the exclusion cost `scanner.ts:905` on all eight profiles. Any comment of the form "not done because it costs X" is a lead, not a verdict — grep the round notes for X before inheriting it.
- **AN OBJECT-LITERAL VALUE IN A TERNARY BRANCH HAS NO CONTEXTUAL TYPE (`ctxObj` is null), SO THE ROUND-438/462/468 FLOW-NARROW ACCEPTANCE CAN NEVER FIRE THERE (round 783).** The narrow IS computed and IS correct; it is then discarded because the acceptance test needs a contextual property type. A rule that must survive that position needs an acceptance that is monotone by CONSTRUCTION (e.g. `enumTargetsAreOwnMembers` bounding the value to a sub-union of what it replaces), not by relation test.
- **`extractNullNarrowing` — the only writer of an if-condition's narrowing into `currentLocalTypes` — has nullish / `typeof` / truthiness / (since round 785) type-guard-CALL arms, and its verdict for the call arm is `narrowByCallPredicate`'s.** It feeds the THEN-branch only, so the else branch still gets no subtractive narrow. Corollary that did NOT change: `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess` flow-narrows a receiver only when its raw type is ALREADY a `Type.Union`, so a guard's narrow-DOWN (`Node` -> `Modifier`) still never reaches a property read through that path.
- **AN INTERFACE MEMBER'S SYMBOL IS SHARED BY EVERY TYPE THAT INHERITS IT, AND `symbolTypes[sym.id]` FOR A TYPE-PARAMETER-TYPED MEMBER IS GLOBALLY `any` (round 761) — `getIndexedAccessType` read it that way until round 783, so every `Token<K.X>["p"]` answered `any` and ONE `any` constituent makes the whole union arm answer `any`.** `Box`/`Fwd`/`S2` all hold ONE `v` symbol whose cached type is `any` (first resolved with no TP in scope), which makes `resolveReferenceMembers`' instantiation loop a NO-OP for such members (`instantiated === propType` → the original symbol is reused). So a member's real type comes from `resolveGenericPropertyType`, never from the member table — do NOT reason about generic member types from `type.members`/`symbolTypes`, and do not "fix" substitution there.
- **AN UNCONSTRAINED TYPE PARAMETER AND `any` ARE INDISTINGUISHABLE TO A READ PROBE — use a WRITE or an ARGUMENT probe (round 761).** `const s: string = x.v` is silent for both, which is how a whole queue item came to say "resolves to `any`" about a value that was really the unsubstituted `T`. `x.v = "s"` prints the actual target type; renaming each declaration's parameter (`TBOX`/`TFWD`) then names WHICH one survived, i.e. which hop failed.
- JSDoc `@typedef`/`@property` are NEVER bound (raw-source scans only) — any JSDoc-type-position resolver must special-case them or it FPs TS2304 on valid typedef references.
- **ENUM-MEMBER TYPES DO NOT DISCRIMINATE IN THE RELATION (measured round 729; queued as (REL.1)): `declare enum SK { A, B }; const k: SK.A = SK.B` emits NOTHING, so two interfaces differing ONLY in `readonly kind: SK.X` are MUTUALLY ASSIGNABLE.** Any new rule that FILTERS a union by assignability (a conditional type, an `Exclude`-shaped materializer, a narrowing subtractor) will therefore silently drop sibling AST node types — round 729's `Exclude<PropertyName, PrivateIdentifier>` lost `Identifier` and invented two FPs at factory/utilities.ts. Until (REL.1) lands, veto such a filter with the round-472 `.kind` DOMAIN readers (`kindDomainKeysExceed` / `kindDomainProvesNotSubtype`), never with the structural verdict alone.
- The weak-type rule (TS2559/TS2560) is dedicated walkers, NOT the relation engine — a weak target passes our relation vacuously (all props optional), so the "must share a property" check is an after-the-relation add-on with its own FP firewall.
- **Under `@useRealLibs` a utility type resolves to its ALIAS BODY, so its resolved SHAPE is not what the name suggests: `NonNullable<T>` is `T & {}` — a `Type.Intersection`, NOT a nullish-stripped type (round 725).** Any per-site rule keyed on `Type.TypeParam`/`Type.Union` silently misses every `NonNullable<…>` argument. Invisible on the default embedded-lib path, which declares NO utility types at all (the name degrades to `any`, and `any` is silent) — so a repro for this family MUST set `@useRealLibs`. An `{}` constituent is tsc's non-nullish marker; treat it as "strips null/undefined/void", never as an empty constraint to ignore.

- **A TARGET TYPE'S MEMBER TABLE IS LAZY, SO ANY NEW READER OF `collectTargetPropertyNames` / `type.members` MUST `resolveStructuredTypeMembers(target)` FIRST — OTHERWISE ITS VERDICT DEPENDS ON WHETHER AN EARLIER LINE IN THE FILE HAPPENED TO RESOLVE THAT TYPE (round 833).** The helper answers "bail" (null `members`) rather than failing, so the excess-property check silently did nothing at the first `satisfies I1` in a file and worked at the second. **A corpus baseline can MASK this exactly** — `typeSatisfaction.ts`'s excess literal is preceded by another `satisfies I1` that resolves the type on the way through — so only a hand-written pin whose fixture has ONE use of the target can see it.
- **`getPropertyOfType`'s UNION arm ANSWERS AN ASSIGNABILITY QUESTION ("the property exists only if EVERY constituent has it") AND THEN RETURNS ONE CONSTITUENT'S SYMBOL — SO IT IS THE WRONG HELPER FOR EVERY "WHERE/WHAT IS THIS MEMBER" QUESTION, AND IT FAILS SILENTLY IN BOTH DIRECTIONS (round 916).** A member present on one constituent only answers **null** (a real declaration, reported as absent), and one present on all N answers **the first** (N−1 declarations dropped). A consumer that needs the member ITSELF — go-to-definition, completions, any per-constituent display — must collect across `Type.Union`/`Type.Intersection` itself, as `typeCaptureCollectMembers` does; `getPropertyAcrossType` is intersection-only and has the same first-wins tail. Reaching a PRIMITIVE's or a bare type parameter's members is `getApparentType` (the wrapper interface / the constraint), and a NAMESPACE's, MODULE's or **ENUM's** members are on no type at all — they live in `Symbol.exports`, so a type-only member lookup is silent for all three.

### Checker walker gotchas

- **A `getTypeOfSymbol` CALL MADE IN ORDER TO *WARM* A CACHE IS A NO-OP WHEREVER THE AMBIENT INSTANTIATION CONTEXT IS NON-EMPTY — ROUND 778's WRITE GATE REFUSES IT — SO EVERY "force it, then let a RAW `symbolTypes[id]` reader read it back" PATTERN SILENTLY DOES NOTHING INSIDE A `namespace` BODY, A TYPE-PARAM SCOPE OR AN ALIAS-ARG INSTALL (2026-08-22, (INC.6); it cost (INC.5) four capture divergences and its own pin was green because that fixture sits at FILE level).** The resolution SUCCEEDS and is thrown away, and `typeToString`'s anonymous-object branch then prints `any` — no diagnostic, no counter, nothing but a hover moves. **A symbol a materializer MINTS must therefore have its type written AT MINT TIME, ungated** (`resolveReferenceMembers`' idiom, now also `materializeModifierUtility`'s): that is sound where the gated write is not, because the id is reachable only from the one type just built, so the frozen value can never be read as another symbol's.
- **A PROPERTY'S INFERRED TYPE IS COMPUTED AT *TWO* INDEPENDENT SITES — `getTypeOfVariableOrProperty`'s `PropertyDeclaration` arm AND `getTypeOfMemberDecl` — SO A RULE LANDED AT ONE PASSES ITS OWN REPRO AND ANSWERS THE OLD TYPE AT THE OTHER (round WIDEN.1(b)).** Both now route through `propertyInitializerLiteralType`; keep any new per-property inference rule behind ONE helper both call.
- **…AND A KEPT LITERAL MUST BE WIDENED BACK AT ITS ASSIGNMENT TARGET, OR EVERY LEGAL WRITE CO-EMITS A SPURIOUS TS2322 BESIDE THE REAL TS2540/TS2588** — `readonly s = 'X'` then rejects even `g.s = 'X'`. `widen1ImmutableLiteralTypeIds` is the registry (written at the MINT, since `literalTypeOfExpression` returns a fresh instance per call) and `checkPropertyAccessAssignment` + `checkAssignmentExpression` are its two consult sites.

- **A NAME-EXTRACTION FAMILY HAS >= 5 INDEPENDENT COPIES OF THE SAME `when` (archive: B451), AND WIDENING *ONE* OF THEM IS HALF A FIX WHOSE TELL IS **TWO CONTRADICTORY DIAGNOSTICS AT ONE POSITION**, NOT A MISSING ONE (round 933).** After `computedLiteralKey` learned a backtick-quoted computed key, ``class C { [`cp`]: number }`` resolved for TS2322 and simultaneously FP'd TS2339 from `classMemberNameText`, which still re-spelled the pre-widening `when`; the second copy now DELEGATES. Grep the family before widening any member-name extraction, and read a co-emitted pair at one span as "one site was widened", never as two bugs.
- **THE EIGHT tsc PROFILES CONTAIN *ZERO* BACKTICK-QUOTED COMPUTED MEMBER KEYS (measured round 933 — the only ``[`…`]`` matches in all eight `src` trees are ARRAY literals), SO A `cost_gate.py` `+0.00%` AND AN `added=0 removed=0` GRID ARE THE *EXPECTED* ANSWERS FOR ANY CHANGE IN THAT SPELLING, NOT EVIDENCE OF ONE.** Three real false positives lived behind that blind spot with every profile instrument and all ~13k corpus baselines green; the corpus plus a hand-written pin is the only gate that sees it. Do the corpus grep before reading a green profile arm as coverage.

- **A NEGATIVE NARROWING BRANCH THAT DECIDES "THE SUBJECT IS THE TARGET, SO THE FALSE BRANCH IS IMPOSSIBLE" MUST NOT USE THE *ASSIGNABLE* RELATION — `any` is assignable to everything, so it washed to `never` and the flow join of (`Foo` from then, `never` from else) then produced `Foo` at the NEXT statement (round 838, two sites).** It presents as a narrow LEAKING out of its `if` and ACCUMULATING across sibling guards (`Error | Function` after two), and round 837 diagnosed it as exactly that — **because nothing in any output ever names `never`**: it is assignable to every probe target, silent at a property access, and an argument position reads the declared type, so a `never` narrow is UNOBSERVABLE in this compiler and no pin can be written for one. tsc survives it structurally (its assumeFalse filter goes through `isTypeSubsetOf`, plain identity for a non-union candidate). The guard is `anyNegativeBranchSurvives` (`TypeFlags.Any` — `any` plus the `error`/`unresolved` sentinels); a NEW negative-branch narrower needs it too.
- **AN `instanceof` RHS IS A *VALUE*, AND EVERY AMBIENT CONSTRUCTOR IN THE LIB IS `interface X { … }` + `declare var X: XConstructor` — so a `SymbolFlags.Class` test answers only for user-written classes (round 838).** `Error`/`Date`/`RegExp`/`Map`/`Set`/`Promise` narrowed NOTHING while a `declare class` narrowed fine, and no gate saw it: the corpus, the cost gate and all eight profiles are green either way, because tsc's own sources `instanceof` their own classes. `resolveInstanceOfRhsType` now falls through to tsc's `getInstanceType` (the `prototype` property, then a lone construct signature's return). **The general trap: a "narrowing is broken" report reproduced on ONE shape must be re-run on the neighbouring shape before the cause is named** — here the reported symptom (`Error | string` un-narrowed) was real and the named cause (a missing `extractNullNarrowing` arm) was not the only thing that produces it.
- **`SymbolFlags.Module` IS THE *UNION* OF `ValueModule` AND `NamespaceModule`, AND tsc GIVES THE TWO DIFFERENT DIAGNOSTICS AT THE SAME SYNTAX — so a rule that reads `Module` conflates them and the corpus notices (round 837).** Assigning to an INSTANTIATED namespace is TS2631 (`Cannot assign to 'M' because it is a namespace.`); assigning to a non-instantiated/type-only one is TS2708 (`Cannot use namespace 'M' as a value.`), because it has no value meaning for the write to be judged against. `Binder.kt` sets the bit from `ModuleInstanceState`. The same split is why one shape can look like a message-form divergence and be a genuine two-diagnostic distinction — check `ValueModule` before "correcting" either code.

- **A DECLARATION HEAD IS A *TOKEN* IN `VariableDeclarationList.flags`, AND THE ~98 SITES THAT READ IT ALL TEST `== ConstKeyword` / `!= VarKeyword` — SO ADDING A HEAD (`using` / `await using`, round 948) IS A FALSE NEGATIVE AT EVERY ONE OF THEM AND NEVER A FALSE POSITIVE.** That asymmetry is what makes a new statement form additive; the binder needs nothing either (`bindVariableStatement`'s `isVar` test already reads any non-`var` head as block-scoped). **BUT THE DUPLICATE-IDENTIFIER CLUSTER HAS *TWO* INDEPENDENT COLLECTORS that both map those flags to a kind STRING** (`checkDuplicateDeclarations`' `varKind`, and the `IdDecl` builder ~178k lines down), so teaching one leaves the other's rules — `hasBlockScoped`, `blockScopedDecls`, `firstIsBlockScoped` — silently unreachable: round 948 wrote all three, measured them dead, and reverted rather than ship an unpinned mark.
- **`spineIanyEnterNode`'s TWO round-798 gates are COUPLED — skipping a call's `isCalleeResolvable` is sound ONLY because every argument edge that could read the resulting `kind = 1` `typed` flag is itself skipped (the "all arguments childless" condition), and the call's FRAME must still be pushed either way because it shadows an enclosing `kind = 0` state for the CALLEE subtree (an IIFE reads that).** Widening either gate alone, or adding a reader of a `kind = 1` `SpineIanyCtx` anywhere, silently breaks the other; `IanyGateTest` + `--ianyGateOff` (the pre-798 path in the same binary) are how you check.

- **A FRAME PUSHED AT A *CHILDLESS* NODE'S PARENT EDGE IS POPPED AT THAT NODE'S OWN LEAVE WITH NO NODE ENTERED IN BETWEEN — so neither `spineIanyCtx` NOR the `implicitAnyScopes` stack can be read there, and the only thing that survives such an edge is any RESOLUTION the arm performed (rounds 798/799).** That, not "a scope push is a mutation", is why removing round 798's scope-push exclusion is invisible to BOTH the 8-profile grid and all 13,449 corpus baselines; it is kept solely because the `ArrowFunction` arm also calls `contextualSigReturnTypeForCtx`, whose interning is first-touch-ORDER sensitive (round-754/776/778 class) for a 1 ms row. Any future "this per-node state is unobservable" argument must ask what runs INSIDE the node's span, never whether the write looks like a mutation.
- **A PRE-GATE THAT MIRRORS AN ARM LIST MUST BE KEPT IN EXACT CORRESPONDENCE WITH IT — `spineIanyEdgeHasArm` ↔ `spineIanyEdgeEnter`'s 19 `is` arms (round 799), the same trap as `ccetPrologueMayFire`.** Adding an arm without adding its `NodeKind` silently makes the arm unreachable, and the tell is a corpus baseline losing a diagnostic with no change anywhere near the arm; `IanyArmGateTest`'s equivalence pin (both settings of `IanySections.armGateOff`) is what catches it.
- **A NEW dedicated walker at the top of `checkSingleCallExpressionTypesCore` MUST extend `ccetPrologueMayFire` or it silently never runs (round 793).** That prologue is pre-gated by a syntactic classification of the CALLEE — `super`, a property access named `reduce`/`filter`/`transform`/`create`, a property access with a string-literal argument (B216's keys), or a file with CJS default-as-namespace shapes — and it refuses 98% of all call expressions. The gate is a superset of each walker's own first gate; a new walker with a different key is invisible to it, and the tell is a corpus baseline losing a diagnostic with no code change near the walker.


- **A RULE WHOSE PREMISE IS "THE PARAMETER REQUIRES THIS NAMED TYPE" MUST NOT FIRE WHEN THE PARAMETER'S *DECLARED* TYPE NODE IS ONE OF THE SIGNATURE'S OWN TYPE PARAMETERS (round 779) — the concrete type it now holds was inferred BY US from the very argument being judged, so the requirement is self-referential and a bare type parameter requires nothing.** The test must read the AST (`paramDeclaredTypeIsOwnTypeParam`): an instantiated `Signature` carries `typeParameters = null` and its parameter symbols hold the SUBSTITUTED type, so nothing on the Type side can see that the annotation ever was a `T` — only `valueDeclaration`, which `instantiateSignature` copies through, survives.
- **A HAND-WRITTEN REDUCTION THAT STAYS SILENT IS NOT EVIDENCE THE SHAPE IS WRONG: the argument loop `continue`s for any NON-simple-checkable PARAMETER unless the argument is primitive, so `f(x: SomeInterface)` called with `SomeInterface | undefined` emits nothing (round 779) while the same call FPs on the profiles through a dedicated walker.** Nor does the message discriminate — the general TS2345 path builds the same `Type 'undefined' is not assignable to type 'X'.` elaboration a nullish-specific walker does. Name the emitter first with the `Diagnostic`-constructor stack hook below.
- **A REFERENCE'S IDENTITY HERE IS A PATH *STRING*, WHERE tsc's `isMatchingReference` COMPARES *SYMBOLS* — SO `x.a` AND `x["a"]` WERE TWO DIFFERENT REFERENCES AND EVERY DISCRIMINANT READER WAS WRITTEN AGAINST THE DOTTED SPELLING ALONE (round 942, `docs/pristine-divergences.md` § 3.4).** `getReferencePath` now NORMALISES an identifier-spellable string index onto the dotted segment (`x["sub"]` -> `x.sub`) while a non-spellable one (`"dash-ok"`, `0`, `"0"`) keeps round 461's `x[seg]` encoding — so the two encodings still cannot collide, and `flowPathRoot`/`pathPrefixOf` already split on `[`. **Two consequences for anything new: a reader that matches `startsWith("$name.")` is now ALSO the bracket spelling's reader (`singleLevelDiscriminantSegment` is the shared one), and a NON-ASCII identifier index (`x["é"]`) is deliberately NOT normalised, so that one spelling pair still does not match.**

- **AND THE SYMMETRIC-LOOKING LINE IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY LOAD-BEARING: `getTypeOfElementAccess` NARROWS ITS *RECEIVER* AND DELIBERATELY NOT ITS OWN *RESULT*, BECAUSE THE RESULT HALF WAS WRITTEN, ABLATED AND MEASURED INERT (round 942 — 0 of 21 pins red, and no probe could be built where it fires).** A `typeof` guard does not reach an element-access reference at all, so `if (typeof h[0] === "string") { … h[0] }` still reports the declared union with the line in place. Adding the 17.34d twin "for symmetry" puts a flow walk on a hot path that nothing can observe — the round-887 shape. Re-open it only WITH an input where it fires.

- **NARROWABILITY IS DECIDED PER CONSUMER, NOT BY THE MACHINERY — every flow-reading call site carries its own NODE-KIND gate, so a kind the whole narrowing stack supports can still read the declared type everywhere (round 780).** An `ElementAccessExpression` with a literal index has had a `getReferencePath` since round 461, gets a flow node from `Flow.kt`'s own arm, and the walk compares path STRINGS — yet `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`'s four flow-reading arms all tested `arg is Identifier || arg is PropertyAccessExpression`, and `getTypeOfElementAccess` applied no narrowing at all until round 942 gave it the RECEIVER half (its `getTypeOfPropertyAccess` twin has had both halves since B1.1/17.34d; the RESULT half was measured INERT there and deliberately not added — see the entry below). So never conclude "this shape is not narrowable" from the shape: ask whether the READ happened, by probing the same subject as an identifier and as a property access in one scratch run.
- **An ENUM's own type is a member-LESS `Type.Object` — neither a `Type.Union` nor a `Type.Interface` — so it falls through EVERY "is this narrowable" gate in the checker, and the flow read is never attempted (round 763).** tsc models a literal enum AS the union of its members, so its gates admit it for free; ours must name it (`isEnumFlavoredObjectType`). The tell is a reference that narrows correctly inside an `if` BLOCK (typed from `currentLocalTypes`) and not in a ternary / `&&` / after an early-return guard / in a `switch` — those positions have no condition-pass recording and need the flow read. Before concluding a narrowing rule is wrong for enums, check whether it is REACHED: `getNarrowedTypeForReference`'s call sites each carry their own type gate, and `narrowByCallPredicate`'s single-type arm already answers enums correctly.
- **`resolveEnumSymbolForDiscriminant` IS THE SINGLE NAME-BASED ENTRY INTO THE `"symId#member"` KEY SPACE, AND WIDENING IT NEEDS A TWO-SIDED PROBE, NOT A REVIEW (rounds 768/769).** It resolves FILE-level (`currentFileLocals`/`lookupPerFileForNode`) plus, since round 769, the ENCLOSING-NAMESPACE chain — a namespace member is in neither of the first two, so before that a `namespace`-declared enum was invisible to switch-`default:`, exhaustion, positive-guard AND subtractive narrowing at once (the enum's DECLARATION site decides, not the code's). The round-769 fallback is deliberately STRICTLY ADDITIVE (consulted only where the file-level lookup returned null) because round 425's failure mode is a SPLIT key space. **Any further widening — e.g. the still-open qualified `N.A.X` direction, which also needs `enumMemberTypeOfExpr`'s `pa.expression is Identifier` gate relaxed — must mint both keys side by side and check BOTH that the spaces are disjoint AND that each newly-reached enum's member `parent` canonicalizes to the same id (that parent is `getDeclaredTypeOfEnumMember`'s interning mint), then RE-CHECK after the flip, on the population the flip enlarges.** `getTypeOfExpression` resolves `N.A.X` fine, so a probe that only reads the member type reports everything healthy.
- **A DASHBOARD-PROFILE ZERO BOUNDS A HAZARD'S FREQUENCY, NEVER ITS EXISTENCE — THE 13k-BASELINE CORPUS IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT THAT SEES A SHAPE THE PROFILES DO NOT CONTAIN (round 792).** A whole-function pre-gate on `checkMemberAccessMissing` ("the property already resolves on the receiver, so nothing here can be true") measured **0 emitting calls in a 22,187-call skip set** on the compiler profile and still killed **7 corpus baselines**, because *resolves* and *legal* come apart in two places tsc's own sources never exercise: a later-lib member RESOLVES and is still an error (TS2550 says "not at this target" — excluded by `LIB_MIN_TARGET_PROPS`), and a CLASS receiver's two sides are not separated in our member tables, so an instance type resolves a STATIC member (TS2576 is that diagnostic — excluded when the raw or apparent type's symbol is a Class). **Any new suppression keyed on "the property resolves" inherits both.**
- **A POPULATION MEASURED THROUGH A CONSUMER THAT BAILS ON FIRST FAILURE UNDER-REPORTS ITSELF BY THE FAN-OUT (round 769).** `narrowBySwitchClause`'s default arm does `enumMemberKeyOfExpr(case) ?: return null`, so a blind resolver was asked **2** times on the compiler profile and **130** after the fix — a 65x under-count, and the number that looks like "this cause is tiny". Size such a population by fixing it behind a flag and re-measuring, never by counting calls at the broken state.
- **A NARROWING CHAIN THAT IS RIGHT FOR ITS EARLY STEPS AND WRONG AT THE END IS FAILING AT ITS *LAST* STEP — READ THE ARITHMETIC, NOT THE SHAPE (round 768).** A 13-arm `k === K.X ? … : assertNever(k)` chain reporting the LAST arm's member means the first twelve subtracted and the thirteenth did not (round 767 read the same output as "only the innermost subtracted" and inverted the whole diagnosis). The usual cause is that peeling a union down to ONE member leaves a non-`Type.Union` the subtractor cannot accept — so a PARTIAL chain narrows correctly and a COMPLETE one does not, which is exactly backwards from how a "narrowing is broken" report reads.
- **An enum MEMBER is not a literal NODE, so `literalTypeOfExpression` answers null for `K.A`** — a new equality/switch narrowing rule must also consult `enumMemberTypeOfExpr` (the value-position twin of `enumMemberKeyOfExpr`), and compare members with `enumMemberTypesAreSameMember`, never identity (a module-scoped enum keys a different interned member type per file). Do NOT fold enum-member types into `narrowBySwitchClause`'s `literalTypes`: the discriminant paths key cases through `literalDiscriminantKeyOfType`, which has no answer for one, so a fold makes every MIXED enum/literal discriminant switch bail.
- **A `FlowSwitchClause` with an EMPTY clause range (`clauseStart == clauseEnd`) is the implicit no-case-matched edge out of a `default`-LESS switch and MUST be read as "no case matched" — the same position as a `default:` clause, hence the same subtraction (round 770, mirroring tsc's `createFlowSwitchClause(…, 0, 0)`).** It is layered ON TOP of the pre-existing "every case expression is falsy" FlowCondition chain, which narrows nothing for a DISCRIMINANT switch (there the case expression is a VALUE, not a condition) — that chain alone is why `Debug.assertNever(x)` after an exhaustive `default`-less switch saw the whole declared type. **A reader keying on `clauseStart` ALONE is wrong the moment an empty range reaches it**: `narrowBySwitchOnTrue` read it as "clause 0 matched" and collapsed `switch (true) { case c1: … }` to `never`. Four readers consult this node today; a fifth must consult `clauseEnd` too.


- **A `walkFunctionBodiesInExpr` arm that descends THROUGH a function-like node must install that function's own parameter scope first — the nested `checkFunctionBody` INHERITS `currentLocalTypes`/`varTypes` (round 757, the `ArrowFunction` expression-body arm: saved/restored `EpochMap` + `ctaTypeParamsIntoLocals`, skipped for a zero-parameter arrow).** Omit it and an inner read of a parameter name resolves to a same-named outer/global binding — the `applyBodyLocalShadowing` FP class. Measured aside worth more than the fix: closing that arm's false negative moved NOTHING (profile byte-identical, all 8,837 corpus baselines unchanged) because the 874 expression-bodied arrows hold SIX block bodies between them — **an arm census counts how often an arm is TAKEN, never the population behind it**; round 756 quoted the first as the second and was 146x out.
- **The `.kind` discriminant key space has TWO reader flavours and a flip must carry BOTH, or it silently goes blind on bare-enum annotations (round 752).** `enumSwitchKeysFromType(Node)` answers "the whole member DOMAIN" (`kind: SyntaxKind` → 396 keys, plus nullish markers) while `enumDiscriminantKeysOfType`/`enumMemberKeysOfTypeNode` answer "this one MEMBER" — a type-first reader written with only the member arm measured 9 profile sightings where it LOST keys the annotation had. Probe categories must include TYPEBLIND (type path null where AST answered), not just MISMATCH; and an optional discriminant's `@undefined` still comes from `PropertyDeclaration.questionToken`, never from the resolved type.
- **A reader that is a VETO over the structural relation is probably UNOBSERVABLE post-(REL.1)(b) — measure before crediting a flip or a deletion with a behaviour change (round 752).** `kindDomainKeysExceed`/`kindDomainProvesNotSubtype`/`typeGuardMemberDisjoint` exist because an enum member used to resolve to `anyType`; now the relation decides siblings itself, so every shape built to expose their answer is silent with AND without them. Corollary for any such flip: check whether the reader's only CONSUMER is already slated for deletion before spending a round on it.
- **An ENUM-flavored type is a MEMBER-LESS `Type.Object`, so a rejection reached in `isSimpleTypeRelatedTo` is NOT a rejection — it falls through to the structural engine, which relates two empty objects VACUOUSLY (round 746).** Any rule that must REJECT an enum pair (enum↔enum, member→enum) belongs in `checkTypeRelatedToCore`; only rules that ACCEPT can live in the fast path. Symptom of getting it wrong: the rule compiles, the ablation shows the pass still uniquely load-bearing, and nothing changes.
- **A value-aware enum relation makes EVERY dedicated enum walker CO-EMIT, not just the one being retired (round 746).** Retiring B425/B266 also duplicated B583's (`checkEnumAsgInFunctionScopes`) six TS2322 and B463's TS2416 — neither appeared in either pass's ablation. Before landing an enum relation rule, run `enumAssignmentCompat3/5/6/7` + `enumLiteralAssignableToEnumInsideUnion` through the scratch CLI loop; a walker that still owns the only correct DISPLAY gets the round-744 `diagnostics.removeAll { … }` retraction, not deletion. **Key such a retraction/dedup on (code, fileName, start, MESSAGE), never on the position alone** — `interfaceExtendsClassWithPrivate2` gets TWO legitimate TS2416 at one position (a member violating both a base class and a base interface), and a position-only key silently swallows the second.
- **A pass that RETRACTS before it emits cannot be ablated by COUNTING — the co-emission is invisible while both are live (round 749).** B583 ran `diagnostics.removeAll { code == 2322 && start == lhs.pos }` before adding its own, so "pass live + general rule live" produced the same count and the same bytes as the pass alone; only DELETING the retraction showed all eight of `enumAssignmentCompat6` duplicated byte-identically. Grep a retirement candidate for `removeAll`/`retract` first: with one present, "no duplication" is evidence of NOTHING, and the ablation must be run with it removed.
- **An enum member's tsc VALUE is not `enumValues[…]` — `enumMemberEntries` is (round 746).** A member with no initializer in an AMBIENT non-const enum has NO value in tsc (`declare enum D { X, Y }`); we auto-number it because the Transformer needs one. Reading `enumValues` in a relation/narrowing rule therefore invents a value and rejects programs tsc accepts.
- **A TS2322/TS2345 message names the DECLARED type, not the flow type, and its elaboration can name a constituent the relation never rejected — so reducing from the message is how a real defect gets three wrong diagnoses in a row (round 744).** `declarations.ts:846` read "the truthiness narrow does not strip `undefined` from `T | undefined`"; the narrow was correct and the failing constituent (`NoSubstitutionTemplateLiteral`) appears nowhere in the message. **Probe the actual flow type with a deliberate mis-assignment** (`const z: number = x` at each flow point prints it) before believing any relation message. Reduce with the 1.3-second scratch-project CLI loop — a throwaway tsconfig dir plus `java -cp <build/classes:build/bench/cp.txt> MainKt --noEmit --listAll <dir>` — not the suite; and build the model from the REAL declarations in `build/bench/tsc-project-*/src/compiler/`, deleting ingredients one at a time, because a hand-written approximation of a tsc shape passes vacuously (round 726's flat `interface Box<T>`, round 743's missing generic overload).
- **A union SOURCE relates to a bare `Type.TypeParam` TARGET in our relation (a single-type source correctly does not), and a bare `Type.TypeParam` SOURCE relates to most object targets — two lenience directions that CANCEL for one candidate and COMPOUND for a union.** That is why type-guard narrowing of a type parameter must NOT ask the relation: `getNarrowedType`'s instantiable rule (`T & candidate`, per candidate constituent) is applied FIRST, before either relation arm (Checker.kt, the `isMatch` non-union branch). Any new narrowing/selection rule that tests a type parameter with `checkTypeRelatedTo` inherits both lenience directions silently.
- **AN INTERSECTION IS STORED UN-DISTRIBUTED WHILE tsc DISTRIBUTES AT CONSTRUCTION, SO EVERY CONSUMER NEEDING A MEMBER LIST MUST BUILD ITS OWN VIEW (round 777).** tsc's `getIntersectionType` rewrites `X & (A | B)` into `X & A | X & B` ("union types are always at the top level in type representations") and `getReducedType` drops the discriminant-disjoint combinations — so in tsc `HasModifiers & HasDecorators` IS a 7-member union everywhere. Ours is a `Type.Intersection`: the relation copes via `intersectionSourceDistributes`, flow narrowing via `distributedNarrowingType` (an on-demand view adopted ONLY where it subtracts, so identity/display are untouched otherwise). A NEW consumer that iterates union members will silently do nothing for such a subject; do NOT "fix it properly" in `getIntersectionType` — that changes every intersection's identity, display and relation behaviour at once.
- **`A & (B | C)` is stored UN-distributed and every relation rule asks the whole intersection against one union member at a time, so it relates to nothing without `intersectionSourceDistributes` — and the defect is invisible whenever both sides are the same interned instance.** A test of this family must write the union constituents in a DIFFERENT ORDER on the two sides or it passes on the `source === target` fast path and measures nothing. Interning unions by SORTED constituent ids would hide it too and must never be done: union display order is pinned to pristine tsc's source order.
- **A dedicated walker and the general relation can reach the SAME verdict and CO-EMIT at the same position — a duplicate diagnostic, not a new one.** When a relation rule lands that a walker already covered, the walker must retract (`diagnostics.removeAll { code/fileName/start match }` immediately before its own `add`, cf. `checkNsEnumUnionOne`), because the walker's DISPLAY is usually the tsc-faithful one and the general union display cannot know its collapse rules. Retiring the walker later has to move that display rule with it.
- **To find out what a pass is really doing, ABLATE it with the PassLab instead of deleting it: write `build/pass-lab.txt` with `disable <passName>` and re-run — ZERO recompile.** Whole-suite (`./gradlew jvmTest --rerun`; the lab file is NOT a task input) gives the failing set; per-walker attribution is far cheaper through the scratch-project CLI, since the lab loads from the CWD for any process started at the repo root. **Remove the file afterwards — it silently applies to every later run from that directory.** Round 744 used this to falsify a queue item's "100% artifacts, deletable" list in one suite run plus six 2-second CLI runs.
- **THERE IS NO FRESH-LITERAL EXPRESSION TYPE: `getTypeOfExpression` answers the BASE PRIMITIVE for a literal NODE (`StringLiteralNode` → `stringType`, `NumericLiteralNode` → `numberType`, …), and a `Type.StringLiteral` is minted ONLY by the AST-based `literalTypeOfExpression` (round 781).** So a literal is already widened before any widener sees it — which is why gating `widenType`/`getVarDeclType`/`inferTypeFromInitializer` on const-ness measured BYTE-INERT, and why the const rule ((WIDEN.1), tsc's declaration-flag `getWidenedLiteralTypeForInitializer`) had to be applied where `currentLocalTypes` is RECORDED (`checkVarDeclAssignabilityCore`'s un-annotated branch, plus `spineArithRecordVarDecl`, which runs later and re-widens). Any rule that needs a literal type must read the AST, never `getTypeOfExpression`; and a name's type at every read site comes from `currentLocalTypes`, so that map — not a widener — is where a per-declaration typing rule belongs.
- **`getTypeOfExpression` NEVER flow-narrows — flow narrowing is OPT-IN PER EMISSION SITE via `getNarrowedTypeForReference`, and `getTypeOfIdentifier` answers only from `currentLocalTypes` + the declaration tables (round 743).** Consequence: an `asserts` narrow is NOT visible to a `getTypeOfExpression` consumer (it exists only in the flow graph), whereas an `if (isFoo(x))` type-guard call IS since round 785 — but only inside the THEN branch, and only for a bare-Identifier subject that is already a tracked local. So a new rule that reads an argument/operand type and is meant to respect narrowing must call `getNarrowedTypeForReference` itself; prefer a SECOND CHANCE on the rejecting path (it can only turn a rejection into an acceptance, so nothing that already passed can start failing) over swapping the primary type.
- **Every site that ADOPTS a call signature must ask `signatureAcceptsArgs` (round 743)** — `resolveCallOverload`'s selection loop and `getReturnTypeOfCallExpression`'s B136 concrete-overload swap disagreed for years because the swap only checked arity + "the return is concrete", so it re-picked an overload the selection loop had rejected. A new overload-adopting path that skips the acceptance test reintroduces exactly that.
- **`Signature.parameters` IS NOT THE DECLARATION'S PARAMETER LIST — `getParameterSymbols` DROPS every BINDING-PATTERN parameter (unless `forSignatureDisplay`) and the builder then zips the declaration's type annotations onto the SURVIVORS **POSITIONALLY**, so for `f({a}: O, b: string)` the single surviving symbol is named `b` and typed `O` (round 921).** Anything that renders, counts or aligns parameters from `sig.parameters` is therefore one short AND shifted for such a signature, with no error and no diff to find it by; `minArgumentCount` counts the pattern parameter and so can legitimately EXCEED `parameters.size`. Read `sig.declaration`'s own list whenever it is longer (`typeCaptureSignatureParameters` is the reference), never "fix" the zip.
- **A REAL PROJECT BUILD uses the REAL libs; the CORPUS uses the embedded one — the split is `projectDefaults()` (round 730), NOT the `CompilerOptions.useRealLibs` constructor default.** Flipping that constructor default puts the whole ~13k-baseline corpus on real libs; flipping `projectDefaults()` moves every project build and every dashboard profile. When probing a real-lib behavior, note that name resolution proves NOTHING (an unknown name degrades to `any`, which is silent) — the decisive control is a MEMBER or REQUIREDNESS probe.
- **Never count a declaration's parameter list inline for `Signature.minArgumentCount` — use `requiredParameterCount` (round 730).** A `this` pseudo-parameter is never a call argument and every builder drops it from `Signature.parameters`, so counting it makes `minArgumentCount` EXCEED `parameters.size` and every arity gate rejects the signature against ITSELF. The tell is a chain whose two numbers are EQUAL ("Expected 3 or more, but got 3"); a builder that also zips param symbols positionally against the declaration list needs round 460's this-aware branch too, or every parameter type shifts by one.
- **A property's type must be read THROUGH its carrier: `getTypeOfSymbol(prop)` answers with the DECLARING interface's member type, which for a generic base is the bare TYPE PARAMETER (round 762).** tsc's `Token<TKind> { kind: TKind }` is inherited by every `ModifierToken<SyntaxKind.X>` in `Modifier`, so any `flags` test (EnumLiteral/Enum) on such a read silently answers "no" — this is what made round 760's veto decline on the real source while every plain-interface reduction stayed clean. Use `propertyTypeOnCarrier` (or the `if (x is Type.Reference) resolveGenericPropertyType(...) ?: getTypeOfSymbol(...)` idiom, cf. `resolvePrefixTailSegment`); a guard onto a SINGLE instantiation can still answer correctly, so only a UNION of them reproduces the bug.
- **A NARROWING PROBE IN CALL-ARGUMENT POSITION READS THE DECLARED TYPE, NOT THE FLOW TYPE — use an ASSIGNMENT (round 777, five pins measured inert).** Flow narrowing is opt-in per emission site and the argument gate performs the read only on its special arms: a `never` parameter (rounds 441/462/766) and a bare-enum argument (round 764). So `declare function take(s: string); take(x)` after a guard chain reports the DECLARED type on a WORKING build — five pins written that way produced byte-identical output on a fixed and an ablated binary. `const probe: string = x` (TS2322) reads the real flow type; `assertNever(x)` works precisely because `never` is one of those arms.
- **TWO TYPE ALIASES WITH IDENTICAL BODIES ARE ONE INTERNED TYPE, so a display assertion over them reads the OTHER alias's name (round 777).** `type Left = A | B; type Right = A | B` then `x: Left & Right` displays as `Left & Left` — the pin is then measuring `aliasDisplayMap`, not what it was written for. Same trap class as round 764's "the expected union equals a declared alias": give the two sides genuinely different member sets.
- **…BUT A PRIMITIVE TARGET IS INERT AT THE RETURN PATH, WHICH GATES ITS WHOLE NARROWING BLOCK TO AN OBJECT-ISH/UNION TARGET (round 784).** `checkReturnAssignabilityCore` only attempts flow narrowing when `targetType` is `Type.Interface`/`Reference`/`Object`/`Union`/`Intersection`, so `function f(): string { … return x.p }` reports the UN-narrowed type on a fully working build — the probe reads as "narrowing is broken" when nothing is. Use a UNION target (`string | boolean`) there. General rule: **a probe shape validated at one site is not portable to another site's gate — re-validate it against a control whose answer you already know.**
- **A TYPE GUARD DECLARED BESIDE ITS USE RESOLVES DOWN A DIFFERENT PATH, so a SINGLE-FILE reduction of a guard-narrowing defect can be silent while the two-file form reproduces it exactly (round 760, cost round 784 a build).** A faithful one-file model of `completions.ts`'s `isModifierLike` reported nothing; the same shape with the guard + types in a second file reproduced the profile line verbatim. Before rewriting a model that "does not repro", split the guards into their own file.
- **A NARROWING probe must target a PRIMITIVE, never an object/branded type (round 762; round 760 lost an arc to the same class of error).** Every enum-flavored type is a member-LESS `Type.Object`, so `K`, `K.A` and a washed `never` are ALL silently assignable to any object-shaped probe target — silence conflates "narrowed", "not narrowed" and "collapsed". `declare function probe(x: string)` (or `number`) fires for all of them and NAMES the narrowed type in the message. Corollary: `const y: K.A|K.B = x` cannot detect a widened `x: K` either, because enum → MEMBER relates vacuously — that is (REL.2)'s leniency, so such a pin can never show the positive.
- **A per-file checker PASS loop iterates `checkedResults` (the INV.6 partition view); a COLLECTOR loop (populates cross-file maps/sets, no `diagnostics.add`) MUST iterate `binderResults`** — a gated collector starves partition workers of program-wide suppression context (round 609: 1,174 TS2339 partition FPs). The `--partitionCheck 2` harness on the 8 profiles is the gate for any new pass loop's choice; the sequential path is identical either way.
- **A `--partitionCheck`/`--workers` divergence is NOT automatically the round-609 collector class — it can be a resolution-ORDER accident in which the SEQUENTIAL run is the one that is right for the wrong reason (round 754).** Diagnose by printing the source type, the target type AND the relation verdict in both views before theorising: if the two views resolve the same types and disagree only on the verdict, no collector is starved and there is no cross-file context to restore. Confirm with a two-file scratch project — the round-754 divergence reproduced with no partitioning at all.
- **A bare reference to a generic whose every type parameter has a DEFAULT resolves to the RAW `Type.Interface` (members keep the un-substituted `T`), and the defaulted instantiation is applied at the RELATION boundary (`defaultedInstantiationOfOpenGeneric` in `checkTypeRelatedToCore`), NOT in `getTypeFromTypeReference` where tsc's `fillMissingTypeArguments` does it.** Do not "fix it properly" at resolution: that makes a bare `TableClass` and an explicit `TableClass<any>` the SAME interned instance, and `aliasDisplayMap` excludes `Type.Reference` deliberately, so `type Table = TableClass` stops displaying as `Table` (measured round 754: 8 baseline lines of `typeVariableConstraintedToAliasNotAssignableToUnion`, error set otherwise unchanged).
- **Symbol/Type id sequences are THREAD-LOCAL (INV.6(6c0), round 607) and `runWithDeepStack` (JVM) hands them off: capture caller's counters → seed the compile thread → WRITE THE ADVANCED VALUES BACK on join.** Removing the write-back (or spawning a compile on a fresh thread without seeding) restarts ids at 1 and COLLIDES with the singleton intrinsics' class-load-thread ids — 51 corpus failures whose listAll output stays IDENTICAL (id-keyed cache confusion, mostly silent). A parallel worker instead calls `Symbol.rebaseThreadIds`/`Type.rebaseThreadId` with a high base at thread start; ids must never cross a worker boundary. **Round 827: the NATIVE actual now spawns a thread too and carries the same seed/write-back plus `forceIntrinsicTypeInit()` — under Kotlin/Native's new memory model those cells are SHARED so the handoff is a no-op, and it is written that way precisely so the actual does not depend on which regime is in force.** `DeepStackHandoffTest` (commonTest) observes the counters directly, because this failure mode leaves every byte of output identical; measured native: `before=191 insideStart=191 insideEnd=196 after=196`, `anyType/stringType/neverType = 1/4/10` with identity preserved across the boundary.

- **Any NEW full-tree expression walker MUST handle `BinaryExpression` iteratively (worklist/right-spine), not recursively** — `binderBinaryExpressionStress` is a deeply-nested `a+b+c+…` chain that StackOverflows a naive `walk(left); walk(right)`, and a crash mid-check ABORTS JS emit, so the regression shows up as a JS-EMIT output diff (NOT in the `find_candidates` EXTRA bucket — only the failing-set diff catches it). B98b's `walkThisTypeExpr` learned this the hard way. Reference iterative patterns: B98b's worklist (`ArrayDeque` frontier) and `getTypeOfBinaryExpression`'s left-spine fold; the old right-spine reference `checkArgCountInExprCore` is deleted (round 535 — its depth-cap geometry lives on in `spineArgDepth`/`spineArgEdge`).
- **Do NOT add ANY `try/catch(StackOverflowError)` in the checker — fix the recursion instead.** The checker's overflows are eliminated (0/suite-run): cycles are guarded at the resolvers by in-progress sentinels (`symbolTypeResolutionInProgress`/`nodeTypeResolutionInProgress`/`memberResolutionInProgress`/`relationComparisonStack`/`functionElaborationStack`) plus `collectInheritedPropertyNames`' `visited` set, and the full-tree binary walkers iterate (worklist / `getTypeOfBinaryExpression`'s left-spine `combineBinaryTypes` fold). A new overflow means a missing guard or a recursive walk that should be iterative — add the guard / iterate, don't swallow. All ~213 inline catches were physically REMOVED (2026-07-02, splice-in-place — no `run {}` wrappers, so no `return@run` re-targeting); the ONLY remaining catch is the `init` boundary guard (`reportCheckerStackOverflow` → TS2589), which surfaces any future escape instead of crashing. On Kotlin/Native even that is inert (a native stack overflow is a hard crash — see StackOverflowError.kt). **The boundary guard is LOAD-BEARING — do NOT remove it** (41b3b909 tried "zero catches" and a raw SOE escaped to the test harness): it had been silently absorbing `spreadOverrideExpr`'s left-spine recursion on binderBinaryExpressionStress (a 6,452-term chain; js-only subtest → the TS2589 was invisible) — that walker now iterates via an ArrayDeque worklist, and a guardless full run doubles as the corpus overflow census (1 crash = 1 overflow site). `checkBigIntLiteralsInExpr` + `collectExpandoDeclsExpr` were converted the same day (caught by `DeepExpressionChainTest` at 10k-term depth — their frames fit the corpus 6,452 but not 10k; ~218 other `is BinaryExpression ->` sites remain unaudited). **The whole pipeline additionally runs on a 256 MB deep-stack thread** (`runWithDeepStack`, DeepStack.kt, wrapping `compileParsed`; virtual-reserved, lazily committed — and since round 827 on native too, via a 256 MB pthread whose glibc stack is likewise a lazily-committed anonymous mmap, so it costs nothing on a zero-swap box: measured max RSS 107-111 MB) — this covers `parseAssignmentExpression`'s right-associative recursion (`a = a = …`) and any unconverted walker, but it is a SAFETY MARGIN, not a license: new walkers still iterate.
- **Defensive `try/catch` in the checker/parser/Vfs uses `catch (_: Exception)`, NEVER `catch (_: Throwable)`** (the whole codebase was swept to Exception on 2026-07-04 — 135 sites). `Throwable` swallows `Error` subtypes too, which defeats the SOE doctrine above: a `StackOverflowError` caught by a `catch (Throwable) { null }` returns a silently-wrong default INSTEAD of reaching the `init` boundary guard (→ TS2589), and an `OutOfMemoryError`/`AssertionError` gets masked into wrong output rather than surfacing. `catch (Exception)` still catches the genuine recoverable cases these guards exist for (NPE / ClassCast / IllegalState are `RuntimeException ⊆ Exception`), so narrowing is behavior-preserving on the corpus (byte-identical) EXCEPT that `Error`s now propagate — which is the point. The narrowing is validated by the full suite (exercises all catch paths) + a byte-identical self-compile; `DeepExpressionChainTest` pins the invariant that a deep chain surfaces TS2589 through the boundary guard rather than being swallowed. Removing the defensive `catch (Exception)` ENTIRELY (to surface NPEs from incomplete modeling as crashes) is a separate, per-site root-cause effort — do not do it blind.
- **The whole checker runs inside `Checker`'s `init` block, so a `private val` PROPERTY declared BELOW that block is still null while any pass executes (round 726).** A debug helper gated on such a property therefore prints NOTHING and reads as "wrong code path" when the path is right — put instrumentation state in a top-level `private val`, or hook `Diagnostic`'s constructor (a filtered `init` + `Throwable().stackTraceToString()`), which names the true emission site in one run.
- **A NEW `Checker` FIELD THAT ANY PASS CONSULTS MUST BE DECLARED BEFORE THE `init` BLOCK** — Kotlin initializes properties in DECLARATION order, so a `private val m = HashMap<…>()` written next to its own helper further down the ~110k-line file is still **null** throughout every init-dispatch pass, and the tell is an impossible-looking NPE on a non-nullable `val` (`because "this.m" is null`). Declare checker state beside the existing cache fields (`canonicalEnumSymCache` & co, ~:5582), never beside its consumer.
- **THE TWO CONSTRUCTS THAT BLOCK A MECHANICAL METHOD SPLIT ARE SCOPING, NOT CONTROL FLOW (round 818, `transformClassBody`): a LOCAL `data class` constructed by a region you want to move (its type is un-nameable from a member function — LIFT it to a private nested class; behaviour-free while it captures nothing and never escapes), and a LOCAL `fun` CALLED FROM BOTH SIDES of the boundary (it can neither move nor be duplicated — pass it as a FUNCTION-TYPED parameter, `::localFun`, which leaves the moved call site textually untouched).** A local `fun` whose call sites are ALL inside one region just moves with it, so the test is where the call sites are, not what the construct is. **The order that makes the function-typed parameter sound — the stage that decides the `var`s it closes over must run first — is enforced by NOTHING in the types; ablate it.**
- **A `Checker.kt` stack-trace line number WRAPS modulo 65536** (the JVM LineNumberTable is u2; the file exceeds 90k lines): `Checker.kt:26287` is really line 91823 — add 65536 before trusting a frame that seems to point at unrelated code. **This bites EVERY reader of a `LineNumberTable`, not only a stack trace** — `javap -l`, and so `scripts/method_bytes_by_line.py`, report a method past line 65,536 at its WRAPPED line, and that range is usually a plausible-looking part of the file (round 820 read `tryInferSingleTypeParamFromArgs` as 49967–51030, i.e. inside the `companion object`). Add 65,536 back and ASSERT the corrected span contains the function, as `scripts/tisp_split_analyze.py --bytes` does.
- **MappedType walkers must remember to walk `typeParameter.constraint`**: A `MappedType` AST node has FOUR meaningful child positions: `typeParameter.constraint` (the constraint of `[K in C]`, e.g. `keyof T`), `nameType` (after `as`), `type` (the value), and the typeParameter NAME (introduced into a child scope). Tree-walking visitors must hit all four. Crucially the constraint must be walked in the OUTER `scope` (not the mapped child scope) — the TP being introduced is NOT in scope inside its own constraint. Reference patterns: `collectTypeReferenceNames` (Checker.kt:3890) and `checkUnresolvedInType` (Checker.kt:13931) both walk the constraint correctly. Symptom of forgetting: TS2304 / TS2503 / TS6133 for a name only-used inside the constraint silently never fires.
- **`lexLevelHasName`'s TWO RULES — the UNTRUSTED Module/Enum skip and `scope.symbols`-only — ARE PROPERTIES OF *THAT* ASCENT, NOT OF THE LEXICAL TABLES, AND NEITHER SURVIVES A TRANSPLANT ONTO `spineScopeLookup`'s CHAIN (round 918, both measured by ablation).** The untrusted skip is sound there because the unresolved-names walk keeps a SECOND, export-filtered threaded population underneath it; `spineScopeLookup` has none, so skipping those levels answers NOTHING inside a namespace body. Round 748's `symbols`-only rule is about a RESOLVER whose soundness is that `symbols` excludes every main-bound name — an ENUMERATOR reading `symbols` only offers no file-level declaration and no import at all. Any new consumer of the INV.2(c) tables must ask which of the two chains it is on before inheriting either rule.
- **A PER-NODE HOOK ON THE SPINE SEES *NONE* OF THE CHECKING AMBIENT — THE ANCHORS INSTALL-AND-RESTORE IT PER DISPATCH, SO AT AN ARBITRARY NODE `currentLocalTypes` IS THE **FILE-LEVEL** MAP AND A BODY LOCAL ANSWERS WITH A SAME-NAMED GLOBAL'S TYPE (round 911, measured).** A function body is checked in one nested call from its declaration's anchor, so anything asking "what is the type HERE" from a spine hook must first reproduce `ctaM3StmtAnchorCore`'s prologue (`classForThis / inFn / inAsync / inGen / fnTpDecls / fnTpScope / currentFlowGraph / currentCheckFileName` + the namespace-chain push) and then `withCtaFrameLocals(ctaFrames.last())` — **the position's SCOPE is the cta frame; the ambient FIELDS are not.** Without it a body local read `string` (the global), and a parameter and a narrowed reference both read **`any`** — silently, since `any` is legal everywhere. Corollary measured the same round: **`checkTypeAssignabilityInStatements` is NOT on the spine path for ordinary function bodies** (a hook in its statement loop fired ZERO times over declaration/arrow/method bodies), so the natural guess that the legacy assignability walk owns body-scoped ambient is false.
- **…AND THE ABOVE DOES *NOT* GENERALISE TO NAME RESOLUTION — `currentLocalTypes` IS `String -> Type` AND HOLDS NO SYMBOL AND NO DECLARATION, so reinstalling the cta ambient cannot answer "what does this name refer to HERE" in either direction (round 913, the (API.3b) queue entry predicted the opposite and was wrong).** The walk-scoped input for THAT question is `spineCurrentScope` (the INV.2(c) chain, via `spineScopeLookup`), and `spineScopeEnterIfOwner` runs BEFORE `spineEnterNode`, so it is already correct at an arbitrary node and needs no reconstruction. **What the two share is the only thing that forces capture: `spineScopeClear` nulls the chain per file, so post-hoc a body local resolves to a same-named FILE-LEVEL binding and a parameter to nothing at all.** Corollary for any new name-position consumer: refuse a MEMBER name (`o.p`, a property signature's name) rather than scope-look it up — the lookup finds whatever unrelated binding shares the spelling, and the pin that proves the refusal is a refusal and not a miss is that the TYPE at the same span still captures.

- **A CONTEXT-FREE `Scanner.scan()` LOOP IS A SAFE APPROXIMATION OF THE PARSER'S LEXER ONLY WHERE IT *SPLITS*; WHERE IT *MERGES* IT DE-SYNCHRONISES THE STREAM FOR THE WHOLE REST OF THE FILE, AND THE ONLY MERGING CASE IS THE TEMPLATE SUBSTITUTION (round 919, `SourceIndex.scanTokens`).** `reScanSlashToken` / `reScanGreaterToken` only ever split one token into several, which adds token ends and leaves every real boundary present; skipping `reScanTemplateToken` makes the `}` of `${…}` a CloseBrace and the CLOSING BACKTICK open a literal running to the next backtick anywhere in the file — measured on tsc's `checker.ts` as **50,684 tokens for 3,151,772 characters, longest 62,089**, which made every `-project` position query answer about a huge enclosing node. **A merge is not a coarser answer, it is a wrong one, and no fixture catches it** unless it carries a substituting template AND asserts something AFTER it.
- **A `CtaFrame` DOES NOT THREAD `this` — `CtaFrame.classForThis` IS NULL INSIDE AN ARROW BODY, AND THE CHECKER IS RIGHT ANYWAY, BECAUSE `this` REACHES THE DIAGNOSTICS THROUGH B101's WALK-SCOPED INSTALLS, WHICH AN ARROW DELIBERATELY PRESERVES (round 923, measured byte-identical to tsc 7.0.2 on 17 `this` positions).** So a PULL-based reconstruction of `currentClassForThis` must be transparent to `ArrowFunction` and opaque to every other `this`-binder — and it may NOT be `spineCaClassCtx`, whose KDoc records that a nested `FunctionDeclaration` is BUG-COMPATIBLY transparent to it, nor `typeCaptureEnclosingClass`, which answers the lexical (accessibility) question and would offer a class's members inside a `function`. `typeCaptureThisClass` is the `this`-meaning one.
- **AND A CTA FRAME IS PUSHED AT A `Block` ENTER, SO AN EXPRESSION-BODIED ARROW PUSHES NONE — any defect attributed to "arrows" must be re-checked WITH BRACES, or the repro measures the enclosing method's frame and reads as working (round 923: go-to-definition on `this.p` was green in `() => this.p` and broken in `() => { return this.p }`).**
- **A CAPTURE THAT TYPES THE CARET'S *NARROWEST* NODE ASKS THE WRONG QUESTION AT ANY NODE WHOSE MEANING COMES FROM ITS PARENT, AND THE MEMBER CASE FAILS *SILENTLY AND PLAUSIBLY*: a member name is bound by NO SCOPE, so `getTypeOfExpression` of the `p` in `o.p` resolves the SPELLING — answering the type of whatever unrelated binding shares it, and `any` only where nothing does (round 924: 16 of 23 member positions read a collider).** The rule is tsc's `getTypeOfSymbolAtLocation` — move onto the ACCESS and type that — which also buys narrowing and every instantiation rule for free; the only receivers needing (API.3d)'s carrier are `this`/`super`, plain identifiers in this parser. **No gate here can see it**: the capture is off in production, so the corpus, `cost_gate.py` and the 8-profile grid are all green either way.
- **GROUND TRUTH FOR A LANGUAGE-SERVICE ANSWER IS OBTAINABLE DIRECTLY — `tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc --lsp -stdio` IS AN LSP SERVER**, so a hover/definition/completion expectation can be READ OUT OF tsc 7.0.2 over the same fixture instead of reasoned from `typescript-go-repo/internal/ls/*.go` (round 924; the client is `scripts/lsp_hover.py`). Two predictions were wrong in the round that used it and were caught by it, so **do not hand-write expected values for an (API.\*) pin while this exists**.
- **AN EDIT SPAN AND AN IDENTITY KEY ARE DIFFERENT QUANTITIES THE MOMENT A NON-IDENTIFIER JOINS THE OCCURRENCE POPULATION, AND EVERY MAP KEYED BY ONE AND READ BY THE OTHER THEN FAILS SILENTLY IN THE CONSERVATIVE DIRECTION (round 926).** A capture answer is filed under the NODE's raw `pos`, while `o["p"]`'s rename edit begins one character later (the text between the quotes — tsc edits `[77,78)` for a literal at `[76,79)`); looking the occurrence's prior resolution up by the EDIT's start missed every time, read as an empty expectation, and refused every element-access rename as `WOULD_CHANGE_MEANING`. Carry the key explicitly (`PlannedEdit.nodePos`) rather than re-deriving it from the span.
- **`SourceIndex.identifiers()` AND `occurrenceNodes()` COINCIDE FOR EVERY FILE WITH NO MEMBER-NAME LITERAL, SO A PIN THAT TWO CONSUMERS ASK THE *SAME* POPULATION IS VACUOUS ON ALMOST EVERY HAND-WRITTEN FIXTURE — (INC.13)'s ablation of exactly that drift read **0 RED** (2026-08-23).** The two sets differ only by `{"p": v}` / `o["p"]` / `` [`p`] `` / a computed member declaration, and nobody writes one of those into a hover fixture; one `const third = o["p"];` is what makes the arm discriminate. Same shape one layer up: `documentHighlightsAt` and `fileSemantics` already shared a memo entry on `binder.ts` BEFORE they were made to, because that file has no such literal either.
- **WIDENING A CAPTURE REQUEST FROM ONE CARET TO A WHOLE FILE IS NEARLY FREE AND DOES NOT MOVE ANY ANSWER — +9 TO +17 ms AT THE MEDIAN FILE OVER TSC'S OWN 78 SOURCES, AND **0 DIVERGENCES IN 1,883 SAMPLED SPANS ACROSS TWO DISJOINT DRAWS** (2026-08-23, (INC.13)).** A narrowed build is mostly FLOOR (~345 ms), so extra spans are cheap beside it; and (INC.10)'s first-touch hazard does NOT fire, because a capture changes WHERE a walk records, not WHEN the compiler resolves a declaration. `scripts/caret-vs-file-capture.sh` is the differential and needs NO baseline (both arms answer the same question) — deliberately separate from `capture-equivalence.sh`, which varies the PARTITION at a fixed request where this varies the REQUEST at a fixed partition.
- **A MEMBER'S RELATION TO THE BASE IT IMPLEMENTS IS A *REFERENCE* FACT AND NOT A *NAVIGATION* ONE — tsc 7.0.2 ANSWERS THE TWO DIFFERENTLY AND SO MUST WE (round 926, measured with `scripts/lsp_member_refs.py`).** `textDocument/definition` on the `p` of `class Impl implements Shape` answers THAT MEMBER; `textDocument/references` on it answers `Shape`'s whole group. Hence `CapturedDefinition.related` beside `locations`. Three further properties of that edge, each measured and each refuting a plausible design: **structural compatibility does not relate** (a same-shaped class with no `implements` is a different symbol), it **is transitive** (an `override` two edges down is in the group), and it **does not chain between siblings** (with `class C implements A, B`, a caret on `A.p` does not reach `b.p`) — so it is matched PER OCCURRENCE as "symbol + the bases that symbol implements, sets intersect", never as a transitive closure over the group, which merges the two interfaces.
- **A MEMBER NAME DERIVED FROM A *TYPE* IS NOT A FUNCTION OF THE PROGRAM HERE, SO tsc's `isTypeUsableAsPropertyName` (late binding) IS NOT PORTABLE AS WRITTEN — a FILE-LEVEL un-annotated `const K = "p"` answers the literal `"p"` in the assignability pass and the widened `string` in the pass behind TS2339, and `const obj = { [K]: 1 }; obj.p` then emits the correct TS2322 *and* `Property 'p' does not exist on type '{}'` in ONE compile (round 935, measured).** Same signature as round 933's two-extraction-sites drift, reached through ambient state (round 911) rather than through a second `when`. Any NAME/KEY a member table is built from must be resolved SYNTACTICALLY (`lateBoundComputedKeyName` — an enum member's value, or an innermost-first walk of the enclosing statement lists; `lookupPerFileForNode` cannot see a body local, B83.5) — and the pin for it asserts the two passes agree, because each pass alone is green.
- **`Node.end` IS NOT THE END OF THE NODE — IT IS THE END OF THE TOKEN *FOLLOWING* IT, FOR EVERY NODE BUT `SourceFile`, SO SIBLING SPANS **OVERLAP** AND `[pos, end)` IS NOT A CONTAINMENT TEST (round 910).** `Parser.getEnd()` is `scanner.getPos()` (`Parser.kt:746`), read after the parser's one-token lookahead: in `const abc = 1;\nlet d = abc;`, `abc` reads `[6,11)` where its text is `[6,9)` (past the ` =`) and statement 1 reads `[0,18)` where its text ends at 14 (past `let`). **So `end - pos` is not a length, a caret on the `=` tests as inside the preceding identifier, and a caret on a statement's first keyword tests as inside the PREVIOUS statement** — any position→node lookup must bound each end by the next sibling's `pos` (or by a `Scanner` re-scan). `SourceFile` is exact only because EOF is zero-width. **And `Node.pos` is the mirror trap in the other direction: it is tsc's `getStart()`, NOT tsc's `pos`** — `Scanner.scan` sets `tokenPos` AFTER `scanLeadingTrivia()` (`Scanner.kt:331-333`), so trivia is already skipped and leading comments hang off `leadingComments` BELOW the node's span; a routine ported from tsc that adds a `getStart()` skip double-skips past the node's own first token. Pinned by `NodeSpanSemanticsTest` in the `-project` module.

- **A HAND-WRITTEN FIXTURE FOR A LEXICAL OR POSITION API DOES NOT CONTAIN WHAT REAL SOURCE CONTAINS, AND NINE GREEN ROUNDS PROVED IT — `(BUG.2)` SHIPPED FROM ROUND 910 TO 919 AND `(GATE.2)` FOUND *FIVE MORE* DEFECTS THE MOMENT REAL TypeScript WAS POINTED AT THE SAME CODE (round 920).** Nobody writes a substituting template, a backtick inside a regex (tsc's own `utilities.ts` has one), or a parenthesis-less arrow parameter into a go-to-definition pin — yet the last of those is **328 sites in tsc's 78 compiler sources**. **So a position/lexical feature is gated by `TokenIndexInvariants` over real source, not by pins**: rules true of any correct implementation (tokens partition the text, gaps hold only trivia, every identifier the PARSER found starts a token of exactly its length, a descent reaches every identifier), with **the parse as the ORACLE** — it is the context-sensitive lexer, so a MERGE is expressible as "an identifier with no token starting at it" and needs no baseline. Hermetic corpus: the adversarial shape set plus `RealLibFiles.files` (2.39 MB of real TypeScript already embedded — no vendored tree, no licensing question). The local one (`build/bench/tsc-project-*`, where every defect actually showed) lives in `scripts/round920-token-gate.sh`, which **REFUSES with exit 2** rather than skipping, because a gate reading a local artifact that passes quietly where it is absent is rounds 853/873 again.
- **THE SAFE APPROXIMATION OF A CONTEXT-SENSITIVE LEXEME IS TO *ASK THE PARSE*, NOT TO GUESS — A `RegularExpressionLiteralNode` AND A `JsxText` EACH CARRY THEIR OWN **RAW** TEXT, SO `pos + text.length` IS EXACT AND HAS NONE OF `Node.end`'s OVERSHOOT (round 920).** Round 919's law (a SPLIT is safe, a MERGE de-synchronises to end of file) says which cases are dangerous; this says what to do about them, and it is strictly better than a heuristic: whatever the parser decided a `/` was, the index reproduces, so the index and the tree **cannot disagree** and the undecidable question is never asked. Two failure directions the law hides: a regex containing a QUOTE merges only to the line break (our `scanStringLiteral` stops there) while one containing a BACKTICK merges to the next backtick anywhere in the file — so the same defect is loud in one costume and near-silent in the other.
- **A NODE BUILT WITH `Parameter`/`VariableDeclaration`'s DEFAULT `pos = 0, end = 0`, OR WITH AN *EXACT* END WHERE EVERY OTHER NODE CARRIES THE FOLLOWING TOKEN'S, IS A NODE NO POSITION LOOKUP CAN EVER ENTER — AND NOTHING IN THE COMPILER NOTICES (round 920, five sites).** `SourceIndex.realEndOf` snaps an end back to the greatest token end strictly below it, so an already-exact end lands on the token BEFORE the node and clamps to `pos`: an empty span. The five were the paren-less arrow parameter, the index-signature parameter, the `catch` variable, `declare global`'s `global`, the JSX tag name and a construct signature's synthetic `new`. **A new synthesized name must either carry the span of the text it names or stay empty-texted** — the corpus cannot see the difference (core pins were unchanged by all eight fixes), only the invariant gate can.

- **A RAW `(pos, end)` PAIR DOES NOT ALWAYS IDENTIFY ONE NODE, AND THE CASE WHERE IT DOES NOT IS EXACTLY THE ONE A COMPLETION IS ASKED IN (round 917).** A dangling `.` makes `Parser.kt`'s `Dot ->` arm synthesize a ZERO-WIDTH `Identifier("")` (TS1003), so when the buffer ends IMMEDIATELY after the dot the `PropertyAccessExpression`'s `end` — read after a lookahead that sees only EOF — equals its RECEIVER's, and `o` and `o.<nothing>` become the same `TypeCaptureSpan`. **Any span-keyed recorder must let a DESCENDANT overwrite its ancestor** (`Checker.typeCaptureMemberNodes`); plain first-wins then answers `any`, and `holder. ` / `holder.;` / `holder.\n` all hide it — one buffer shape in five fails.

- **`StringLiteralNode.isUnterminated` IS *FALSE* FOR A LONE OPENING QUOTE — `Parser.kt` decides it as `startsWithQuote && raw.last() != quote`, and for a one-character raw text the first character IS the last, so `o["` at end of file parses as a TERMINATED empty string (round 929).** That is exactly the state an editor asks a completion in, and nothing else notices: the node is well formed and no diagnostic moves — only a consumer that BRANCHES on the flag answers wrongly. Check the arithmetic beside it (`end - pos < 2` cannot be a closed literal), or fix the parser and expect a new TS1002 on that shape.
- **EVERY OFFSET→LINE CONVERSION IN THIS COMPILER GOES THROUGH `lineBreakWidthAt` (`LineStarts.kt`) — `\n`, `\r\n` AND A LONE `\r` EACH END EXACTLY ONE LINE — AND A SIXTH PRIVATE COPY IS A SELF-INCONSISTENCY NOTHING HERE CAN SEE (round 910 found the split, round 915 unified it).** There were **six** implementations in **three** conventions, none referring to any other: the Parser's (correct), `Checker.lineStartsFor` and its INVERSE `posOfLineCol` (`\n` only, so the two directions did not round-trip), `TypeScriptCompiler.positionToLineCharacter` + its inline TS2688 twin, the `Transformer`'s JSX dev-runtime coordinates (EMITTED JavaScript, not a diagnostic), and `CompilerOptions.computeLineAndColumn` (which treated `\r` as **zero-width**). Add a converter only by delegating to `computeLineStarts` (memoized/indexed) or `lineAndCharacterAt` (bounded scan); `LineTerminatorConsistencyTest` + `ProjectPositionTest`'s lone-`\r` differential are the pins, and `-project`'s `LineMap` is the ONE sanctioned reimplementation (it also needs `lineContentEnds`).
- **…AND THE REASON THAT FAMILY WAS INVISIBLE IS NOT "no fixture has a lone `\r`" BUT "NO FIXTURE CAN": `parseMultiFileSource` — the `// @directive`/`@Filename` splitter behind `TypeScriptCompiler.compile(String)`, i.e. the WHOLE generated corpus and every `diagnose()` pin — opens with `.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace("\r", "\n")` (round 915).** `String.trimIndent` in `diagnose` does the same one layer up. So a line-terminator pin written the normal way is VACUOUS AND PASSES (round 915's first one did); use `diagnoseVerbatim`, which hands the pipeline a `ParsedSource` directly. Only the project/`Vfs` path preserves terminators — which is the `(API.*)` embedding path, so anything about source TEXT SHAPE is corpus-invisible and `-project`-visible. A fixture BEGINNING with a break is vacuous for a second reason: blank lines before the first content line are dropped.
- **A DOC SECTION THAT SUMMARISES BEHAVIOUR IS NOT EVIDENCE OF IT, AND MEASURED HERE IT DECAYS AT ~ONE FALSE CLAIM PER THREE ROUNDS — `docs/language-service.md` § 14 was written in round 928 and audited by EXECUTION in round 930 with FOUR of its claims false, one a defect closed BEFORE the page was written and one a defect the page's own § 9 had been promising for two rounds.** It fails in BOTH directions (two of the four were wrong in the compiler's FAVOUR) and re-READING the source confirms a claim rather than testing it, which is how the stale one survived three readings. So: a behavioural claim a test can express belongs in a test the page NAMES (`LanguageServiceStateTest`), and a claim no test can express — a wall figure, "there is no X layer" — must be MARKED as such in the page. **Corollary from the same round: hover and go-to-definition are TWO mechanisms over one caret** (`typeCaptureThisMemberType` vs `typeCaptureMemberSymbols`), so a receiver leg added to one is absent from the other — `super.p` typed correctly and navigated NOWHERE — and a claim confirmed at § 8 is no evidence at all about § 9.

- **Every AST node class extends `NodeBase` (INV.2(a), round 495): `nodeId`/`parent` are stamped by `indexSourceFile` at the end of `Parser.parse()`; data-class `copy()` / Transformer-synthesized nodes stay UNINDEXED (−1/null) — never rely on nodeId/parent after a transform.**
- **M0.2 (round 621): every node class ALSO stamps `NodeBase.kindId` via `init { kindId = NodeKind.X }` (runs on `copy()` too — synthesized nodes STAY stamped, unlike nodeId/parent), and the hot dispatchers (`forEachChild`, the checkSpine when-chains) switch on it.** A NEW node class is compile-forced through `nodeKindIdOf` (sealed-exhaustive) into a dense `NodeKind` const, but the class's own stamp is pinned only at runtime (NodeKindIdTest + forEachChild's loud `else`); keep NodeKind ids DENSE (density buys forEachChild's tableswitch). When converting another `when(node)` dispatcher to `when(kindId)`, a MULTI-LINE `if`-header arm is the mangle hazard (an inserted arm-close can empty the gate and run the body unconditionally) — after any scripted conversion, scan for a line ending `{` followed by a DEDENTED bare `}`. A NEW node class needs `NodeBase(), ` first in its supertype list (else indexSourceFile's cast crashes every parse) plus a `forEachChild` arm (compile-enforced exhaustive `when`); a new NODE-TYPED property on an existing class must be walked in `forEachChild` — the jvmTest reflection oracle (`ForEachChildOracleTest`) diffs the enumeration against the data-class componentN properties and fails on the gap. LUB trap: the shared superclass makes an inferred-return `when`/`?:` mixing node classes degrade to `Any` — annotate the return type explicitly (cf. `parsePropertyName`).
- **Every option-derived `Parser(...)` constructor argument MUST flow through `ParserFlags`/`computeParserFlags` (INV.1(e), round 494)**: the project crawl pre-parses each file with those flags and the core's multi-file site REUSES the tree when recorded content+flags match (`ParsedSource.preParsed`) — a parse-affecting parameter computed inline at a parse site is INVISIBLE to the match, so the core silently reuses a tree parsed WITHOUT it (a wrong tree, not just lost perf). Extend `ParserFlags` + `computeParserFlags` instead; `Inv1PreParseReuseTest` pins the gate.
- **An INV.3(c) per-file flip of a `globals[name]` consult goes through `globalsForFile(fileName, name)` — never substitute `lookupPerFile`'s return directly**: byte-identity depends on returning the MERGED-globals instance for legitimately visible names (`perFileScope` holds the first-occurrence script symbol, `globals` the first-declarer merged instance — different objects for lib/script names), and the conflated branch must not touch `globals` (keeps the `--passTiming` conflated tables = un-migrated traffic). Mirrored consult sites (e.g. the TS2315/TS2346 heritage gate pair) flip together as ONE logical site. **A name read FROM AN AST NODE keys by the node's OWNING file — `lookupPerFileForNode(node, name)`, never `currentCheckFileName` (a types.ts annotation resolves under types.ts's visibility; the checking-file key silently KILLS narrowing wherever that file doesn't import the name). The kind-domain/enum-discriminant readers (`resolveEnumSymbolForDiscriminant`/`kindDomainTypeDeclSymbol` + the alias fallbacks in `enumSwitchKeysFromTypeNode`/`enumMemberKeysOfTypeNode`) are flipped (round 505) — a NEW discriminant-key reader's merged-globals fallback must consult the same primitive, keeping `currentFileLocals` as the first consult. The bare-Identifier VALUE/receiver/callee cluster is flipped too (rounds 506/507 — protected members/ctors, checkPrivateMemberAccess, getCalleeType's Identifier branch, resolveFlowCalleeDecl, resolveNamespaceMemberFnDecl, the ns-fallback receiver resolvers, isCalleeResolvable, the two mam receiver consults): key by the name's own Identifier node. `getTypeOfIdentifier`'s globals fallback is flipped too (round 507b) — the round-442 by-NAME dead-end does NOT reproduce per-FILE because imports resolve through the visibility probe to the same merged instance; `lookupPerFileForNode` fast-paths non-module-only names straight to `globals[name]` (no parent walk — the fallback is ~2M calls/compile).
