The TYPE-position resolvers are flipped too (rounds 508/509 — the (c) migration is COMPLETE; conflated 157k → 917 = the shadow ecology): `resolveTypeNameToSymbol`'s Identifier branch, `typeNodeDefinitelyNonNullish`'s two fallbacks, `getTypeFromBaseTypeExpression`'s Identifier arm, and the tiny value tail — TRAP: a flipped resolver's CALL SITE must not keep its own trailing `?: globals[name]` for Identifier names (byte-redundant pre-flip, silently re-leaks the node-keyed null post-flip); gate such fallbacks to QualifiedName (`getTypeFromTypeReference`, `checkConstraintsInTypeNode`). `lookupPerFileForNode` additionally grants a node inside a `declare module "<relative-spec>"` AUGMENTATION block the augmented module's direct named exports (the round-443 rule — without it, this-predicate narrowing inside services-style augmentations dies). STILL-LEGACY on purpose: the shadow-detection ecology (`registerNestedGlobalShadow*`/`applyBodyLocalShadowing`/`shadowNestedFunctionNames` — their consults ASK "does a merged global collide"; they die with INV.3(d), do not flip them).**
- **INV.3(d) retire staging (round 510, measured; the retire lives on branch `wip/inv3d-merge-retire`): the step-1 merge retire must exclude ONLY module-only names — SHARED names (a module local colliding with a lib/script global: `Symbol`/`Node`/`Performance`) must KEEP merging until every lib-name consumer resolves per-file (naive full retire = 861 compiler FPs; importers free-ride on the merge because the general resolver can't follow `.js` barrels).** Companion trap: `mergeModuleAugmentations`' "new export → `globals[exportName] = augSymbol`" add must be gated to FILELESS ambient targets — with the base name retired it inserts an AUGMENTATION-ONLY stub that the 1b visibility delta marks non-module-only, so every annotation of `SourceFile`/`Node` resolves to the stub (services TS2339×5355); a `.js` relative augmentation target must resolve via `resolveModuleSpecifierRelativeJsAware` so the target-LOCALS merge carries the members instead.
- **Block-scoped declarations are NOT bound** (B83.5, 2026-05-27): `Binder.kt:bindStatement` only dispatches symbol-declaration cases via explicit `when` arms (Variable/Function/Class/Interface/TypeAlias/Enum/Module/Import/Export). The catch-all `else` branch silently ignores `WhileStatement`/`Block`/`IfStatement`/`ForStatement`/`TryStatement`/`SwitchStatement` and every other non-declaration statement, so any `class X` / `interface I` / `type T` / `enum E` / `function F` declared INSIDE their bodies is NEVER bound to file `locals` or `globals`. Consequence: any checker walker that looks up such a declaration by name via `globals[name]` / `result.locals[name]` / `perFileScope[file]?.get(name)` returns null. Symptom: TS2339/TS2551/TS2693/TS2304 silently never fires inside block-scoped class bodies because `classType = null`. Surgical fix pattern when the AST is in-hand: synthesize a transient `Symbol(SymbolFlags.Class, stmt.name.text)` with `declarations = [stmt]` and pass to `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol(symbol)`. The synthetic symbol must NOT be published to binder tables (`globals.put` / `result.locals.put`) — it's a checker-pass-local temporary; cross-decl merging or multi-pass identity is the binder's job. Used in `checkPropertyAccessInStatement`'s ClassDeclaration branch (Checker.kt:~61539); extend to similar `globals[stmt.name.text]`-based lookups if more shapes surface (other checker walkers that recurse into block bodies but resolve nested decls by name). **IN TYPE POSITION THE FAILURE IS NOT ALWAYS A NULL, AND THAT IS THE TRAP (round 747): a UNIQUE name degrades to `any` — SILENTLY, with no TS2304, because the INV.4(c)(iii) family finds it through the INV.2(c) lexical scopes while `getTypeFromTypeReference` does not consult them — but a name that SHADOWS an outer declaration resolves to the OUTER symbol, so the annotation is judged against the wrong type rather than an absent one.** So a fix here cannot be a "when the lookup misses" fallback; a walker needing the inner declaration must read it from the AST (cf. `checkEnumAsgInFunctionScopes`' `eafsScanIife`). `computeAllEnumValues` has the same blind spot — it walks `result.locals` + namespace exports, so a body-scoped enum has no `enumValues` entry either. **CLOSED FOR `enum` ONLY (round 748, `lexicalTypeSymbolForNode`); the other kinds are still unbound.** **AND A NAME-BASED ENUM READER MUST CONSULT ALL THREE SCOPES OR IT IS SILENTLY BLIND: file locals, the enclosing-NAMESPACE exports (round 769), and the function-body SCOPE space (round 782) — the enum's DECLARATION site decides, and a miss blinds EVERY narrowing direction at once.** The scope-space consult goes FIRST, not as a miss-fallback (the opposite of round 769's additive namespace rule): `resolveTypeNameToSymbol` already prefers it, so preferring the file-level answer for a SHADOWING name keys one enum through two `Symbol` instances = the round-425 split key space. Still open in VALUE position: a block-scoped enum shadowing a file-level one FPs TS2339 on its member accesses.
- **A checker resolver consulting the INV.2(c) `lexicalScopes` must read `scope.symbols` ONLY, never `LexicalScope.existing` (round 748).** `declareLexical` skips any name the main binder already bound in that container, so a conventionally-bound name is ABSENT from `symbols` at every level — which is what makes such a consult provably unable to change how any existing name resolves, and keeps the INV.3(c)/(d) per-file/merged-instance minefield entirely out of scope. Reading `existing` (the alias of the main binder's table) looks like the "complete" thing to do and puts every INV.3 name back in play for nothing. The same property gives shadowing for free: a scope-space binding exists only where the main binder had none, so an innermost-first ancestor walk reaches the inner declaration before the file root's aliased locals offer the outer one — no "miss" needs detecting. A resolver so extended must also feed whatever program-wide pass precomputes state for that symbol kind (`computeAllEnumValues` needed a scope-space leg, or the round-745 value-aware relation receives a value-less enum and silently accepts everything).
- **The INV.4 check spine (`checkSpine`, round 514) is THE walk for migrated per-node checks (`spineEnterNode`/`spineLeaveNode` `when` dispatch; always-on since batch 1) — a NEW handler carries its OWN option/file-kind gates (there is NO file-level dts/js skip; per-run gates are fields computed at checkSpine entry, pre-`init` declared).** Prefer a spine handler over a new full-tree pass; a handler sees ALL nodes (a hand-walk's under-visits become visits — decide widen-vs-gate per the emission-direction rule; a module-scope-only old walk reproduces as the module-chain parent-walk gate, cf. spineCheckGlobalAugmentation; a COLLECT-then-SCAN walker migrates as spine collection + BUFFERED positions resolved at file END — per-file state cleared in checkSpine's loop, cf. spineCollectObjLitVar/spineResolveDeferredIterationChecks, preserving use-before-decl matching with no prepass walk; a per-PARAMETER rule keyed on the owning construct dispatches on `param.parent` kind, cf. spineCheckRestParam's value-vs-type-position split; a walker threading MULTIPLE flags with different reset rules (ambient + atTopLevel) migrates as ONE parent-chain walk where the INNERMOST flag-deciding ancestor wins per flag and any non-descended ancestor kind returns null = the old no-visit, cf. spineDupModContext/spineAmbientInitContext; a per-statement-LIST stateful walker — fresh binding maps per nested block, accumulated in statement order — migrates as a preceding-sibling scan of the parent list AT THE CONSUMER node, single-statement positions degrading to `listOf(stmt)`, cf. spineSwitchSubjectBinding/spineSiblingStatements; a walker whose STATEMENT walk stops at ambient decl bodies but whose EXPRESSION walk resets ambient unconditionally needs BOTH rules in one parent-chain walk — own-`declare` on a passed decl-body owner returns null, a declare-module found ABOVE a passed decl-body returns null via a `passedDeclBody` flag, and expression-fn boundaries (arrow/fn-expr/class-EXPR-member/objlit-method bodies) CLEAR that flag while deciding ambient=false, cf. spineAmbientImplContext — forgetting the clear wrongly nulls a fn nested arrow-deep inside a declare-module; a walker that SKIPS a subtree by construction-context — the destructuring-assignment-LHS pattern — migrates as a came-from-child parent climb through pattern-position parents only (a PropertyAssignment continues the climb ONLY when the child is its INITIALIZER; any other parent kind terminates → checked), answering skip iff a `=` BinaryExpression is reached with the climbed child as its LEFT, cf. spineObjLitInDestructuringLhs; a walker threading NEAREST-BOUNDARY flags (isAsync + the related-info FuncRef) migrates as a full parent-chain walk where the FIRST function-like boundary decides the flags but EVERY step up to the SourceFile must still be an old-walked position — deciding flags at the first boundary and STOPPING wrongly reaches e.g. an arrow nested in a parameter default, cf. spineAwaitCtx; a TYPING walker with per-scope stacks (INV.4(d), cf. spineUncalledWithScopes round 530) REBUILDS them pull-based at each emission from the anchor's ancestor chain with per-owner memoized LAZY levels — sound only when the level collection is position-independent and consults only OUTER levels — and save-set-restores its ambient state (currentFlowGraph/currentCheckFileName) around EACH dispatch, never walk-wide (the currentArithmeticFlowGraph 78-test hazard); an emission that legacy-ran INSIDE the owner's scope push anchors the rebuild at the BODY, not the owner (arrow expr-body truthiness); an ORDER-DEPENDENT recording pass (statement-ordered currentLocalTypes recordings that deliberately LEAK across blocks/if-branches/accessor bodies — checkArithmeticOperandTypes, round 531) migrates PUSH-based instead: ONE LIFO of (child-node, saved-value) frames maintained by per-node enter/leave hooks, edge frames keyed on the CHILD and popped at its leave AFTER the node's own emissions, shared fields swapped to pass-private structures only inside the per-emission install; its legacy left-spine flatten reproduces as chain-root LEAVE emission (innermost-first; root = non-`&&`/`=`-objlit and not left-ABSORBED — the flatten descends left unconditionally, so any left-edge ancestor with a non-special operator absorbs the node and its special branch never runs); a pass migrating from AFTER the three giants must be SLOT-MOVE A/B'd first (move the intact pass to the spine slot, listAll ×8) — the arithmetic pass coupled BOTH ways: its currentParamBindingNames leak into the giants (now pass-private), and it CONSUMED the TS2322 walk's namespace-level currentLocalTypes recording residue — only the CORPUS caught that (qualify.ts; zero instances on the 8 tsc profiles), reproduced as the pass's own ModuleBlock-gated identifier-init chain recording — so a slot-moved pass needs the corpus suite run on the slot-move experiment too, not just the profile A/B; a DOWNWARD-CONTEXT-THREADING walker (explicit context params passed through an expression recursion — checkImplicitAnyParameters, round 532) migrates as ONE push-maintained ctx value with frames defined at EXACTLY the edges the legacy recursion passed arguments over — the load-bearing rule: every REACHED expression-position edge must DEFINE the ctx (even to null), or the parent ctx silently LEAKS where the legacy call passed defaults; one-level special states (call-args/objlit-enrichment/destructure-defaults) ride KINDED carrier values that only the immediate child edges read, a per-operand-uniform binary left-spine loop dissolves into per-edge operator rules (no chain-root machinery — unlike arith, whose EMISSIONS were chain-rooted), and statement-threaded flags (returnCtxAnnotation/inAmbientContext) pull-derive from parent chains because the reach classifier already guarantees the path shape; a STATELESS multi-COMPONENT pass (tree walk + per-file top-level scans — checkDuplicateIdentifiers, round 533) migrates as a boolean reach classifier + anchor dispatch running the RETAINED bounded leaf utilities, its file-level scans riding checkSpine's loop at file enter/leave in the legacy within-file order — and must REPRODUCE THE LEGACY PASS-AMBIENT: the old slot ran with `currentFileLocals = null` while checkSpine sets it per file, so every migrated scan consulting it needs a null install (checkClassNamespacePrototypeConflict's `currentFileLocals ?: globals` fallback silently changes meaning otherwise); a per-statement-LIST ordered walker with DOWNWARD context (checkDefiniteAssignment, round 534) migrates as CORE FRAMES pushed at list-owner enters (per-statement steps at direct-child statement enters — `parent === top.owner`, no classifier) + a multi-state reach classifier whose context-flavored statuses READ the top frame's per-statement snapshot at spawn time — sound ONLY while context-preserving edges never cross another frame spawn (here: every core-spawning Block/ModuleBlock drops or consumes the leak); a sibling pass that DEDUPS one-directionally against the migrating pass's emitted diagnostics (checkDefiniteAssignmentViaFlowGraph scans `diagnostics` for TS2454 starts) must MOVE WITH it to keep emitter-order (siblings re-dispatched right after the spine); a walker whose recursion carries a DEPTH CAP (checkArgumentCounts' argCountDepth ≤200, round 535) migrates as an INT-valued reach classifier — the legacy counter reproduced per edge (expression-position edges +1 then capped; a binary RIGHT operand that is itself a binary is ABSORBED at the parent's depth = the old right-spine loop, so right-leaning chains never consume depth while left-nested ones prune); a MAP-valued downward context that is a pure function of the ancestor chain (whole-list overlays, never a positional prefix) rebuilds PULL-based via per-list-owner memoized levels, and a pull rebuild that RE-ENTERS itself through its own memoized levels must reuse its shared ascent buffer MARK-based, never clear()-based (the clear silently drops the outer frame's recorded edges — the for-of loop-shadow pin caught it); a walker whose per-statement work is a RETAINED bounded sub-walk that itself recurses some statement kinds (checkUBDForwardRefs descends if/labeled) anchors ONLY at DIRECT statements of activated lists — anchoring at nested positions double-emits (round 536); a pass whose emitter CO-EMITS a second code consumed by a later pass's dedup scan (emitTS2448's TS2454 vs checkDefiniteAssignmentViaFlowGraph) changes dedup visibility when it moves before that pass — slot-move pre-gate with the CORPUS, not just listAll; a pass whose emission gates PROBE THE DIAGNOSTICS LIST at positions inside the anchor's own subtree (checkBodyForImplicitReturnCore's 17.135 TS2304/TS2314 suppression) must dispatch at the anchor's LEAVE, not enter; a per-anchor ambient install of a WIDELY-CONSUMED field (currentLocalTypes for checkConstAssignment's B116 param typing, round 538) must wrap ONLY the emission call, never the subtree walk — a walk-wide install leaks into every other spine handler's ambient — the probed diagnostics are emitted by OTHER spine handlers at the subtree nodes' enters (round 537: enter-dispatch over-emitted TS2355 on 2 corpus tests the 8 profiles missed); a migrated pass's per-dispatch AMBIENT must reproduce the LEGACY DRIVER's own install, not a copied template: a pass whose driver set `currentFileLocals = result.locals` itself installs the FILE's locals per dispatch (round 655), while a pass whose legacy slot ran OUTSIDE the spine's per-file install captures the spine-entry RESTING value (rounds 624/625/631/649) — copying the wrong one silently changes name resolution with no gate signal when the code never fires on the profiles; a pass whose walk threads NO downward value at all (constant recursion arguments — grep the deleted walker's signature) needs none of the ctx/frame/status machinery, only a memoized BINARY reach classifier + anchor (round 655), but its edge set must be diffed against the same-shaped sibling classifier it resembles rather than transcribed (spineNaEdge vs spineDelEdge differ on objlit method bodies / for-head DECL-LIST initializers / switch case expressions); a downward context that is a type-system AMBIENT REGION the legacy walk held OPEN across a subtree (`withInternedTpScope`) cannot stay open across spine nodes — the boundary CAPTURES the region's result instead (run it for its interning/constraint-materialization side effects, read the ambient fields from INSIDE the block, carry the pair on the frame, install it per dispatch only), which keeps first-touch identical while obeying the never-walk-wide rule (round 658, checkTypeParamTypedOps); a pass that OWNS NO WALK — its driver only drives a SHARED walker with its emitter as the callback (grep the driver for a `walk*(stmt, …, ::emit*)` call) — migrates as a FOLD-IN when a sibling driving that walker is already on the spine: add the leaf to that arm (POSITIONED by the legacy slot order, since the stable diagnostic sort only hides insertion order away from exact start/length/code/message ties) and delete the driver; the reach is the sibling's classifier by construction (round 657, checkArrayToClassCastOverlap → spineCoEnterNode) — and on such a SHARED anchor a "this leaf stays silent" negative pin must discriminate by the emitter's own message CHAIN, never by the code count (a sibling emitter fires the same code at the same cast); a TWO-INTERLEAVED-WALKS pass whose walks OVERLAP on most arms (round 656: ~30 of ~45 shared, each shared arm's child keeping the parent's status) keys its fold on the node KIND with `pStatus` branching only inside the DIFFERING arms — the walk identity rides a pass-through arm as `-> pStatus`; round 640's outer `when (pStatus)` form is right only when the walks' node sets are near-DISJOINT, else it duplicates the shared arms verbatim and an arm diff silently drifts; a pass consuming a PRODUCER sibling's side-set (spreadNonIterableHandledCalls, populateAmbientCyclicBaseClasses' TS2449 suppression set) needs the producer moved BEFORE the spine, and a LATER pass that retracts/edits the migrating pass's diagnostics (checkCloduleTest2 removes TS2554 at NewExpression positions) needs no move when it sits after both orders, and the spine stays at its FIXED init slot (the stable diagnostic sort hides insertion-order deltas except exact start/length/code/message ties). `spineWalkFile` visits EVERY node of EVERY file — per-frame costs are the whole game (a boxed `ArrayList<Boolean>` phase stack + a leave frame per leaf measured +1.0 s on the compiler profile; the primitive BooleanArray stack + inline-leave leaf shortcut are load-bearing — don't "simplify" them away). **The walk also maintains `spineCurrentScope` (INV.4(c)(i)) — the authoritative lexical scope backed by the INV.2(c) `lexicalScopes` tables, pushed BEFORE a node's own enter handlers (params/type-params visible to them) via a per-file nodeId→scope ARRAY (never a HashMap<Int,·> probe per node — Int-key boxing); a SwitchStatement's scope is re-keyed onto its CLAUSE nodeIds at fill so the switch EXPRESSION stays in the outer scope. A change to scope activation must keep `spineScopeChainDerivedAt` (the audit's parent-chain derivation) in sync — Inv4SpineScopeStateTest's audit mode diffs the two at every node.**
- **INV.4(c)(iii) (rounds 524–528): the spine maintains the checkUnresolvedNames family's NameScope chain (`spineUResEnter`/`spineUResLeave`, shared per-file ROOT via `unresolvedFileRootFor`) and DISPATCHES the statement-level (batch 2) + class-member (batch 3) + EXPRESSION (batch 4) + TYPE (batch 5) emissions (`spineUResDispatch`); ALL the family's recursive walkers are DELETED (statement / class-element / expression / type / JSX helpers).** Member-kind coverage is NOT uniform — `spineUResFnSigDispatch` (marks TYPE roots only since batch 5; `checkTps` false for fn-exprs/objlit-methods, whose legacy arms registered TPs without checking constraints; class-member dispatch is gated on `spineUResIsClassMember` — interface members belong to the batch-2 interface handler; widening any of these double-emits or over-emits). A NEW input to the root build must be computed BEFORE the checkSpine slot (cf. `computeTypeLibResolution`); a NEW scope-creating boundary in the still-legacy walkers must be mirrored in `spineUResEnter` (Inv4UnresolvedSpineScopeTest's per-Identifier fingerprint audit is the gate). Key traps: (1) `forEachChild` visits decorators LAST — a class level DEACTIVATES at its first Decorator child, class-method decorators get the pre-population `decoratorScope` view; TPs/params populate LAZILY at the first TypeParameter/Parameter child so computed method names and TP constraints stay signature-blind — the signature dispatch at child enters RELIES on this staging. (2) EVERY family emission site on the spine must route through `spineUResEmit` — it enforces the suppressed regions (with-statement bodies / skipped outside-function returns / `declare` fn+class), the declare-module 2304/2552-only filter, AND nulls `currentFileLocals` (the legacy pass ran unscoped; checkSpine sets it per file — an unwrapped emission resolves names through a different path). (3) `checkUnresolvedNames` is the declarationOnly-ONLY driver (`spineUResOnly` walk) — re-adding a normal-mode dispatch double-emits the whole family. (4) Since batch 2 the CLI `--listAll` raw print order is NOT position-sorted within a file — A/B listAll by SORTED error lines; the corpus baseline output stays byte-identical via the formatter's stable sort. (5) **Batch 4: expression positions self-emit at their own enters, gated by `spineUResExprChecked` — a per-file nodeId-memoized ancestor walk over `spineUResExprEdge(parent, child)` verdicts (ROOT = a position the old arms dispatched, DESCEND = an edge the old walker recursed, NONE = never reached: names, types, closing JSX tags, type-literal member names). A NEW expression-position emission or descent must be added as an EDGE in that classifier, never as a walker call (double-emission); scope comes from the maintained level chain at the node's position (switch-inactive/decorator-deactivation/lazy-population state included), and the TS2422 implements-bare-TP `continue` is the per-file `spineUResHeritageSkip` nodeId set registered at the class arm BEFORE the heritage subtree walks.** (6) **Batch 5: TYPE positions self-emit at their own enters — but unlike the expression classifier, the ROOT positions are not static: the dispatch sites MARK them (`spineUResMarkTypeRoot`, always strictly before the marked subtree walks) and `spineUResTypeChecked` walks ancestors over `spineUResTypeDescends` edges (the deleted checkUnresolvedInTypeCore recursion arms) up to a mark. A NEW type-annotation position the family should check must call `spineUResMarkTypeRoot` at its dispatch site (never a walker — there is none); a NEW type-node kind with own emissions needs a `spineUResDispatch` arm + a descent arm. The last recursion-owned expression region — a type-literal member's computed NAME — is an expression ROOT gated on `spineUResTypeChecked(typeLiteral)` (`spineUResTypeLiteralMemberNameEdge`); TL member name emissions (TS2690/TS2693/TS2464) fire in one batch at the TypeLiteral's enter.**
- **INV.4(c)(ii) (round 523): checkUnresolvedNames' NameScope queries are HYBRID — the threaded sets ∪ the `lex`-linked INV.2(c) scopes, interleaved per NameScope level (`lex` down to `parent.lex`).** Three load-bearing rules: (1) function-like scopes link at the BODY statement list ONLY (owner = the FN node passed to `checkUnresolvedInStatements`), AND the interleave walks skip NON-HEAD fn-like levels — a nested fn inside a PARAM DEFAULT links a scope whose binder parent chain crosses the outer fn's flat table (params + body decls), which was never activated on that walk path (functionLikeInParameterInitializer); fn signature content (params/TPs) is always threaded, so the skip loses nothing; (2) ModuleDeclaration/EnumDeclaration lexical levels are UNTRUSTED (skipped in `lexLevelHasName`/`lexLevelHasType`) — the binder aliases ALL merged exports while the walk export-filters (buildNamespaceScope) / EnumMember-filters, so linking them suppresses genuine TS2304; (3) the SourceFile level filters `existing` by `unresolvedLexRootExcluded` (ambient external module names + the declare-global quirk) — its scope-space `symbols` (block-hoisted file-level vars, B83.5-unbound) stay unfiltered. A population site may be skipped ONLY on a probe hit (`lexHit != null`); unindexed trees must keep the threaded path (that fallback IS the legacy behavior).
- **INV.4(c)(iv) (round 529): checkTypeUsedAsValue is ON THE SPINE — its recursive walkers + ScopeNameSet are deleted.** Reach = the memoized 3-state `spineTavStatus`/`spineTavEdge` ancestor classifier (the deleted arms verbatim: NO descent into for/while/do/switch/try bodies, class accessors/EXPRESSIONS, shorthand props, objlit-method param defaults; a plain-`=` LHS rides REACHED_NONS = TS2708 suppressed, checkConstAssignment owns it); scope = pull-based `tavLevelAt` levels (an objlit method's computed NAME skips its own level — outer view). A NEW value-position for the family is a new EDGE in `spineTavEdge` (never a walker call); TS2689 marks `spineTavHeritageSkip` at the CLASS enter so the pure-interface extends subtree is never also walked.
- **Every init-dispatch pass is wrapped `pass("checkFoo") { checkFoo() }` (INV.0, PassTiming.kt — the opt-in `--passTiming` table): wrap a NEW init-level pass the same way, keep every PassTiming hook additive-only behind `PassTiming.enabled` (false must stay behavior-free — the corpus suite + listAll A/B pin it), and `pass` stays NON-inline (~514 inline expansions of its try/finally body would push the ctor toward the JVM 64 KB method limit).**
- **A PROBE'S `if (mode != ON) return` GUARD CANNOT PROTECT ITS OWN ARGUMENT — KOTLIN EVALUATES ARGUMENTS STRICTLY, SO `census(lazyThing.size)` DOES THE WORK ON EVERY PRODUCTION COMPILE AND INV.0's "false must stay behaviour-free" IS SILENTLY UNTRUE (round 900).** `FrontEnd.addClosureCensus(reassigned.size.toLong())` materialised all 1,143 of round 801's lazy `SuffixNameSet` views for ninety-nine rounds with the probe OFF — and round 801's own census (`created 1143, materialized 1143`) was written up as "every set is eventually asked" because **the asker was the instrument**. Pass the OBJECT and derive the value AFTER the guard; and pin a lazily-materialising type with a PRODUCTION-mode compile asserting its materialisation counter stays 0, since no output diff, corpus baseline or `cost_gate.py` counter can see the difference.
- **A BACKGROUNDED `sleep` IS PREEMPTED BY YOUR NEXT COMMAND, so "I waited 25 minutes" is usually false (round 722).** Launch a wait and then END THE TURN; real time passes only while you are idle awaiting its notification. Rounds 718–721 recorded 16-, 25- and 40-minute compiles that were really ~3-minute ones, and built a whole theory (contention, a competing bench loop, failing hardware) on the broken clock — the tell is a process whose `/proc`-verified age is far younger than your supposed wait. **Also launch long builds DETACHED (`nohup … &`), log gradle output to a FILE (a `| tail` pipeline shows nothing until it ends), and stamp the script with `date` before/after so a restart is visible.**
- **A FROM-SCRATCH `compileKotlinJvm` needs 5 GB, and gradle.properties now pins it (BUILD.1, owner-approved, rounds 717–720). Do NOT lower `kotlin.daemon.jvmargs`, and do NOT `pkill -9` the Kotlin daemon.** The measured ladder on this module: 2g GC-thrashes forever (350% CPU, RSS pinned at the ceiling, ZERO class files — it looks exactly like a hang, because Kotlin's backend only writes output at the end), 3g dies mid-compile, 4g fails in ~6 min with the explicit `Not enough memory to run compilation`, 5g succeeds in ~6.5 min. `org.gradle.jvmargs` was cut 2g → 1g to make room (Gradle's own daemon peaks ~1.1 GB; 5g + 2g oversubscribed the 7.7 GB box and the kernel killed the compile daemon mid-run with nothing in any log). Why one module needs this: Checker.kt is ~110k lines in a SINGLE file plus the generated real-lib sources' 3.71 MB of embedded string constants (566 KB before round 731 shipped the DOM set), so the unit is all resident at once. **An INCREMENTAL compile takes ~2 min and needs none of it — what forces the expensive path is invalidating the caches, above all `pkill -9` of the Kotlin daemon, which is exactly what the memory-freeing ritual below tells you to do.** Prefer a graceful `pkill` (no -9), and read a suddenly-slow build as "I invalidated the caches", not "my change broke something". **AND: an IDLE daemon from the previous build squats on its heap for its 2-hour idle timeout — measured 4.33 GB held while the next build tried to start, leaving 297 MB free and starving it (round 721).** Before a build that will compile from scratch, stop the previous daemons (`./gradlew --stop`, or `kill <pid>` for the Kotlin one) — otherwise the second build fights the first one's corpse for RAM. **A `pkill -f <name>` whose pattern appears in your own waiting tasks kills THOSE too** (round 721 killed its own timers with `pkill -f gate721`); use a bracket pattern or kill by PID.
- **REVERSE-DEPENDENCY CLOSURE BUYS NOTHING ON A BARREL-EXPORTING CODEBASE — measured round 772 on the compiler profile, and it kills the "incremental compile server" idea for SELF-COMPILE.** Touching `semver.ts` (a LEAF: 3 direct dependents) makes `--watch` report `incremental recheck of 77/78 file(s)` and cost **13,529 ms**; touching `checker.ts` and `types.ts` does not qualify as incremental at all and falls back to `rebuilding…` at 12,002 / 11,208 ms. **All three are just the warm FULL-rebuild time** (~11.6 s, independently measured by `BenchMain`), and the descending trend across the three is JIT warm-up, not closure size. Cause: tsc's own sources are `export *` barrels, so every file transitively depends on every other and INV.7(d1)'s closure has nothing to bite on. **Do NOT price a compile-server/incremental design off the closure machinery existing** — the prize is warm-JVM reuse (26.5 s cold → 11.6 s warm) and nothing more. This is a statement about THIS corpus, not all projects: a well-layered app with real module locality could still benefit, so re-measure before reusing the verdict.
- **NATIVE BUILDS ARE RE-AUTHORIZED ON THIS BOX (owner, 2026-08-04, on a 8-core / 15.6 GB VPS) — SUPERSEDING the round-775 "never run a Kotlin/Native or GraalVM build here" directive, which was issued for a 4-core / 7.7 GB host after one froze it for ~2 HOURS.** What has NOT changed and is still the whole hazard: **swap is ZERO** (`free -m` → `Swap: 0`), so memory over-commit does not degrade into paging — the kernel reclaims file-backed pages including running processes' text, and the box stalls instead of OOM-killing cleanly. The konan/LLVM backend and the linked native executable are separate processes outside every JVM heap, so no `-Xmx` bounds them. **Therefore, before any native task: `./gradlew --stop` + a graceful bracket-pattern `pkill -f 'KotlinCompile[D]aemon'` (an idle Kotlin daemon squats on ~3.8-5 GB for its 2-hour timeout), then watch `free -m`; run native tasks ALONE, never beside a suite run or a bench.** K/N compiles inside the GRADLE daemon, so its heap is `org.gradle.jvmargs` — see the round-772 entry below. Native is opt-in via `-PenableNativeTargets=true` (the linuxX64 target does not register without it, so a plain `./gradlew build` never sees it); CI still runs it in `.github/workflows/native.yml`. The JVM gates (`./gradlew jvmTest`, `compileKotlinJvm`, `scripts/cost_gate.py`) are routine and unaffected — one at a time, as always.
- **KOTLIN/NATIVE COMPILES INSIDE THE *GRADLE* DAEMON, SO ITS HEAP IS `org.gradle.jvmargs` — NOT `kotlin.daemon.jvmargs`, AND THERE IS NO `kotlin.native.jvmArgs` (round 772, after round 771 concluded the opposite and was WRONG).** [Kotlin's own tips page](https://kotlinlang.org/docs/native-improving-compilation-time.html) states it: "Kotlin/Native runs from the Gradle daemon … the same JVM process is used". BUILD.1 cut `org.gradle.jvmargs` 2g → 1g to make room for the 5 GB *Kotlin* daemon, **so the JVM-compile memory fix is exactly what starves Kotlin/Native** — `compileKotlinLinuxX64` OOMs at the inherited 1g no matter what you pass to the Kotlin daemon. With `-Dorg.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx6g` it SUCCEEDS in **2m04s**, and `compileTestKotlinLinuxX64` in a further 23s. **BUT `-Xmx6g` ON THIS BOX WILL FREEZE IT, AND THE OWNER FEELS IT — THIS MACHINE HAS ZERO SWAP (`free -m` → `Swap: 0`), so memory over-commit does not degrade gracefully into paging: the box stalls, becomes unresponsive for minutes, and the kernel OOM-kills something.** Before any ≥5g experiment: `./gradlew --stop` AND `pkill -f 'KotlinCompile[D]aemon'` first (an idle Kotlin daemon squats on **3.76 GB** measured, for its 2-hour timeout, so 6g + 3.76g on 7.7g is an instant stall), then watch `free -m`. The OOM-killer leaves NOTHING greppable in a build log — its signature is `Gradle build daemon disappeared unexpectedly` or a shell that simply dies — and `dmesg` is not readable as non-root here, so process forensics (`ps -eo rss --sort=-rss`) is the only diagnosis. Round 771's "K/N cannot compile this module on a ~8 GB box, a HARDWARE limit" is **retracted**: it tuned a property nothing reads (`-Pkotlin.native.jvmArgs`) and misread the resulting 1g failures as a 5–6g ceiling. **The LINK is no longer unmet (round 822 supersedes this half): on the 15.6 GB box `linuxX64Test` compiles, LINKS and RUNS the whole suite green in 25m33s — 13,689 tests, 0 failures.** What made it fit is heap going DOWN, not up: 6g was never the floor, `gradle.properties` now pins the measured **4g**, and the link gets the difference. Round 772's ~22-min `Gradle build daemon disappeared unexpectedly` was the OS OOM-killer (not a Java OOM — nothing greppable in the log) and remains the signature to expect if that headroom is spent again. Also true and independently useful: **do NOT pass `--no-daemon`** — it forces the compile into Gradle's own JVM and fails differently again.
- **THERE ARE THREE AOT/WARM ARTIFACT POINTS AND THE QUEUE CONFLATES TWO OF THEM: the "native 13.4 s" figure quoted at (JIT.2a) and (INV.7b) is the *GraalVM native-image*, and Kotlin/Native has never been near it — K/N release measures 21.8 s (round 772) and 20.0 s (round 823), i.e. 1.26x a cold JVM against Graal's 1.97x.** There are now FIVE points — cold JVM ~25-26 s, warm `--serve` 11.9 s, GraalVM 13.4 s, K/N release 20.0 s, and the JDK 25 AOT cache (round 828, 1.638x its OWN round's plain arm); `docs/perf/aot-native-image.md` § 0 is the index and only within-round paired deltas compare. **ROUND 841: the GraalVM point is no longer a one-box datum — CI builds and benches the image on EVERY push since 2026-07-30, and over 75 rows x 2 modes the native arm is `1.02x` tsc (check-only 1.04x, emit 1.01x) while the JVM arm is 2.51x/2.25x. So "xtsc is ~2.4x tsc" is a claim about the JVM ARM, not about the compiler — always name the arm as well as the mode (§ 0a is the authority).** **K/N is a REACH artifact (26 MiB, no JVM, the corpus runs natively), never a speed one** — do not price a shipping decision off the wrong backend's number.
- **A JDK AOT/CDS CACHE CANNOT BE DUMPED FROM AN EXPLODED CLASS DIRECTORY (`Cannot have non-empty directory in paths`, after burning a full compile first), so `ab-interleaved.sh` / `ab-warm.sh` / `bench-compile-tsc.sh` — all of which run `-cp build/classes/kotlin/jvm/main` — are structurally unable to carry an AOT arm: both arms must run from `build/libs/*-jvm-*.jar` (round 828).** The mirror trap is worse: **the JVM NEVER invalidates a stale cache** — a class DELETED from the jar still loads out of it (`NoClassDefFoundError` without the cache, `OK` with it) under changed size, fresh mtime and `-XX:AOTMode=on` alike — so a cache outliving its jar silently runs old bytecode, and only DUMPING refuses a directory (at runtime the cache is served to an exploded-dir classpath too). `-Xlog:class+load=info` (`source: shared objects file`) is the only probe that separates "cache opened" from "cache used"; `-Xlog:aot=info` says `Opened AOT cache` even in the broken cases.
- **AT RUNTIME AN AOT CACHE IS SERVED TO ANY CLASSPATH THAT HAS THE DUMP-TIME ONE AS A *PREFIX* — that, not a purpose-built dump, is how the SHIPPED cache gets validated by the whole corpus suite (`scripts/aot-corpus-suite.sh`, round 839: trained jar + its 7 deps in that exact order, test classes appended), and it is why Gradle's test worker can never carry an AOT arm (Gradle builds the worker's classpath).** Two traps in that harness: a suite driven outside Gradle needs `kotlin-power-assert-runtime-jvm` (a separate artifact from the stdlib, absent from `build/bench/cp.txt`) or **4,998 tests die with `NoClassDefFoundError: kotlin/powerassert/CallExplanation` — identically in BOTH arms**, i.e. a same-in-both-arms result is not automatically a valid one; and the ~10 pins that read the classpath LAYOUT (`AotCacheGuardTest`, `HugeMethodLimitTest`) fail there by construction because the run is from a jar.
- **AN AOT BINARY'S RUN-TO-RUN SPREAD IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY TIGHT, AND n=3 CANNOT SHOW THAT IT IS (round 823 vs round 772).** Round 772 read 0.2% over three K/N runs and generalised it to "what an AOT binary with no warm-up should look like"; five interleaved runs measure **11.3%**, *wider* than the JVM's 9.2% in the same interleave. The box's ±13% swing (CLAUDE.md, `--workers` entry) applies to native runs exactly as to JVM ones.
- **The generated real-lib sources MUST stay split across `RealLibFilesPart*.kt` (~250 KB each) — merging them back into one file breaks the BUILD, not the tests (round 731).** All 3.71 MB of `src/lib` in one generated file with one `buildMap { }` lambda fails `compileKotlinJvm` with the BUILD.1 message `Not enough memory to run compilation` after 7m34s at the pinned 5 GB; split, a COLD compile takes 2m25s — so the single big file was itself the memory cost, and 3.71 MB in pieces is CHEAPER than the old 566 KB in one. The facade cuts the concatenation apart by recorded CHAR lengths, so `libNames`/`libLengths` must stay declared BEFORE `files` (object properties initialize in source order), and the generator wipes its output dir first (a shrinking pin would otherwise leave a stale part file that still compiles in).
- **Running a `-Xmx4g` self-compile (`MainKt --noEmit`) MANUALLY (not via the bench script) while the Gradle daemon (~1.8 GB) + KotlinCompileDaemon (~2.7 GB) are up exceeds the 7.7 GB box → swap → apparent "hang"/timeout that looks like a code perf bug but is NOT.** Free memory first: `./gradlew --stop && pkill -9 -f KotlinCompileDaemon` (verify with `free -m`, want ≥5 GB free), THEN run. A stubbed-to-no-op helper that STILL times out is the tell it's memory, not your code.
- **Do NOT run `compileKotlinJvm` while a background self-compile A/B (`MainKt` on the bench project) is in flight** — the recompile rewrites class files the running JVM lazily loads → `ClassNotFoundException` mid-run (looks like a compiler crash). Concurrent CLI RUNS are safe; only recompiles clobber.
- **`propTypeContainsLiteral` is NOT a display predicate — ~25 consumers, and several choose `literalTypeOfExpression` over `getTypeOfExpression` for the SOURCE type of an assignability check.** Widening it (round 745 admitted `TypeFlags.EnumLike`) silently rewrites what the RELATION is asked: `literalTypeOfExpression` answers `undefinedType` for the identifier `undefined` and knows nothing about a following `!`, so `x = undefined!` began rejecting (4 profile FPs at parser.ts:1790). Use `ts2322KeepsSourceLiteral` for a message-only change, and a construction-restricted helper (cf. `enumNumericLiteralSource`) when a site genuinely needs the literal source type.
- **`Type.Reference` interning**: Construction goes through `getOrInternReference(target, args)` (CheckerState `referenceCache` keyed on `target.id|arg1.id,arg2.id,...`). Identical instantiations share an instance and `Type.id`, which is what the id-based cycle detection in `relationComparisonStack` relies on. Do NOT call `Type.Reference(...)` directly in new code.
- **An enum MEMBER has had its own `Type` since round 741 ((REL.1)(a)) — `Type.Object(Object or EnumLiteral)` interned on `"<canonicalEnumSymbol.id>#<memberName>"` — and its symbol carries `SymbolFlags.EnumMember`, NOT `SymbolFlags.Enum`.** Two consequences a new classifier gets wrong by default: (1) every "an enum value is a number/string at runtime, never an object" rule must accept BOTH flags — use `isEnumFlavoredObjectType`, since testing `SymbolFlags.Enum` alone let `ModuleKind.ESNext` survive a `typeof x === "object"` narrow (program.ts:1341 TS2339); (2) `isNumericEnumObjectType`/`isStringEnumObjectType` are symbol-flag-keyed and therefore BLIND to member types, which is why the enum-member ↔ base-primitive legs in `isSimpleTypeRelatedTo` exist separately. Those legs are VALUE-AWARE since round 745 ((REL.1)(c)) — `numericLiteralFitsEnum` — and `checkEnumLiteralAssignments` is DELETED; a second value judgement anywhere would DOUBLE-EMIT. **A numeric-literal rejection needs a domain that is COMPLETE, which is `enumValueDomainIsComplete`, and its non-obvious clause is that a member with NO initializer in an AMBIENT non-const enum has no value in tsc (`declare enum D { X, Y }; let d: D = 7` is legal) while WE auto-number it for the Transformer — never read `enumValues` directly to reject.** Mint through `getDeclaredTypeOfEnumMember` only — a per-symbol mint splits one member into two non-equal types (the same enum arrives as the merged global, a file-local and a barrel-resolved alias). **Since round 742 ((REL.1)(b0)) an enum-member ACCESS EXPRESSION (`E.A` in value position) resolves to that type too, not `anyType` — so any new rule reached by an enum-member expression must WIDEN it back to the enum (`widenEnumMemberTypes`, which also distributes over a union) wherever a `let`/inference position is involved. There is more than one widener: `widenType` is NOT the only one — `checkVarDeclAssignabilityCore` has its own inline literal-widener, and that one is what the TS2322 assignment check reads as the target's declared type, so a widening rule added only to `widenType` silently fails on `let flags = E.None; flags = E.Other`.**
- **An `anyType` operand SHORT-CIRCUITS the arithmetic/comparison pass entirely (`if (leftType === anyType || rightType === anyType) return`), so removing an `any` anywhere unmasks whatever was already wrong on the OTHER operand (round 742).** Both round-742 unmaskings were pre-existing bugs, not new ones: `isComparableType` never resolved a TYPE PARAMETER's constraint (the sibling rule had always been in `isValidArithmeticOperand`), and the pass's first-wins local recording refused a nested body's own `const x = …` when an ENCLOSING body's `let x: T` was still in the INHERITED locals map. The pass copies its locals map per scope frame, so "already recorded" does NOT mean "recorded here" — ask `spineArithInheritedName` (instance identity against the innermost saved map) before letting first-wins refuse a shadow.

### Test assertion gotchas

- **A MEMO OVER A BUILD CAN ONLY SERVE A STALE ANSWER WHEN ITS KEY IS BYTE-IDENTICAL ACROSS THE CHANGE — SO A STALENESS PIN THAT EDITS THE QUERIED FILE IS VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION, BECAUSE THE EDIT MOVES THE SPAN AND THEREFORE THE KEY (2026-08-22, (INC.12)).** Keep the caret's own file untouched and edit something ELSE: another file's content (the answer must move), and — the direction CLAUDE.md already names as the dangerous one — an edit that ADDS A FILE to the program, where a mis-keyed hit is a MISSING FILE and not a wrong type. `ProjectCaptureMemoTest` is the reference pair.
- **AND TWO LANGUAGE-SERVICE FEATURES CAN BUILD THE *IDENTICAL* `TypeCaptureRequest`, WHICH IS WHY THE REQUEST IS THE RIGHT MEMO KEY AND THE CARET IS NOT (2026-08-22).** `quickInfoAt` and `definitionsAt` at one caret name the same single span and read different channels of the one answer; `documentHighlightsAt`'s request is derived from the FILE's occurrence nodes with the caret used only to pick the seed AFTERWARDS; and a ONE-CARET `semanticsAt` batch asks exactly what hover asks. All three hits fall out of one map and none is special-cased — but it also means a build-COUNT pin is measuring the memo unless it re-dirties between rows.
- **A COUNT OF *ALL* Vfs READS IS NOT A COUNT OF BUILDS — SOME COMPILER CACHE WARMS ACROSS BUILDS INSIDE ONE JVM AND TAKES A SOURCE READ WITH IT, SO A SIX-BUILD LOOP READ **29** TOUCHES ONCE AND **30** ON EVERY RERUN (round 914).** The stable unit is reads of the project's `tsconfig.json` — exactly one per `ProjectCompiler.build`, cached nowhere across them — and a control pin (three dirty builds -> three) is what makes that a measurement rather than an assumption. A sum-based build counter is order-dependent, i.e. the pin that reddens for a reason the round cannot name.

- **A `.tsx`/`.jsx` FIXTURE CANNOT PIN THAT `computeParserFlags` WAS CONSULTED, AND NEITHER CAN A TOP-LEVEL `await` — BOTH MEASURED, BOTH VACUOUS (round 910).** `Parser.isJsxFile` is `forceJsx || fileName.endsWith(".tsx") || fileName.endsWith(".jsx")`, so a `.tsx` parses JSX from its EXTENSION whatever flags it is handed (the flag computed for it, `needsJsxFlag`, drives a diagnostic, not the grammar); and our parser produces an `AwaitExpression` with `topLevelAwait = false` too. **The one option-derived GRAMMAR difference is `forceJsx` on a plain `.js` file** (`allowJs` or `jsx` set) — pin against that or the assertion is measuring nothing.

- **AN IN-MEMORY FIXTURE PINNING MODULE RESOLUTION MEASURES NOTHING UNLESS ITS tsconfig SETS AN **ES `module` KIND** AND THE PROGRAM CARRIES **>= 2 FILES** — AND IT FAILS IN THE SILENT DIRECTION, AS AN EMPTY DIAGNOSTIC LIST (round 909, three tests green before it was found).** Two independent gates: (i) the unresolved-module region returns early on `binderResults.size <= 1 && !isMultiFileSource` (`Checker.kt:45409`, `:45853`) and **the real libs bind through their own path and do not count**, so a two-file fixture whose second file IS the missing import reduces to ONE program file and reads as "TS2307 does not exist"; (ii) the relative-specifier leg additionally demands `options.module in ES_MODULE_KINDS` (`:46098` — ES2015/ES2020/ES2022/ESNext/Preserve only) with `moduleResolution`/`paths`/`baseUrl`/`rootDir(s)`/`moduleSuffixes` all unset, so a tsconfig carrying only `target`/`strict` leaves `module` unset and **every "the import is unresolved" assertion is vacuous**. node16/nodenext/bundler are deliberately unmodelled. Any pin about imports needs a NEGATIVE CONTROL that the diagnostic fires when the module is genuinely absent, or it is measuring its own vacuity.

- **A PassLab ABLATION CANNOT REACH A FIXTURE BUILT BY A DIRECT `Checker(options, results)` CONSTRUCTION — `PassLab.ensureLoaded()` lives in `runWithDeepStack`, which that harness never crosses, so the pin runs UN-ABLATED and reads GREEN against a binary whose pass is disabled (round 860).** Worse, it is ORDER-dependent: the lab loads on the first `diagnose()` in the JVM, so the same pin passes or fails depending on where JUnit schedules it. Wrap the multi-file harness in `runWithDeepStack { … }` (what production does) before believing any "this pass is not what emits it" verdict.


- **IN A SMALL FIXTURE ONLY A BARE EXPRESSION-STATEMENT PROPERTY ACCESS (`x.p;`) HAS A *FIRST* NARROWING WALK — in an argument or a `return`, an earlier pass already walked that reference, the round-664 inter-walk memo serves it, `narrowWalkLaunches` does not move, and every round-790-style "did the walk really run" bracket answers UNKNOWN (round 794).** Six candidate fixtures read `loopFree = 0` before that was understood, which would have made a whole pin population EMPTY and its verifier assertions vacuously true. Assert the population is non-empty (`assert(loopFree > 0)`) in any pin that measures over such a bracket.
- **An un-annotated PARAMETER is invisible to the body walkers no matter what its SIGNATURE says: they read `currentLocalTypes`, filled by `populateParameterLocalTypes`, whose first gate is `paramType != null` — writing `symbolTypes[param.id]` in `getTypeOfArrowFunction` (where `applyContextualParameterTypes` lives) has NO effect on TS2339/TS7006/etc. (round 694).** Contextual parameter typing that must reach the body — IIFE arguments, and any future source of parameter types — belongs in `populateParameterLocalTypes`. The probe that detects the mistake must FAIL if the change works (`((a) => a.nope)("x")` must become TS2339); a probe that merely watches the target case stay quiet cannot tell an inert change from a working one.
- **A `const k = <expr> as unknown as E` SUBJECT IS INVISIBLE AT THE ARGUMENT GATE — for a FILE-LEVEL enum too — so a narrowing pin written that way passes on a broken build for a reason unrelated to the fix (round 782, two pins).** Make the subject a PARAMETER (`function inner(k: E)`). General form: validate a probe SHAPE against a control whose answer is already known before using it to measure anything; a silence-asserting pin on an unobservable shape measures nothing.
- **A PRICE OR RESIDUAL MEASURED ON A SUBSET OF THE 8 PROFILES IS A CLAIM ABOUT THAT SUBSET ONLY (round 782).** Rounds 777-781 each quoted a scaffolded price as `compiler N -> M, services N -> M` and concluded "the residual is empty"; the 16th site lived in `src/harness` and was structurally invisible to all four. Run the full 8-profile grid before declaring a family closed — profile-unique files (harness/server/services) are exactly where a last straggler hides.
- **A NARROWING pin whose EXPECTED union happens to equal a type ALIAS declared in the same file reads the ALIAS NAME, not the members (round 764).** Unions are interned and carry `aliasDisplayMap`'s name, so with a `type U = K.A | K.B | K.C` in the prelude, `k !== K.D` reports `Argument of type 'U'` — the pin then measures the alias table instead of what it was written for. Choose a subtraction whose survivors match no declared alias (removing a MIDDLE member also tests member ORDER, which removing the last one cannot).
- **A NARROWING probe must assign to an INCOMPATIBLE type and read the narrowed type out of the error message — "no error here" cannot tell correct narrowing from a reference washed to `never` (round 760).** `never` is assignable to everything, so an over-narrowing is SILENT at every use except a call argument, and its symptom surfaces far away as the DECLARED type ("Argument of type 'Node' …"), which reads like no narrowing happened at all rather than too much. `const s: string = x` after the guard prints `Type 'Node'`/`Type 'Ident'`/nothing-at-all (= `never`) and discriminates all three in one run; the same trick reads any resolved member type (`const s: string = m.kind`).
- **SINCE THE MOD MODULE SPLIT THE TEST XMLs ARE PER-MODULE — `build/test-results/jvmTest/*.xml` AT THE REPO ROOT MATCHES NOTHING, SO THE DOCUMENTED COUNTING SNIPPET PRINTS `0 0 0`, WHICH IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A SUITE THAT NEVER RAN (round 846).** Glob `*/build/test-results/jvmTest/*.xml` and SUM the four modules (core + api + client + daemon); core alone is ~99.5% of the count, so a core-only read looks plausible and is short by ~75 tests.
- **WIPE THE WHOLE `build/test-results/jvmTest` DIR, NEVER JUST `/binary` — the documented incantation was changed to the whole dir; do not "restore" the `/binary` form (rounds 638 + 690).** `/binary` clears the binary results but NOT the per-class XMLs, so a script globbing `build/test-results/jvmTest/*.xml` sums the PREVIOUS run's files too. That is harmless only while the test SET is stable, and it lies silently the moment a change adds or removes tests — a removed test keeps "failing" from its stale XML, and a COUNT that should have moved and did not means the results dir, not the change. Worse for an AWAITED run (round 638): gradle deletes the stale XMLs only when the test task starts, i.e. after the multi-minute compile, so a count-based wait "succeeds" on stale results while the new suite is still compiling. The whole-dir wipe makes any XML seen later fresh by construction.
- **Hand-written test conventions (round-437 sweep, 2026-07-07) — new local tests MUST follow them**: compile via the shared `diagnose(source, directives = "// @strict: true", fileName = "t.ts")` helper (CompilerTestSupport.kt; trimIndents the source and prepends the directives line) — never a per-file `TypeScriptCompiler().compile(...)` helper (EXCEPTION: a test asserting SYMBOL IDENTITY may construct `Checker(options, binderResults)` directly — cf. Inv3PerFileLookupTest — but its fixture fileNames MUST be path-shaped (`/proj/c.ts`): flat corpus-style names silently defeat directory-relative module-specifier resolution, so every import resolves null); assert via the `should`/`have` idiom (`diagnose(...) should { have(none/any { it.code == NNNN }) }`, com.xemantic.kotlin.test) or power-assert `assert(actual == expected)` — never `assertEquals`/`assertTrue(d.isEmpty(), msg)` (round 682 converted 792 `assertEquals` call sites; `assert` and `have` are the ONLY power-assert-transformed functions per build.gradle.kts, and they take NO message — the diagram renders every subexpression value, which beats any hand-written one). TWO traps when writing `assert(a == b)`: an empty-collection comparison must be `assert(xs.isEmpty())`, since `== emptyList()` gives the compiler no way to infer the element type; and an AST-valued subexpression must stay on `assertEquals` (next entry); sources are multiline templates (not buildString), class-shared TS preludes are a trimIndented `private val` concatenated by the caller (`diagnose(prelude + """…""")`), test names are backtick sentences (dots replaced — see the Kotlin backtick-name rule above; use `negative control - …` phrasing for negative tests).
- **Never put an AST node / node list / SourceFile subexpression inside a power-assert-transformed assertion (`have`/`assert` from com.xemantic.kotlin.test)** — on failure the diagram toStrings every captured subexpression, which for a data-class node dumps (or, on deep chains, STACK-OVERFLOWS rendering) whole subtrees, masking the real failure as SOE/OOM. Compute ints/booleans into locals first, or use plain `kotlin.test.fail` with a hand-built message (cf. ForEachChildOracleTest). This is why the round-682 `assertEquals` sweep DELIBERATELY left 27 sites on `assertEquals` — every site in Inv2NodeIndexTest / Inv2LexicalScopeTest / ForEachChildOracleTest whose compared value is a node, a `.copy()`, a `preorder(...)` element or a lexical scope. A `List<Diagnostic>` or a `CompilationResult` is SAFE to render (strings + diagnostics, no AST); a `SourceFile`/`NodeBase`/`Symbol`/lexical scope is not. **This is the ONLY sanctioned exception to the one-idiom rule above** — the 65 surviving `kotlin.test` calls are ALL in the six AST-centric files (Inv2LexicalScopeTest, Inv2NodeIndexTest, Inv3NodeKeyedLookupTest, Inv3PerFileLookupTest, RealLibSnapshotTest, ForEachChildOracleTest); a `grep assertEquals src/` hit outside those files is drift to convert, not precedent to copy.
- **ONE assertion idiom in tests: power-assert `assert(<boolean expr>)` (com.xemantic.kotlin.test) — do NOT reintroduce `kotlin.test.assertEquals`/`assertTrue`/`assertFalse`/`assertNull`/`assertNotNull`/`assertSame`/`assertContains` (round 682 converted 1,009 call sites; only `assert` and `have` are power-assert-transformed per build.gradle.kts `powerAssert.functions`).** They take NO message — the diagram renders every subexpression value, which strictly beats a hand-written one (a broken pin prints the operand value, the `false` verdict AND the receiver's contents). Translations: `assertEquals(exp, act)` → `assert(act == exp)` (ACTUAL first); `assertTrue(c)` → `assert(c)`; `assertFalse(c)` → `assert(!c)` (parenthesise if `c` has a top-level operator, else `!` mis-binds); `assertNull(x)` → `assert(x == null)`; `assertSame(exp, act)` → `assert(act === exp)`; `assertContains(coll, e)` → `assert(e in coll)`; `assertNotNull(x)` → `assert(x != null)` — and in VALUE position (`val v = assertNotNull(expr)`) split into `val v = expr` + `assert(v != null)`: `assert` carries `contract { returns() implies actual }`, so `v` SMART-CASTS to non-null afterwards (no `!!` needed). FOUR traps: an empty-collection compare must be `assert(xs.isEmpty())` (`== emptyList()` gives the compiler no element type to infer); `kotlin.assert` is a different function (see the Kotlin/Native entry above); an AST-valued subexpression must stay on `assertEquals`/`assertNotNull` (next entry); and **a SCRIPTED conversion may re-flow the argument EXPRESSIONS but must NEVER re-flow their string literals** (round 684: whitespace-normalising every rewritten argument collapsed the needle `"…\n    // second comment line"` — whose four spaces ARE the emit indentation the pin exists to check — into one space). Audit any scripted sweep by diffing the multiset of string literals before/after and accounting for EVERY disappearance; the only expected ones are the dropped messages.
- Avoid partial `assert("x" in result)` — always assert the full expected output.
- **A PIN ON AN ARM'S OWN DIAGNOSTIC CODE CANNOT SEE A DROPPED *SUPPRESSION* — when the failure mode ADDS a diagnostic rather than removing one, the seam pin must assert a DIFFERENT code that must NOT appear (round 812).** Both discriminating seams of the `checkDuplicateDeclarations` split fail by letting a SUPERSEDED check run: the arm's own count pin (TS2395 x2 / TS2813 x2) stays green under the ablation and only `none { it.code == 2300 }` moves. Ask what the dropped signal was PROTECTING, and pin that.
- **A SUPPRESSION SEAM CAN BE PROVABLY UNOBSERVABLE, WHICH IS A STRONGER ANSWER THAN A FAILED RETRY (round 812).** If the predicate that reaches an early exit is the term-by-term COMPLEMENT of every guard below it, no input can observe the exit — read the guards before building a purpose-built retry, and record the pin as an ARM pin.
- **A NARROWING pin that asserts a TS2339 APPEARS discriminates NOTHING — it passes on a working AND a broken build, for opposite reasons (round 751).** `case K.Alpha: return x.b` errors both when narrowing correctly REMOVED `B` (missing on `A`) and when narrowing never ran at all (missing on `A | B`); two such pins passed on the pristine build they were written to fail on. Assert the NEGATIVE branch instead — `default: return x.b` with `none { it.code == 2339 }` — which only a build that actually subtracted the constituent can satisfy. Same rule for any "the filter removed something" pin: assert what becomes LEGAL, not what stays broken.
- **A memo whose decision reads MUTABLE state cannot be validated by inspecting its answers — a frozen-wrong entry looks exactly like a correct one (round 751).** `canonicalEnumSymbol` freezes per `sym.id` a verdict depending on `globals[name]`/`enumValues[global.id]`, and a first touch before the enum's values exist returns `sym` itself — i.e. reports `canonical(sym) === sym`, which is indistinguishable from a genuinely-canonical symbol, so round 750's "153 canonical, 0 redirected" was ALSO what a fully-frozen cache would print. The only probe that can see it recomputes the decision with the memo BYPASSED and diffs against the memoized answer (measured 0 divergences round 751). Applies to any order-dependent memo in the checker, not just this one.
- **A SEAM PIN IS ONLY A SEAM PIN ONCE IT HAS FAILED AGAINST THE MISTAKE IT NAMES — AN AMBIENT REINSTALL UPSTREAM CAN MAKE A WHOLE CLASS OF SEAMS UNOBSERVABLE (round 806).** `withCpaFrameAmbient`/`withCcetFrameAmbient` save and restore `currentLocalTypes` around EVERY per-statement anchor dispatch, so a scope restore dropped inside a checker walker's arm is ERASED at the statement boundary: two pins asserting "the next STATEMENT sees the outer binding again" stayed green against a binary with those restores deleted. Such a leak is observable only WITHIN one statement (a later argument of the same call, a later object-literal property, the comma operator). Corollary measured the same round: **a shape validated with the project CLI is not automatically a valid pin** — `diagnose()` uses the EMBEDDED lib and the CLI uses the real ones, so a shape that discriminated 1-vs-0 under the CLI can be silent as a test.
- **DO NOT PIN A KNOWN-OPEN GAP AS A "control" — it is a countdown, not a guard (round 765, one suite failure).** Round 764 pinned "a switch `default:` still answers the whole enum" so the gap would read as measured; round 765 closed the gap and the pin failed by construction. Record an open gap in the SESSION NOTE instead. The corollary for the closing round: **the filtered batch must include the test class of every previous round whose session note named the shape you are fixing** — a batch of corpus letters plus your own new class will not catch it, because the stale pin is in neither.
- **Test ordering sensitivity**: Adding new checker code that increases diagnostics can cause deterministic failures in unrelated JS emit tests — tests pass individually but fail in the full suite. Always run the full suite to verify no regressions.

### Kotlin idioms


- **A `when (value) { null, SOME_OBJECT, false -> … }` COMPILES TO A CHAIN OF `Intrinsics.areEqual` CALLS, AND IT IS INVISIBLE IN THE SOURCE — `jsTruthy`'s five-case `when` was **5.0% of a benchmark's samples**, spent asking whether a value equals `false` (2026-08-21).** A `when { value == null -> …; value is Boolean -> … }` form decides the same cases for a type test each. Only a leaf profile names it; the equality `when` reads as the tidier of the two.
- **NEVER key a HashMap/HashSet by an AST NODE with a large subtree (FunctionDeclaration, class/namespace bodies) — AST nodes are Kotlin data classes, so `hashCode()`/`equals()` DEEP-recurse the whole subtree on every lookup** (round 471: a `HashMap<FunctionDeclaration, String>` in buildNestedFunctionMap cost the services self-compile +10 s, 39 → 51 s; replaced by cluster-order-aligned file lists). The existing `builtinLibDecls`/`builtinLibMemberDecls` node-sets survive because lib decl subtrees are small — not a licence. Key by `Symbol.id`/`Type.id`/pos-based keys, or keep parallel lists aligned to an existing order.

- **Kotlin's `mutableMapOf()`/`mutableSetOf()` return LinkedHashMap/LinkedHashSet — ordered, so they pay `LinkedHashMap.afterNodeInsertion` on every put and an ordered copy on construction (round 483: ~5% of the compiler self-compile self-time was `afterNodeInsertion`).** On a HOT, order-INDEPENDENT map/set — especially one COPIED per scope entry — write `HashMap()`/`HashSet()` (and `HashMap(other)`/`HashSet(other)` for copies, NOT `.toMutableMap()`/`.toMutableSet()`, which always return the linked variant). The per-function-body scope structures (`currentLocalTypes`, `currentLocalDeclTypeNodes`, `currentShadowedNames`, `currentParamBindingNames`) and `getUnionType`'s dedup set are HashMap/HashSet for exactly this reason. **Before converting, PROVE order is unused**: grep the field for `.keys/.values/.entries/.forEach/.map/.iterator/.first/.joinToString/.sorted` — a map whose iteration feeds DISPLAY or DIAGNOSTIC output (member order, union display) MUST stay ordered, so this is a per-field audit, not a blanket sweep.
- **The build is WARNING-CLEAN (0 compiler warnings; the 2026-07-04 sweep cleared 1,025) — KEEP IT THAT WAY: fix every new compiler `w:` the moment it appears, never let them accumulate.** `extraWarnings = true` + `progressiveMode = true` (build.gradle.kts) mean some warnings become HARD ERRORS on a Kotlin bump (any "will become an error in language version 2.4" note), and a warning routinely flags a REAL BUG — the sweep found `minArgumentCount` was always 0 because `it.questionToken == null` compares a `Boolean` (never null). Check with `./gradlew compileKotlinJvm compileTestKotlinJvm --rerun-tasks 2>&1 | grep -a '^w:'` (needs `--rerun-tasks`; Gradle caches by content hash and does NOT re-emit warnings on an up-to-date compile). Every fix must be behavior-preserving — gate on the full corpus suite; `USELESS_CAST`/redundant-`!!`/redundant-`?.` are identical static types (safe), but "always true/false" and unused-var removals can drop a load-bearing smart-cast or side effect (a bare `x as T` statement smart-casts `x`), so compile + suite after each batch.
- **`*/` inside KDoc terminates the comment**: A `/** ... */` doc comment ends at the first `*/`. Embedding `*/` (e.g. when documenting a JSDoc pattern) silently breaks the rest of the declaration — a data-class body following a broken doc comment surfaces as a cascade of confusing errors like "Modifier 'override' is not applicable to 'local variable'", "No parameter with name 'pos' found", "Unresolved reference 'jsDocTagEnd'". Reword to "comment-close" or escape as `*\/`.
- **Kotlin block comments NEST — a literal `/**` *inside* a KDoc opens a nested comment level (B151)**: writing the JSDoc string `/** @overload ... */` inside a `/** ... */` doc comment makes the inner `/**` open a NESTED block comment, so the doc comment's closing `*/` only drops back to depth 1 and the comment runs to EOF → `Syntax error: Unclosed comment` at the last line + a cascade of "Unresolved reference" errors for class members BEFORE the doc comment (the whole class fails to parse). This is DISTINCT from the `*/`-closes-early gotcha above: here it's the `/**` opener that bites. When documenting JSDoc tag syntax in a KDoc, never write a literal `/**` (or `/*`) — describe it as "a JSDoc comment" or use single-line `//` comments. **WORSE, SILENT variant (round 390, build.gradle.kts): a path GLOB in a KDoc (e.g. writing src-slash-lib-slash-star-dot-d-dot-ts) also contains the nested-comment opener — and when a LATER declaration's `*/` happens to re-balance the depth, the file COMPILES with a silently-DEAD region: tasks/vals declared inside it simply don't exist ("Task not found", top-level statements never run), and even a syntax error appended inside the swallowed span "builds successfully". If a build-script region mysteriously does not execute, scan comment-nesting depth before anything else.**
- **No non-stdlib dependencies in `commonMain`**: The project targets Kotlin Native (in addition to JVM/JS), so `commonMain` must use only `kotlin.*` and `kotlinx.*` packages. No `java.*`, no `BigDecimal`, no JVM-only types. Use Kotlin's built-in numeric types and stdlib math (`kotlin.math.*`).
- **Enum context resolution** (Kotlin 2.1+): When the expected type is an enum, use unqualified entry names — write `Equals`, not `SyntaxKind.Equals`. This applies to `when` branch conditions, named arguments (`operator = Equals`), comparisons (`flags == VarKeyword`), and any other position where the enum type is inferred. Caveat: if a data class has the same name as an enum entry (e.g. `LabeledStatement`), keep the `SyntaxKind.` prefix to avoid ambiguity.
- **No JVM-only APIs in `commonMain`**: `Map.putIfAbsent` → use `getOrPut`; `Math.pow` → use `kotlin.math.pow` extension; `RegexOption.DOT_MATCHES_ALL` → use `[\s\S]` in the pattern (B259). `./gradlew compileKotlinJvm` does NOT catch these — it compiles only the JVM target. Always use Kotlin stdlib equivalents for multiplatform compatibility.

## AI agent mission


**Phase 17: Self-compile the TypeScript compiler, then performance** (owner directive 2026-07-03, re-scoping 2026-07-02's "any TypeScript project": fully compile the TypeScript compiler ITSELF, then optimize performance — the v1 exit is all 8 tsc-source profiles at zero FPs, all files emitted, zero crashes; "any project" and the items that only serve it (M2.4/M3.0/M3.5/M4) are parked in `PLAN-PHASE-5.md` § "Post-v1 backlog", which the loop skips until v1 lands). Pipeline: Scanner → Parser → **Binder → Checker → Transformer → Emitter**. The corpus suite (Phase 16, 100% green) is now a permanent zero-regression GATE, not the goal; the success metric is the real-world dashboard (per-project FP counts / emit diffs / crashes / throughput — see `PLAN-PHASE-5.md` § "The dashboard" and `bench/*.tsv`). Live counts in `STATUS.md`. The M3 engine items' accumulated analysis lives in `docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-4.md` § "Known architectural blockers" — read it before starting one. **M5 was re-scoped 2026-07-13 (owner) to the staged architecture-inversion arc (INV.0–INV.7): read `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md` before ANY M5/INV work — flat-profile micro-opt rounds are closed; kotlinx-coroutines-core in commonMain is owner-approved for the Flow front-end / parallel phases.**

### Execution protocol


PLAN-PHASE-5.md contains the **live QUEUE** under the heading `## Phase 17 — Self-compile the TypeScript compiler (M0–M5)` → `### QUEUE — work top-to-bottom; promote unblockers per protocol` (its `### Post-v1 backlog` section is parked — skip it until v1 lands). (docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-4.md and its earlier `### QUEUE` sections are archived from prior phases — do not work them.) Live session notes also live under that heading, with the most recent at the top. Default order is top-to-bottom, **fixing as many items per session as the budget allows** — do not stop after a single item if there is remaining context and more tractable work ahead. The outer loop:

1. Pick the first unchecked (`- [ ]`) item in the live queue (or next unfinished sub-step of an `IN PROGRESS` item). If the top item appears blocked on missing infrastructure, **do NOT skip it** — instead, promote the unblocker to the top of the queue and work that (see autonomous-decision policy below). The original item stays unchecked and gets picked up automatically once the unblocker lands. Skipping past a blocker is reserved for the narrow user-policy cases listed under "Guardrails."
2. Implement it — the item describes the deliverable. **Every fix ALSO gets hand-written LOCAL tests for its corner cases** (a `*Test.kt` in `src/commonTest/kotlin/`, kotlin.test — user directive 2026-07-02): the generated corpus suite only pins baseline bytes for corpus shapes; the local test pins the fix's INVARIANT beyond them (both associativity directions, nested contexts, off-corpus depths/inputs). Assert the SHARP signal, not just success — e.g. `diagnostics.none { it.code == 2589 }`, because a boundary-guard-masked stack overflow still "passes" (`DeepExpressionChainTest` is the reference; its first run caught 3 latent overflows the corpus depth missed).
3. Run the full suite (`rm -rf build/test-results/jvmTest && ./gradlew jvmTest 2>&1 | grep -a "tests completed"`).
4. Verify no regressions from the currently passing test count.
5. Check off the item (`- [x]`), **bump STATUS.md** (corpus count if it moved, dashboard metrics if the item moved them), add a session note to PLAN-PHASE-5.md (under the live notes heading, most recent on top), add a CLAUDE.md gotcha if applicable, **commit and push** (one commit per sub-step — keeps history bisectable and lets the next agent pick up mid-stream without re-running everything). STATUS.md drift is a real failure mode — past sessions committed `(+N)` work without bumping the count, so the next agent saw a stale baseline. **Trim-on-write (the anti-balloon rule)**: when bumping STATUS.md, keep only the ~5 most-recent round notes — move older ones to docs/history/STATUS-HISTORY.md in the same commit; when adding a session note to PLAN-PHASE-5.md, if more than ~10 rounds are live, move the oldest to docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-5-HISTORY.md in the same commit. Both files ballooned (160 KB / 1.7 MB) before the 2026-06-10 trim because sessions appended without trimming.
6. **Loop back to step 1** and pick up the next item. Keep going until one of these stop conditions:
   - Context budget is running out — finish the current item cleanly, commit, then stop.
   - Every remaining queue item is gated on user-policy decisions (Guardrails) AND no in-scope unblocker work remains. This should be rare — under the promote-unblocker default, blockers turn into queue items, so the queue rarely runs dry.
7. End the session with a concise summary of items completed, unblockers promoted (with what they unblock), items left pending user decisions, and net test count delta.

**Autonomous-decision policy (when an item appears blocked):**

When an item can't be implemented surgically — e.g. the diagnostic relies on infrastructure that doesn't exist yet (super-keyword resolution, full flow-graph, generic argument inference) — **promote the unblocker, do not skip the item**:

- **Promote-unblocker (default)**: identify the smallest concrete infrastructure piece that would unblock the item, insert it at the TOP of the queue with a one-line description and a back-pointer to the original item (and any other items it would also unblock — list them). Then start work on the unblocker. The original item stays unchecked; it gets picked up automatically on a future loop. Cross-phase work (binder + checker together, scope-chain restructuring, new cache layers) is in scope — that is exactly the work this policy exists to drive.
- **Decompose first if large**: if the unblocker is itself a multi-step change, split it into the smallest standalone sub-step that lands as a single commit without regressions, queue that, and only that. Do not attempt the whole infrastructure piece in one pass. Subsequent sub-steps get queued as separate items as the work progresses.
- **Time budget per attempt**: cap each attempt at one full-suite verification cycle. If it regresses, revert. After revert, you may either (a) try a different decomposition for the same unblocker, or (b) move to the next queue item — but record both attempts in the session note. Do not iterate more than twice in one session on the same regressing approach; if neither works, write up what you learned (which paths broke, what the underlying coupling is) and queue a follow-up "investigate X" item.
- **Skip is reserved for Guardrails only**: the only legitimate skip is when the unblocker requires a user-level decision listed under Guardrails below. For those, leave a `BLOCKED-PENDING-USER:` marker on the item with a one-paragraph proposal for the user to react to, then continue with the next queue item. Do NOT end the session.
- **No silent abandonment**: every promote/decompose/skip writes a session note (docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-4.md, most recent on top) with root cause, what was attempted, and what landed. Never leave an item half-done in the working tree.

**Anti-loop rule — recon-only sessions are a protocol failure:** ending a session with no code landed, on the grounds that recon found nothing, is NOT a legitimate outcome while the queue holds unchecked items. In Phase 17 the live pool is the PLAN-PHASE-5.md QUEUE plus any dashboard regression, so "the pool is dry" is never true while unchecked queue items remain; recon is part of step 1, never the deliverable. The full protocol — the `0/0/0` false-exhaustion mechanism, the three-bucket dryness rule, the mis-structured-queue diagnostic, and the forbidden session-end commit patterns — is in the **`corpus-triage` skill**; load it if you are about to end a session without landing code, or when triaging a corpus regression.

**Guardrails requiring user input** (do NOT decide autonomously):
- Build system changes (Gradle config, dependency add/remove/upgrade, Kotlin version bump).
- Test-generation pipeline changes (`generateTypeScriptTests`, baseline format, skipped-tests log structure).
- Re-enabling currently-disabled test categories (e.g. `.errors.txt` → +9k tests).
- EXCEPTIONS pre-approved 2026-07-02 (see PLAN-PHASE-5.md § Approvals): conformance-category adoption in the generator (M3.0), the real-lib migration (M2), and the M5 native-target re-enable.

**Other rules:**
- **Do NOT switch items** mid-task — finish the current item (commit + push) before starting the next.
- **Commit between items** — never bundle two unrelated sub-steps into one commit. Each committed sub-step leaves the repo in a clean state for the next agent.
- **Analysis items** (item 0) should produce written artifacts (design docs, categorized lists) before any code is written.
- **No regressions** — the currently passing tests must continue to pass after every change. Re-run the full suite between sub-steps; a +1 / -2 swap is still a regression.
- **Full-suite run caveat**: a clean JVM test run takes ~4-6 minutes. Budget accordingly when deciding whether to attempt another item.

### Reference TypeScript sources


The original TypeScript compiler source is in `typescript-repo/src/compiler/` (if cloned — only test fixtures are present in the sparse clone). When a fix is ambiguous or behavior is unclear, consult TypeScript's public documentation and source on GitHub. The 1M context window can accommodate large files when needed.

## AI agent workflow


- Keep this file and `PLAN-PHASE-5.md` up to date after each session so the next agent/developer starts with accurate state.
- `PLAN-PHASE-5.md` is the live plan (Phase 17 queue + dashboard + session notes). `docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-4.md` is archived Phase-16 state — its "Known architectural blockers" section is still the reference for the M3 engine items; `docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-4-HISTORY.md` has everything archived — older session notes, completed queue items, the explored-but-skipped SKIP LOG (add new skip entries there, above the `### End of skip log` terminator), the strategic-map archive, gotchas moved out of CLAUDE.md, and the full Phase 5–15 record. `STATUS.md` keeps the headline test count plus the last ~5 round notes; older entries live in `docs/history/STATUS-HISTORY.md`. `docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-3.md` has deferred Phase 3 items (pre-phase-17 artifacts — DESIGN docs, PLAN.md, NEXT-SESSION.md, TYPESCRIPT-TEST-HARNESS.md — also live in `docs/history/`). When grepping for whether a test was previously triaged, grep BOTH `docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-4.md` and `docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-4-HISTORY.md`.
- For subagent-based parallel work (rarely needed — most surgical fixes are faster done in-line), see `docs/subagent-workflow.md`.

## How to run tests


```bash
# Full suite (wipe the WHOLE results dir first — not just /binary; stale XMLs inflate
# the count and survive a /binary-only wipe, see the round-638/690 gotcha above):
rm -rf build/test-results/jvmTest && ./gradlew jvmTest 2>&1 | grep -a "tests completed"

# Single test (get expected vs actual diff):
rm -rf build/test-results/jvmTest && ./gradlew jvmTest --tests '*.<TestName>*' 2>&1 | grep -a -A 40 "message" | head -50
```

**Corpus-regression triage** — the `find_candidates.py` buckets and flags, the skip-log
filtering caveat, the pin-position rules, and the tsgo-relevance policy live in the
**`corpus-triage` skill** (CORPUS-era machinery; load it when diagnosing a corpus-test
regression). Two facts that stay resident because they bite outside triage: all failures
are DETERMINISTIC (run-to-run count variance is a dirty binary cache, never JVM
instability), and **Gradle wipes the XMLs when run with `--tests '*Name*'`** — so the full
suite must be re-run before any XML-reading script can report accurate results.

- **When hand-parsing JUnit XML to enumerate failing tests, use a real XML parser (`xml.etree.ElementTree`), NEVER a regex like `<testcase name="X"[^>]*>(.*?)</testcase>`.** JUnit emits PASSED testcases as self-closing `<testcase .../>` (no body) and FAILED ones with a `<failure>` child. A naive regex consumes a passed test's `/>`, then `(.*?)</testcase>` captures the NEXT (failing) testcase's body and ATTRIBUTES that failure to the passed test — silently shifting names and producing a garbage failing-list. This caused round-174's false "338/364 failures are order-dependent" conclusion (which mis-promoted B450); round-175 re-measured with `xml.etree` and found the 364 failures are GENUINE and DETERMINISTIC (running the failing-base patterns as an isolated batch reproduces all 364). The "Test ordering sensitivity" gotcha above is real but bounded (~25–50 boundary tests, both directions), not the bulk of the failing set.

## Anti-patterns to avoid


- Do not add content to this file that is already discoverable by reading the source or build scripts — that inflates context without adding signal, reducing AI agent task success rates (see [arxiv 2602.11988](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11988)).
- Do not use `grep` (without `-a` flag) on Gradle test output — it may contain binary content. Always use `grep -a`. **SOURCE greps too: `Checker.kt` — and `-project`'s `Project.kt`, measured 2026-08-22 — trip grep's binary heuristic (control bytes in string literals), so a plain `grep pattern src/.../Checker.kt` silently returns NOTHING for text that IS there** — round 500 nearly re-derived `perFileScope` from scratch this way, and an earlier session wrote a stale "never consumed" queue claim the same way. Retry any surprising empty grep over `src/` with `-a` before concluding code is absent.
- **Do not re-analyze what to fix next.** docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-4.md is already the prioritized plan. Pick the top unfinished item, implement it, done. Do not scan lists of failing tests or explore "low-hanging fruits" — that is wasted analysis time that could be implementation time.
